## National Defence Act {#national-defence-act .Title-of-Act}

[R.S.C.]{.abbr title="Revised Statutes of Canada"}, 1985, c. N-5
:::

An Act respecting national defence
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-374483 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 1
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-374488 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this
  Act,

  [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means flying machines and guided
      missiles that derive their lift in flight chiefly from aerodynamic
      forces, and flying devices that are supported chiefly by their
      buoyancy in air, and includes any aeroplane, balloon, kite
      balloon, airship, glider or kite; ([aéronef]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[aircraft material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aircraft material]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means engines, fittings,
      armament, ammunition, bombs, missiles, gear, instruments and
      apparatus, used or intended for use in connection with aircraft or
      the operation thereof, and components and accessories of aircraft
      and substances used to provide motive power or lubrication for or
      in connection with aircraft or the operation thereof; ([matériel
      aéronautique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[civil court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[civil court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a court of ordinary
      criminal jurisdiction in Canada and includes a court of summary
      jurisdiction; ([tribunal civil]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[civil custody]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[civil custody]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the holding under
      arrest or in confinement of a person by the police or other
      competent civil authority, and includes confinement in a
      penitentiary or civil prison; ([garde civile]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[civil prison]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[civil prison]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any prison, jail or
      other place in Canada in which offenders sentenced by a civil
      court in Canada to imprisonment for less than two years can be
      confined, and, if sentenced outside Canada, any prison, jail or
      other place in which a person, sentenced to that term of
      imprisonment by a civil court having jurisdiction in the place
      where the sentence was passed, can for the time being be
      confined; ([prison civile]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Code of Service Discipline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Code of Service Discipline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      provisions of Part III; ([code de discipline
      militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[court martial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[court martial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a General Court
      Martial and a Standing Court Martial; ([cour
      martiale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Court Martial Appeal Court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Court Martial Appeal Court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Court
      Martial Appeal Court of Canada established by section 234; ([Cour
      d'appel de la cour martiale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[criminal organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[criminal organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
      as in subsection 467.1(1) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([organisation criminelle]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[criminal organization offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[criminal organization offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence under section 467.11, 467.111,
        467.12 or 467.13 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or a
        serious offence committed for the benefit of, at the direction
        of, or in association with, a criminal organization, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a conspiracy or an attempt to commit, being an
        accessory after the fact in relation to, or any counselling in
        relation to, an offence referred to in paragraph
        (a); ([infraction d'organisation criminelle]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[defence establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[defence establishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any area or
      structure under the control of the Minister, and the materiel and
      other things situated in or on any such area or
      structure; ([établissement de défense]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Department of
      National Defence; ([ministère]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Deputy Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Deputy Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Deputy Minister
      of National Defence; ([sous-ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[detention barrack]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[detention barrack]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a place designated
      as such under subsection 205(1); ([caserne
      disciplinaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an insurrection, riot,
      invasion, armed conflict or war, whether real or
      apprehended; ([état d'urgence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[enemy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[enemy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes armed mutineers, armed
      rebels, armed rioters and pirates; ([ennemi]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[enrol]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[enrol]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to cause any person to become
      a member of the Canadian Forces; ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[finding of not responsible on account of mental disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[finding of not responsible on account of mental
      disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a finding made under
      subsection 202.14(1); ([verdict de non-responsabilité pour cause
      de troubles mentaux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Grievance Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Grievance Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2013, c.
      24, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[Grievances Committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Grievances Committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Military
      Grievances External Review Committee continued by subsection
      29.16(1); ([Comité des griefs]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Her Majesty's Canadian Ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Her Majesty's Canadian Ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
      vessel of the Canadian Forces commissioned as a vessel of
      war; ([navire canadien de Sa Majesté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Her Majesty's Forces]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Her Majesty's Forces]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the armed
      forces of Her Majesty wherever raised, and includes the Canadian
      Forces; ([forces de Sa Majesté]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[man]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[man]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st
      Supp.), s. 42\]]{.Repealed}

  [[materiel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[materiel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all public property, other
      than real property, immovables and money, provided for the
      Canadian Forces or for any other purpose under this Act, and
      includes any vessel, vehicle, aircraft, animal, missile, arms,
      ammunition, clothing, stores, provisions or equipment so
      provided; ([matériels]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[mental disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[mental disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a disease of the
      mind; ([troubles mentaux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[military]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[military]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} shall be construed as relating to
      all or any part of the Canadian
      Forces; ([militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[military judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[military judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a reserve force
      military judge; ([juge militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[military justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[military justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all aspects of the
      application of the Code of Service Discipline; ([justice
      militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[military justice system participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[military justice system participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      a person who plays a role in the administration of military
      justice, including

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Judge Advocate General,

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an officer and non-commissioned member who acts
        under the supervision of the Judge Advocate General,

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a prosecutor and counsel for an accused person,

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a military judge,

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a [[superior commander]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
        a [[commanding officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and a [[delegated
        officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 162.3,

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a [[custody review
        officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 153,

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a member of a panel of a General Court Martial
        and an officer and non-commissioned member who has been
        appointed to be a member of a such a panel,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an officer and non-commissioned member who is
        appointed by a commanding officer for the purpose of supporting
        a court martial,

      - [(j)]{.lawlabel} an officer and non-commissioned member who is
        authorized to lay or refer a charge,

      - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a prospective witness, a witness who has been
        summoned to appear and a witness who has testified,

      - [(l)]{.lawlabel} an officer and non-commissioned member referred
        to in paragraph (g) of the definition [[peace
        officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 2 of the [Criminal
        Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

      - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a commanding officer of a service prison or
        detention barrack and a person who acts under the supervision of
        such a commanding officer, and

      - [(n)]{.lawlabel} a person who acts under the supervision of the
        Chief Military Judge or the Court Martial
        Administrator; ([personne associée au système de justice
        militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[military police]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[military police]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the officers and
      non-commissioned members appointed under regulations made for the
      purposes of section 156; ([police militaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of National
      Defence; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[mutiny]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[mutiny]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means collective insubordination or
      a combination of two or more persons in the resistance of lawful
      authority in any of Her Majesty's Forces or in any forces
      cooperating therewith; ([mutinerie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[non-commissioned member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[non-commissioned member]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person,
      other than an officer, who is enrolled in, or who pursuant to law
      is attached or seconded otherwise than as an officer to, the
      Canadian Forces; ([militaire du rang]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[non-public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[non-public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all money and property, other than issues of
        materiel, received for or administered by or through messes,
        institutes or canteens of the Canadian Forces,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all money and property contributed to or by
        officers, non-commissioned members, units or other elements of
        the Canadian Forces for the collective benefit and welfare of
        those officers, non-commissioned members, units or other
        elements,

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by-products and refuse and the proceeds of the
        sale thereof to the extent prescribed under subsection 39(2),
        and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all money and property derived from, purchased
        out of the proceeds of the sale of, or received in exchange for,
        money and property described in paragraphs (a) to (c); ([biens
        non publics]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who holds Her Majesty's commission in
        the Canadian Forces,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who holds the rank of officer cadet in
        the Canadian Forces, and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person who pursuant to law is attached or
        seconded as an officer to the Canadian
        Forces; ([officier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} means a penitentiary established under Part I
        of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
        Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6),

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes, in respect of any punishment of
        imprisonment for life or for two years or more imposed outside
        Canada pursuant to the Code of Service Discipline, any prison or
        place in which a person sentenced to imprisonment for life or
        for two years or more by a civil court having jurisdiction in
        the place where the sentence is imposed can for the time being
        be confined, and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} means, in any place outside Canada where there
        is no prison or place for the confinement of persons sentenced
        to imprisonment for life or for two years or more, a civil
        prison; ([pénitencier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[personal equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[personal equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all materiel
      issued to an officer or non-commissioned member for the personal
      wear or other personal use of that officer or non-commissioned
      member; ([équipement personnel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[possession]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[possession]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 1995, c. 39, s.
      175\]]{.Repealed}

  [[Provost Marshal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Provost Marshal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2013, c.
      24, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[public property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means all money and
      property of Her Majesty in right of Canada; ([biens
      publics]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[regular force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[regular force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the component of the
      Canadian Forces that is referred to in subsection 15(1); ([force
      régulière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the termination of the
      service of an officer or non-commissioned member in any
      manner; ([libération]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[reserve force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[reserve force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the component of the
      Canadian Forces that is referred to in subsection 15(3); ([force
      de réserve]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[scale of punishments]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[scale of punishments]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the scale of
      punishments as set out in subsection 139(1); ([échelle des
      peines]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[serious offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[serious offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an offence under
      this Act or an indictable offence under any other Act of
      Parliament, for which the maximum punishment is imprisonment for
      five years or more, or an offence that is prescribed by regulation
      under subsection 467.1(4) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([infraction grave]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[serious personal injury offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[serious personal injury offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a serious offence, or an offence referred to in
        section 77, 86, 87, 92, 95, 113, 120, 124 or 127, involving

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the use or attempted use of violence against
          another person, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} conduct endangering or likely to endanger
          the life or safety of another person or inflicting or likely
          to inflict severe psychological damage upon another person, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is
        an offence under section 151, 152, 153, 153.1, 155, 160, 170,
        171, 172, 271, 272 or 273 of the [Criminal
        Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or an attempt to commit such an
        offence; ([infraction grave contre la personne]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[service convict]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service convict]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      under a sentence that includes a punishment of imprisonment for
      life or for two years or more imposed on that person pursuant to
      the Code of Service Discipline; ([condamné
      militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[service custody]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service custody]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the holding under
      arrest or in confinement of a person by the Canadian Forces, and
      includes confinement in a service prison or detention
      barrack; ([garde militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[service detainee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service detainee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      under a sentence that includes a punishment of detention imposed
      on that person pursuant to the Code of Service
      Discipline; ([détenu militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[service infraction]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service infraction]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a service
      infraction created by regulations made by the Governor in
      Council; ([manquement d'ordre militaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[service offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an offence under
      this Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
      Parliament, committed by a person while subject to the Code of
      Service Discipline; ([infraction d'ordre
      militaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[service prison]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service prison]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a place designated as
      such under subsection 205(1); ([prison militaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[service prisoner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service prisoner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      under a sentence that includes a punishment of imprisonment for
      less than two years imposed on that person pursuant to the Code of
      Service Discipline; ([prisonnier militaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[service tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2019, c.
      15, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[special force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[special force]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means such component of the
      Canadian Forces as may be established pursuant to subsection
      16(1); ([force spéciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[summary hearing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[summary hearing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a hearing conducted
      under section 163; ([audience sommaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[summary trial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[summary trial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s.
      2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[superior officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[superior officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any officer or
      non-commissioned member who, in relation to any other officer or
      non-commissioned member, is by this Act, or by regulations or
      custom of the service, authorized to give a lawful command to that
      other officer or non-commissioned
      member; ([supérieur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[terrorism offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[terrorism offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence under any of sections 83.02 to 83.04
        or 83.18 to 83.23 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act for which the maximum
        punishment is imprisonment for five years or more, or an offence
        punishable under section 130 that is an indictable offence under
        the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
        Parliament, that is committed for the benefit of, at the
        direction of or in association with a terrorist group,

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act for which the maximum
        punishment is imprisonment for five years or more, or an offence
        punishable under section 130 that is an indictable offence under
        the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
        Parliament, where the act or omission constituting the offence
        also constitutes a terrorist activity, or

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a conspiracy or an attempt to commit, being an
        accessory after the fact in relation to, or any counselling in
        relation to, an offence referred to in paragraph (a), (b) or
        (c); ([infraction de terrorisme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[terrorist activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[terrorist activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
      in subsection 83.01(1) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([activité terroriste]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[terrorist group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[terrorist group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      subsection 83.01(1) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([groupe terroriste]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[unfit to stand trial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unfit to stand trial]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means unable on
      account of mental disorder to conduct a defence at any stage of a
      trial by court martial before a finding is made or to instruct
      counsel to do so, and in particular, unable on account of mental
      disorder to

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} understand the nature or object of the
        proceedings,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} understand the possible consequences of the
        proceedings, or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} communicate with counsel; ([inaptitude à subir
        son procès]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an individual body of the
      Canadian Forces that is organized as such pursuant to section 17,
      with the personnel and materiel thereof; ([unité]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[victim]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person against whom a
      service offence has been committed, or is alleged to have been
      committed, who has suffered, or is alleged to have suffered,
      physical or emotional harm, property damage or economic loss as a
      result of the commission or alleged commission of the offence and
      includes, for the purposes of Division 1.1 of Part III and
      sections 202.201, 203.6 and 203.7, a person who has suffered
      physical or emotional harm, property damage or economic loss as a
      result of the commission of a service offence against any other
      person. ([victime]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting on victim's behalf

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Any of the following individuals may exercise a
  victim's rights under Division 1.1 of Part III or may act on a
  victim's behalf for the purposes of sections 189.1, 202.201, 203.6,
  203.7 and 203.81:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the victim is dead or is incapable, other than
    for operational reasons, of acting on their own behalf,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the victim's spouse or the individual who was at
      the time of the victim's death their spouse,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the individual who is, or was at the time of the
      victim's death, cohabiting with them in a conjugal relationship,
      having so cohabited for a period of at least one year,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a relative or dependant of the victim,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an individual who has in law or fact custody of,
      or is responsible for the care or support of, the victim, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} an individual who has in law or fact custody of,
      or is responsible for the care or support of, a dependant of the
      victim; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if, for operational reasons, the victim is unable
    to act on their own behalf and has requested the appointment of a
    member of the Canadian Forces to act on their behalf, a member
    appointed by the Chief of the Defence Staff or any officer
    authorized by the Chief of Defence Staff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- not a victim

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} An individual is not a victim in relation to a
  service offence, or entitled to exercise a victim's rights under
  Division 1.1 of Part III, if the individual is charged with the
  offence, found guilty of the offence or found unfit to stand trial or
  not responsible on account of mental disorder in respect of the
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- acting on victim's behalf

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} An individual is not entitled to act on a victim's
  behalf for the purposes of sections 189.1, 202.201, 203.6, 203.7 and
  203.81 if the individual is an accused person in relation to the
  offence or alleged offence that resulted in the victim suffering harm
  or loss or is an individual who is found guilty of that offence or who
  is found not responsible on account of mental disorder or unfit to
  stand trial in respect of that offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[possession]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the Code of Service Discipline
  and Part VII,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person has anything in possession when the person
    has it in the person's personal possession or knowingly

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has it in the actual possession or custody of
      another person, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has it in any place, whether or not that place
      belongs to or is occupied by the person, for the use or benefit of
      the person or of another person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where one of two or more persons, with the
    knowledge and consent of the rest, has anything in the person's
    custody or possession, it shall be deemed to be in the custody and
    possession of each and all of them.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 2
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), ss. 42, 60
- 1991, c. 43, s. 11
- 1992, c. 20, s. 216
- 1993, c. 34, s. 91(F)
- 1995, c. 39, s. 175
- 1998, c. 35, s. 1
- 2001, c. 32, s. 67, c. 41, s. 97
- 2004, c. 15, s. 74

- 2008, c. 29, s. 1
- 2013, c. 24, s. 2
- 2014, c. 17, s. 17
- [2019, c. 15, s. 2](#1149701-1353036){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149701-1353036.html"
  role="button"}

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Department of National Defence]{.HTitleText1} {#h-374577 .Part}

### [Establishment of the Department]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374578 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation of department

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** There is hereby established
a department of the Government of Canada called the Department of
National Defence over which the Minister of National Defence appointed
by commission under the Great Seal shall preside.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 3
:::

### [Minister]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374583 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** The Minister holds office
during pleasure, has the management and direction of the Canadian Forces
and of all matters relating to national defence and is responsible for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the construction and maintenance of all defence
  establishments and works for the defence of Canada; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} research relating to the defence of Canada and to the
  development of and improvements in materiel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 4
- R.S., 1985, c. 6 (4th Supp.), s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of person to execute
Minister's functions

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council, on
the recommendation of the Minister, may designate any other person in
addition to the Minister to exercise any power or perform any duty or
function that is vested in or that may be exercised or performed by the
Minister under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Associate Minister

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** The Governor General may,
by commission under the Great Seal, appoint an Associate Minister of
National Defence to hold office during pleasure and to exercise and
perform such powers, duties and functions of the Minister as may be
assigned to the Associate Minister by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 6
:::

### [Deputy Minister]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374598 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** There shall be a Deputy
Minister of National Defence who shall be appointed by the Governor in
Council to hold office during pleasure.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Associate Deputy Ministers

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council may
appoint not more than three Associate Deputy Ministers of National
Defence, each of whom shall have the rank and status of a deputy head of
a department and as such shall, under the Minister and the Deputy
Minister, exercise and perform such powers, duties and functions as
deputy of the Minister and otherwise as the Minister may specify.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 8
:::

### [Judge Advocate General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374607 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may appoint an officer who is a barrister or
  advocate with at least ten years standing at the bar of a province to
  be the Judge Advocate General of the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General holds office during
  pleasure for a term not exceeding four years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General is eligible to be
  re-appointed on the expiry of a first or subsequent term of office.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 9
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legal adviser

**[[9.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.1 .sectionLabel}** The Judge Advocate
General acts as legal adviser to the Governor General, the Minister, the
Department and the Canadian Forces in matters relating to military law.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Superintendence of military justice

- **[[9.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Judge Advocate General has the superintendence of the administration
  of military justice in the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular reviews

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General shall conduct, or cause to
  be conducted, regular reviews of the administration of military
  justice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsible to Minister

- **[[9.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Judge Advocate General is responsible to the Minister in the
  performance of the Judge Advocate General's duties and functions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General shall report annually to
  the Minister on the administration of military justice in the Canadian
  Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall have a copy of the report laid
  before each House of Parliament on any of the first fifteen days on
  which that House is sitting after the Minister receives it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank

**[[9.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9.4 .sectionLabel}** The Judge Advocate
General holds a rank that is not less than brigadier-general.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to act for Judge Advocate
General

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** The powers of the Judge
Advocate General may be exercised, and the duties and functions of the
Judge Advocate General may be performed, by any other officer who has
the qualifications set out in subsection 9(1) that the Minister may
authorize to act for the Judge Advocate General for that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 10
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-derogation

**[[10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.1 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, section 9.1 is not in derogation of the authority of the
Minister of Justice and Attorney General of Canada under the [Department
of Justice Act](/eng/acts/J-2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 2
:::

### [Materiel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374649 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery of materiel for sale or disposal

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may authorize the Minister to deliver to any department or agency of the
Government of Canada, for sale or disposal to any countries or
international welfare organizations and on any terms that the Governor
in Council may determine, any materiel that has not been declared
surplus and is not immediately required for the use of the Canadian
Forces or for any other purpose under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 11
- 1998, c. 35, s. 3
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374654 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of Governor in Council to make
regulations

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations for the organization,
  training, discipline, efficiency, administration and good government
  of the Canadian Forces and generally for carrying the purposes and
  provisions of this Act into effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's power to make regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 13 and any regulations made by the
  Governor in Council, the Minister may make regulations for the
  organization, training, discipline, efficiency, administration and
  good government of the Canadian Forces and generally for carrying the
  purposes and provisions of this Act into effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treasury Board's power to make
  regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Treasury Board may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the rates and conditions of issue of
    pay of military judges, the Director of Military Prosecutions and
    the Director of Defence Counsel Services;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the forfeitures and deductions to which
    the pay and allowances of officers and non-commissioned members are
    subject; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} providing for any matter concerning the pay,
    allowances and reimbursement of expenses of officers and
    non-commissioned members for which the Treasury Board considers
    regulations are necessary or desirable to carry out the purposes or
    provisions of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retroactive effect

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under paragraph (3)(a) may, if they
  so provide, have retroactive effect. However, regulations that
  prescribe the rates and conditions of issue of pay of military judges
  may not have effect

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inquiry under section 165.34,
    before the day referred to in subsection 165.34(3) on which the
    inquiry that leads to the making of the regulations is to commence;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an inquiry under section 165.35,
    before the day on which the inquiry that leads to the making of the
    regulations commences.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 12
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on Minister's power

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** Where in any section of
this Act, other than section 12, there is express reference to
regulations made or prescribed by the Governor in Council or the
Treasury Board in respect of any matter, the Minister does not have
power to make regulations pertaining to that matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military Police Professional Code of
Conduct

**[[13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations, to be known as the Military Police
Professional Code of Conduct, to govern the conduct of members of the
military police.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 5
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[The Canadian Forces]{.HTitleText1} {#h-374679 .Part}

### [Constitution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374680 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian Forces

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** The Canadian Forces are
the armed forces of Her Majesty raised by Canada and consist of one
Service called the Canadian Armed Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regular force

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There
  shall be a component of the Canadian Forces, called the regular force,
  that consists of officers and non-commissioned members who are
  enrolled for continuing, full-time military service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of regular force

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The maximum numbers of officers and non-commissioned
  members in the regular force shall be as authorized by the Governor in
  Council, and the regular force shall include such units and other
  elements as are embodied therein.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reserve force

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} There shall be a component of the Canadian Forces,
  called the reserve force, that consists of officers and
  non-commissioned members who are enrolled for other than continuing,
  full-time military service when not on active service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of reserve force

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The maximum numbers of officers and non-commissioned
  members in the reserve force shall be as authorized by the Governor in
  Council, and the reserve force shall include such units and other
  elements as are embodied therein.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 15
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special force

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In an
  emergency, or if considered desirable in consequence of any action
  undertaken by Canada under the United Nations Charter or the North
  Atlantic Treaty, the North American Aerospace Defence Command
  Agreement or any other similar instrument to which Canada is a party,
  the Governor in Council may establish and authorize the maintenance of
  a component of the Canadian Forces, called the special force,
  consisting of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} officers and non-commissioned members of the
    regular force who are placed in the special force under conditions
    prescribed in regulations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} officers and non-commissioned members of the
    reserve force who, being on active service or having applied and
    been accepted for continuing, full-time military service, are placed
    in the special force under conditions prescribed in regulations; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} officers and non-commissioned members not of the
    regular force or the reserve force who are enrolled in the special
    force for continuing, full-time military service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of special force

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The maximum numbers of officers and non-commissioned
  members in the special force shall be as authorized by the Governor in
  Council, and the special force shall include such units and other
  elements as are embodied therein.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 16
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2004, c. 15, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Units and Other Elements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374706 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organization

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Canadian Forces shall consist of those of the following elements that
  are from time to time organized by or under the authority of the
  Minister:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} commands, including the Royal Canadian Navy, the
    Canadian Army and the Royal Canadian Air Force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} formations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} units; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} other elements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Components

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A unit or other element organized under subsection
  (1), other than a command or a formation, shall from time to time be
  embodied in a component of the Canadian Forces as directed by or under
  the authority of the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 17
- 2014, c. 20, s. 168
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Chief of the Defence Staff]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374718 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment, rank and duties of Chief of
Defence Staff

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may appoint an officer to be the Chief of the
  Defence Staff, who shall hold such rank as the Governor in Council may
  prescribe and who shall, subject to the regulations and under the
  direction of the Minister, be charged with the control and
  administration of the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibility and channels of
  communication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Governor in Council otherwise directs, all
  orders and instructions to the Canadian Forces that are required to
  give effect to the decisions and to carry out the directions of the
  Government of Canada or the Minister shall be issued by or through the
  Chief of the Defence Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 18
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vice Chief of the Defence Staff

**[[18.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.1 .sectionLabel}** There shall be an
officer appointed by the Chief of the Defence Staff to be the Vice Chief
of the Defence Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity of Chief of the
Defence Staff

**[[18.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.2 .sectionLabel}** In the event of the
absence or incapacity of the Chief of the Defence Staff, the Vice Chief
of the Defence Staff, or any other officer that is specified by the
Minister or the Chief of the Defence Staff, has the control and
administration of the Canadian Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 6
:::

### [Canadian Forces Provost Marshal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374734 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[18.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may
  appoint an officer who has been a member of the military police for at
  least 10 years to be the Canadian Forces Provost Marshal (in this Act
  referred to as the "Provost Marshal").

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal holds a rank that is not less
  than colonel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office and removal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal holds office during good
  behaviour for a term not exceeding four years. The Chief of the
  Defence Staff may remove the Provost Marshal from office for cause on
  the recommendation of an inquiry committee established under
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of inquiry committee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An inquiry committee has the same powers, rights and
  privileges --- other than the power to punish for contempt --- as are
  vested in a superior court of criminal jurisdiction with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the attendance, swearing and examination of
    witnesses;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and inspection of documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of its orders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all other matters necessary or proper for the due
    exercise of its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal is eligible to be reappointed on
  the expiry of a first or subsequent term of office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and functions

**[[18.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.4 .sectionLabel}** The Provost Marshal's
responsibilities include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} investigations conducted by any unit or other element
  under his or her command;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of selection and training standards
  applicable to candidates for the military police and the ensuring of
  compliance with those standards;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of training and professional
  standards applicable to the military police and the ensuring of
  compliance with those standards; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} investigations in respect of conduct that is
  inconsistent with the professional standards applicable to the
  military police or the Military Police Professional Code of Conduct.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General supervision

- **[[18.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal acts under the
  general supervision of the Vice Chief of the Defence Staff in respect
  of the responsibilities described in paragraphs 18.4(a) to (d).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General instructions or guidelines

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Vice Chief of the Defence Staff may issue general
  instructions or guidelines in writing in respect of the
  responsibilities described in paragraphs 18.4(a) to (d). The Provost
  Marshal shall ensure that they are available to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific instructions or guidelines

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Vice Chief of the Defence Staff may issue
  instructions or guidelines in writing in respect of a particular
  investigation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Availability to public

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal shall ensure that instructions
  and guidelines issued under subsection (3) are available to the
  public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply in respect of an
  instruction or guideline, or of a part of one, if the Provost Marshal
  considers that it would not be in the best interests of the
  administration of justice for the instruction or guideline, or that
  part of it, to be available to the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

**[[18.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.6 .sectionLabel}** The Provost Marshal
shall, within three months after the end of each fiscal year, submit to
the Chief of the Defence Staff a report concerning the activities of the
Provost Marshal and the military police during the year. The Chief of
the Defence Staff shall submit the report to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 4
:::

### [Powers of Command]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374777 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority of officers and
non-commissioned members

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** The authority and powers
of command of officers and non-commissioned members shall be as
prescribed in regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 19
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Enrolment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374782 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioned officers

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Commissions of officers in the
  Canadian Forces shall be granted by Her Majesty during pleasure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer cadets and non-commissioned
  members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Persons shall be enrolled as officer cadets or as
  non-commissioned members for indefinite or fixed periods of service as
  may be prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When consent of parent or guardian
  required

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person under the age of eighteen years shall not be
  enrolled without the consent of one of the parents or of the guardian
  of that person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 20
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ranks of officers and non-commissioned
members

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this Act, the ranks of the officers and
  non-commissioned members of the Canadian Forces shall be as set out in
  the schedule.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person holding a rank set out in the schedule shall
  use, or be referred to by, a designation of rank prescribed in
  regulations made by the Governor in Council but only in the
  circumstances prescribed in those regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 21
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2014, c. 20, s. 169
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Numbers in ranks and trade groups

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The maximum number of
persons in each rank and trade group of the Canadian Forces shall be
determined as prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to serve

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  enrolment of a person binds the person to serve in the Canadian Forces
  until the person is, in accordance with regulations, lawfully
  released.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oaths and declarations on enrolment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Oaths and declarations required on enrolment shall be
  taken and subscribed before commissioned officers or justices of the
  peace and shall be in such forms as may be prescribed in regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 23
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to transfer

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** No officer or
non-commissioned member shall be transferred from the regular force to
the reserve force or from the reserve force to the regular force unless
the officer or non-commissioned member consents to the transfer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 24
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of receipt of pay if not enrolled

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** A person who, although
not enrolled or re-engaged for service, has received pay as an officer
or non-commissioned member is, until the person claims to be released
and is released, deemed to be an officer or non-commissioned member, as
the case may be, of that component of the Canadian Forces through which
the pay was received and to be subject to this Act as if the person were
such an officer or non-commissioned member duly enrolled or re-engaged
for service.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 25
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of receipt of pay if irregularly
enrolled

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who, although erroneously or irregularly enrolled or
  re-engaged, has received pay as an officer or non-commissioned member
  of that component of the Canadian Forces in which the person was so
  enrolled or re-engaged, is deemed to be an officer or non-commissioned
  member, as the case may be, regularly enrolled or re-engaged, and is
  not, except as provided in subsection (2), entitled to be released on
  the ground of the error or irregularity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision for release

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is, by virtue of subsection (1), deemed
  to be an officer or non-commissioned member and who claims to be
  released within three months after the date of commencement of the pay
  and establishes the error or irregularity referred to in that
  subsection shall, except when on active service or during an
  emergency, be released.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method of release

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The commanding officer of a person who claims to be
  released on the ground of not having been enrolled or re-engaged, or
  not having been regularly enrolled or re-engaged, shall forthwith
  forward the claim to the authority having power to effect the release
  and the person, if entitled to be released, shall be released with all
  convenient speed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 26
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Attachment and Secondment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374827 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner and conditions of attachment and
secondment

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** An officer or
non-commissioned member may be attached or seconded to another component
of the Canadian Forces or to any department or agency of government, any
public or private institution, private industry or any other body in
such manner and under such conditions as are prescribed in any other Act
or in regulations, but no officer or non-commissioned member of the
reserve force who is not on active service shall be attached or seconded
pursuant to this section unless the officer or non-commissioned member
consents to the attachment or secondment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 27
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Promotion]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374832 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to promote

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 22 and
to regulations, officers and non-commissioned members may be promoted by
the Minister or by such authorities of the Canadian Forces as are
prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 28
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Grievances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374837 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to grieve

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer or non-commissioned member who has been aggrieved by any
  decision, act or omission in the administration of the affairs of the
  Canadian Forces for which no other process for redress is provided
  under this Act is entitled to submit a grievance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} There is no right to grieve in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decision of a court martial or the Court Martial
    Appeal Court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a decision of a board, commission, court or
    tribunal established other than under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a matter or case prescribed by the Governor in
    Council in regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military judges

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A military judge may not submit a grievance in
  respect of a matter that is related to the exercise of his or her
  judicial duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner and conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A grievance must be submitted in the manner and in
  accordance with the conditions prescribed in regulations made by the
  Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No penalty for grievance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An officer or non-commissioned member may not be
  penalized for exercising the right to submit a grievance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of error

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (4), any error discovered
  as a result of an investigation of a grievance may be corrected, even
  if correction of the error would have an adverse effect on the officer
  or non-commissioned member.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 29
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 43
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorities for determination of
grievances

- **[[29.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The initial authority and subsequent
  authorities who may consider and determine grievances are the
  authorities designated in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Different authorities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide that different types of
  grievances may be considered and determined by different authorities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grievances submitted by military judges

**[[29.101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.101 .sectionLabel}** Despite
subsection 29.1(1), a grievance submitted by a military judge shall be
considered and determined by the Chief of the Defence Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final authority

**[[29.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.11 .sectionLabel}** The Chief of the
Defence Staff is the final authority in the grievance process and shall
deal with all matters as informally and expeditiously as the
circumstances and the considerations of fairness permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.11-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to Grievances Committee

- **[[29.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall
  refer every grievance that is of a type prescribed in regulations made
  by the Governor in Council, and every grievance submitted by a
  military judge, to the Grievances Committee for its findings and
  recommendations before the Chief of the Defence Staff considers and
  determines the grievance. The Chief of the Defence Staff may refer any
  other grievance to the Grievances Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Material to be provided to Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When referring a grievance to the Grievances
  Committee, the Chief of the Defence Staff shall provide the Grievances
  Committee with a copy of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the written submissions made to each authority in
    the grievance process by the officer or non-commissioned member
    presenting the grievance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any decision made by an authority in respect of the
    grievance; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other information under the control of the
    Canadian Forces that is relevant to the grievance.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 7, 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.12-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 29.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief of the Defence Staff not bound

- **[[29.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff is
  not bound by any finding or recommendation of the Grievances
  Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall provide reasons
  for his or her decision in respect of a grievance if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Chief of the Defence Staff does not act on a
    finding or recommendation of the Grievances Committee; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the grievance was submitted by a military judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 8, 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.13-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 29.13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

- **[[29.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may
  delegate any of his or her powers, duties or functions as final
  authority in the grievance process to an officer who is directly
  responsible to the Chief of the Defence Staff, except that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a grievance submitted by an officer may be
    delegated only to an officer of equal or higher rank; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a grievance submitted by a military judge may not
    be delegated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer who is placed in a real, apparent or
  potential conflict of interest as a result of a delegation may not act
  as final authority in respect of the grievance and shall advise the
  Chief of the Defence Staff in writing without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subdelegation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may not delegate the
  power to delegate under subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 9
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.14-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision is final

**[[29.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.15 .sectionLabel}** A decision of a
final authority in the grievance process is final and binding and,
except for judicial review under the [Federal Courts
Act](/eng/acts/F-7), is not subject to appeal or to review by any court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.15-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.15"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Military Grievances External Review Committee]{.HTitleText2} {#h-374905 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grievances Committee

- **[[29.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Forces Grievance Board
  is continued as the Military Grievances External Review Committee,
  consisting of a Chairperson, at least two Vice-Chairpersons and any
  other members appointed by the Governor in Council that are required
  to allow it to perform its functions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full- or part-time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson and one Vice-Chairperson are each
  full-time members and the other members may be appointed as full-time
  or part-time members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure and removal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each member holds office during good behaviour for a
  term not exceeding four years but may be removed by the Governor in
  Council for cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A member is eligible to be re-appointed on the expiry
  of a first or subsequent term of office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of full-time members

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Full-time members shall devote the whole of their
  time to the performance of their duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest --- part-time
  members

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Part-time members may not accept or hold any office
  or employment during their term of office that is inconsistent with
  their duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Members who are not officers or non-commissioned
  members are entitled to be paid for their services the remuneration
  and allowances fixed by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel and living expenses

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Members who are not officers or non-commissioned
  members are entitled to be paid reasonable travel and living expenses
  incurred by them in the course of their duties while absent from their
  ordinary place of work, if full-time members, or their ordinary place
  of residence, if part-time members, subject to any applicable Treasury
  Board directives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of members

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Members who are not officers or non-commissioned
  members are deemed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to be employed in the public service for the
    purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to be employees for the purposes of the [Government
    Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to be employed in the federal public administration
    for the purposes of any regulations made pursuant to section 9 of
    the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secondment

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An officer or a non-commissioned member who is
  appointed as a member of the Grievances Committee shall be seconded to
  the Grievances Committee in accordance with section 27.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of office

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Every member shall, before commencing the duties of
  office, take the following oath of office:

  I, ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}, do solemnly swear
  (or affirm) that I will faithfully and honestly fulfil my duties as a
  member of the Military Grievances External Review Committee in
  conformity with the requirements of the [National Defence
  Act](/eng/acts/N-5), and of all rules and instructions under that Act
  applicable to the Military Grievances External Review Committee, and
  that I will not disclose or make known to any person not legally
  entitled to it any knowledge or information obtained by me by reason
  of my office. (*And in the case of an oath*: So help me God.)

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 224(E), 225(E)
- 2013, c. 24, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.16-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 29.16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson

- **[[29.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson is the chief
  executive officer of the Grievances Committee and has supervision over
  and direction of its work and staff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  Chairperson or if that office is vacant, the Minister may authorize a
  Vice-Chairperson to exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions of the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may delegate to a Vice-Chairperson
  any of the Chairperson's powers, duties or functions under this Act,
  except the power to delegate under this subsection and the duty to
  submit an annual report under subsection 29.28(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.17-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office

**[[29.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.18 .sectionLabel}** The head office of
the Grievances Committee shall be at the place in Canada designated by
the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.18-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

- **[[29.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employees that are necessary for
  the proper conduct of the work of the Grievances Committee shall be
  appointed in accordance with the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Experts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Grievances Committee may, with the approval of
  the Treasury Board, engage on a temporary basis the services of
  counsel and other persons having technical or specialized knowledge to
  assist the Grievances Committee in its work, establish the terms and
  conditions of their engagement and fix and pay their remuneration and
  expenses.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.19-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and functions

- **[[29.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Grievances Committee shall
  review every grievance referred to it by the Chief of the Defence
  Staff and provide its findings and recommendations in writing to the
  Chief of the Defence Staff and the officer or non-commissioned member
  who submitted the grievance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to act expeditiously

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Grievances Committee shall deal with all matters
  before it as informally and expeditiously as the circumstances and the
  considerations of fairness permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[29.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.21 .sectionLabel}** The Grievances
Committee has, in relation to the review of a grievance referred to it,
the power

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to summon and enforce the attendance of witnesses and
  compel them to give oral or written evidence on oath and to produce
  any documents and things under their control that it considers
  necessary to the full investigation and consideration of matters
  before it;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to receive and accept any evidence and information
  that it sees fit, whether admissible in a court of law or not.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

**[[29.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.22 .sectionLabel}** The Grievances
Committee may not receive or accept any evidence or other information
that would be inadmissible in a court of law by reason of any privilege
under the law of evidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.22-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness not excused from testifying

- **[[29.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No witness shall be excused from
  answering any question relating to a grievance before the Grievances
  Committee when required to do so by the Grievances Committee on the
  ground that the answer to the question may tend to criminate the
  witness or subject the witness to any proceeding or penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer not receivable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No answer given or statement made by a witness in
  response to a question described in subsection (1) may be used or
  receivable against the witness in any disciplinary, criminal,
  administrative or civil proceeding, other than a hearing or proceeding
  in respect of an allegation that the witness gave the answer or made
  the statement knowing it to be false.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.23-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

**[[29.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.24 .sectionLabel}** Travel and living
expenses incurred in appearing before the Grievances Committee shall, in
the discretion of the Grievances Committee, be paid, in accordance with
applicable Treasury Board directives, to the officer or non-commissioned
member whose grievance is being heard, and to that person's assisting
officer or counsel, if the Grievances Committee holds a hearing at a
place in Canada that is not their ordinary place of residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.24-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of documents, etc.

**[[29.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.25 .sectionLabel}** Documents and
things presented to the Grievances Committee at a hearing shall, on
request, be returned to the person who presented them within a
reasonable time after the Grievances Committee has provided its findings
and recommendations to the Chief of the Defence Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.25-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

- **[[29.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may make rules
  respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner of dealing with grievances referred to
    the Grievances Committee, including the conduct of investigations
    and hearings by the Grievances Committee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the apportionment of the work of the Grievances
    Committee among its members and the assignment of members to review
    grievances; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performance of the duties and functions of the
    Grievances Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearings in private

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A hearing of the Grievances Committee is to be held
  in private, unless the Chairperson, having regard to the interests of
  the persons participating in the hearing and the interest of the
  public, directs that the hearing or any part of it be held in public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.26-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of members

**[[29.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.27 .sectionLabel}** No criminal or
civil proceedings lie against any member of the Grievances Committee, or
against any person acting on its behalf, for anything done, reported or
said in good faith in the exercise or purported exercise of a power or
in the performance or purported performance of a duty or function of the
Grievances Committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.27-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

- **[[29.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall, within three
  months after the end of each year, submit to the Minister a report of
  the activities of the Grievances Committee during that year and its
  recommendations, if any.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall have a copy of the report laid
  before each House of Parliament on any of the first fifteen days on
  which that House is sitting after the Minister receives it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.28-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29.28"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Release]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375009 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  during an emergency, an officer or non-commissioned member who is not
  on active service is entitled to be released at the expiration of the
  term of service for which the officer or non-commissioned member is
  enrolled or re-engaged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of illegal absence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as may be prescribed in regulations made by
  the Governor in Council, any period during which an officer or
  non-commissioned member is in a state of desertion or is absent
  without leave shall not be reckoned toward the completion of the term
  of service for which that officer or non-commissioned member was
  enrolled or re-engaged.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception in emergency or when on
  active service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the term of service for which an officer or
  non-commissioned member is enrolled or re-engaged expires during an
  emergency or when the officer or non-commissioned member is on active
  service or within one year after the expiration of an emergency or
  after he has ceased to be on active service, the officer or
  non-commissioned member is liable to serve until the expiration of one
  year after the emergency has ceased to exist or after he has ceased to
  be on active service, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reinstatement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations made by the Governor in
  Council, where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member has been
    released from the Canadian Forces or transferred from one component
    to another by reason of a sentence of dismissal or a finding of
    guilty by a court martial or any civil court, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the sentence or finding ceases to have force and
    effect as a result of a decision of a competent authority,

  the release or transfer may be cancelled, with the consent of the
  officer or non-commissioned member concerned, who shall thereupon,
  except as provided in those regulations, be deemed for the purpose of
  this Act or any other Act not to have been so released or transferred.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 30
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- [2019, c. 15, s. 3](#1149709-1353038){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149709-1353038.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Active Service]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375024 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Placing forces on active service
