- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may place the Canadian Forces or any component,
  unit or other element thereof or any officer or non-commissioned
  member thereof on active service anywhere in or beyond Canada at any
  time when it appears advisable to do so

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by reason of an emergency, for the defence of
    Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in consequence of any action undertaken by Canada
    under the United Nations Charter; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in consequence of any action undertaken by Canada
    under the North Atlantic Treaty, the North American Aerospace
    Defence Command Agreement or any other similar instrument to which
    Canada is a party.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When officers and non-commissioned
  members deemed on active service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer or non-commissioned member who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a member of, serving with, or attached or
    seconded to, a component, unit or other element of the Canadian
    Forces that has been placed on active service,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been placed on active service, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} pursuant to law has been attached or seconded to a
    portion of a force that has been placed on active service,

  shall be deemed to be on active service for all purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 31
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2004, c. 15, s. 76
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proclamation for meeting of Parliament

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** Whenever the Governor in
Council places the Canadian Forces or any component or unit thereof on
active service, if Parliament is then separated by an adjournment or
prorogation that will not expire within ten days, a proclamation shall
be issued for the meeting of Parliament within ten days, and Parliament
shall accordingly meet and sit on the day appointed by the proclamation,
and shall continue to sit and act in like manner as if it had stood
adjourned or prorogued to the same day.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 32
:::

### [Service]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375043 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability in case of regular force

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  regular force, all units and other elements thereof and all officers
  and non-commissioned members thereof are at all times liable to
  perform any lawful duty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability in case of reserve force

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The reserve force, all units and other elements
  thereof and all officers and non-commissioned members thereof

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may be ordered to train for such periods as are
    prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may be called out on service to perform any lawful
    duty other than training at such times and in such manner as by
    regulations or otherwise are prescribed by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception in case of reserve force

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (2) shall be deemed to impose
  liability to serve as prescribed therein, without his consent, on an
  officer or non-commissioned member of the reserve force who is, by
  virtue of the terms of his enrolment, liable to perform duty on active
  service only.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[duty]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[duty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
  any duty that is military in nature and includes any duty involving
  public service authorized under section 273.6.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 33
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 8
:::

### [Limitation on Deployment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375057 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons under eighteen

**[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** A person who is under the
age of eighteen years may not be deployed by the Canadian Forces to a
theatre of hostilities.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 34
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 9
- 2000, c. 13, s. 1
:::

### [Pay and Allowances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375062 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treasury Board to establish

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  rates and conditions of issue of pay of officers and non-commissioned
  members, other than military judges, shall be established by the
  Treasury Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursements and allowances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The payments that may be made to officers and
  non-commissioned members by way of reimbursement for travel or other
  expenses and by way of allowances in respect of expenses and
  conditions arising out of their service shall be determined and
  regulated by the Treasury Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 35
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60(E)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 10
:::

### [Supply and Issue of Materiel]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375070 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial approval

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** The materiel supplied to
or used by the Canadian Forces shall be of such type, pattern and design
and shall be issued on such scales and in such manner as the Minister,
or such authorities of the Canadian Forces as are designated by the
Minister for that purpose, may approve.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 36
:::

### [Public Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375075 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for loss or damage

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** The conditions under
which and the extent to which an officer or non-commissioned member is
liable to Her Majesty in respect of loss of or damage to public property
shall be as prescribed in regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 37
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Non-public Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375080 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public property of units

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  non-public property of a unit or other element of the Canadian Forces
  shall vest in the officer from time to time in command of that unit or
  other element, and shall be used for the benefit of officers and
  non-commissioned members or for any other purpose approved by the
  Chief of the Defence Staff in the manner and to the extent authorized
  by the Chief of the Defence Staff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public property of disbanded units

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The non-public property of every disbanded unit or
  other disbanded element of the Canadian Forces vested in the officer
  in command of that unit or other element shall pass to and vest in the
  Chief of the Defence Staff, and may be disposed of at the discretion
  and direction of the Chief of the Defence Staff for the benefit of all
  or any officers and non-commissioned members or former officers and
  non-commissioned members, or their dependants.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-public property of units or
  elements in altered circumstances

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where, by reason of a substantial reduction in the
  number of officers and non-commissioned members serving in a unit or
  other element of the Canadian Forces or by reason of a change in the
  location or other conditions of service of a unit or other element,
  the Chief of the Defence Staff considers it desirable to do so, he may
  direct that the non-public property or any part thereof that is vested
  in the officer in command of that unit or other element shall pass to
  and be vested in the Chief of the Defence Staff on the terms set out
  in subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 38
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other non-public property

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Non-public property acquired by
  contribution but not contributed to any specific unit or other element
  of the Canadian Forces shall vest in the Chief of the Defence Staff
  and, subject to any specific directions by the contributor as to its
  disposal, may be disposed of at the discretion and direction of the
  Chief of the Defence Staff for the benefit of all or any officers and
  non-commissioned members or former officers and non-commissioned
  members, or their dependants.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-products and refuse

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} By-products and refuse derived from rations and other
  consumable stores issued to the Canadian Forces for use in service
  kitchens, and the proceeds of the sale thereof, shall, to the extent
  that the Governor in Council may prescribe, be non-public property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alienation of non-public property

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except as authorized by the Chief of the Defence
  Staff, no gift, sale or other alienation or attempted alienation of
  non-public property is effectual to pass the property therein.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 39
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability for loss or damage

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** The conditions under
which and the extent to which an officer or non-commissioned member is
liable to make restitution or reimbursement in respect of loss of or
damage to non-public property resulting from the negligence or
misconduct of that officer or noncommissioned member shall be as
prescribed by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 40
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial directions

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief of the Defence Staff shall exercise his authority under
  subsections 38(1) and (2) and 39(1) subject to any directions that may
  be given to him by the Minister for carrying the purposes and
  provisions of this section and sections 38 to 40 into effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Non-public property accounts shall be audited as the
  Minister may from time to time direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special provision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11)
  does not apply to non-public property.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 38
:::

### [Service Estates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375112 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection, administration and
distribution

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  service estates of officers and non-commissioned members who die
  during their service in the Canadian Forces may be collected,
  administered and distributed in whole or in part as prescribed in
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[service
  estate]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, but subject to any
  exceptions prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council,
  [[service estate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the following parts of
  the estate of a deceased officer or non-commissioned member mentioned
  in subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} service pay and allowances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all other emoluments emanating from Her Majesty
    that, at the date of death, are due or otherwise payable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} personal equipment that the deceased person is,
    under regulations, permitted to retain;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} personal or movable property, including cash, found
    on the deceased person or on a defence establishment or otherwise in
    the care or custody of the Canadian Forces; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an officer or non-commissioned
    member dying outside Canada, all other personal or movable property
    belonging to the deceased and situated outside Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 42
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 11
:::

### [Presumption of Death]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375125 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to issue certificate

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** Where an officer or
non-commissioned member disappears under circumstances that, in the
opinion of the Minister or such other authorities as the Minister may
designate, raise beyond reasonable doubt a presumption that the officer
or non-commissioned member is dead, the Minister or any such other
authority may issue a certificate declaring that the officer or
non-commissioned member is deemed to be dead and stating the date on
which the death is presumed to have occurred, and the officer or
non-commissioned member shall thenceforth, for the purposes of this Act
and the regulations and in relation to his status and service in the
Canadian Forces, be deemed to have died on that date.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 43
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Personal Effects of Absentees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375130 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vesting and disposal

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** The personal belongings
and decorations of an officer or non-commissioned member who is absent
without leave that are found in camp, quarters or otherwise in the care
or custody of the Canadian Forces vest in Her Majesty and shall be
disposed of in accordance with regulations made by the Governor in
Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 44
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Boards of Inquiry]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375135 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convening boards

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister, and such other authorities as the Minister may prescribe or
  appoint for that purpose, may, where it is expedient that the Minister
  or any such other authority should be informed on any matter connected
  with the government, discipline, administration or functions of the
  Canadian Forces or affecting any officer or non-commissioned member,
  convene a board of inquiry for the purpose of investigating and
  reporting on that matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A board of inquiry has, in relation to the matter
  before it, power

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to summon any person before the board and compel
    the person to give oral or written evidence on oath and to produce
    any documents and things under the person's control that it
    considers necessary for the full investigation and consideration of
    that matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to receive and accept, on oath or by affidavit or
    otherwise, any evidence and other information the board sees fit,
    whether or not the evidence or information is or would be admissible
    in a court of law; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to examine any record and make any inquiry that the
    board considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to on-board recordings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a board of inquiry may have
  access to an on-board recording, as defined in subsection 22(1) of the
  [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2), only if it is made available under
  that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 45
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 13
- 2014, c. 29, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-45-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 45"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness not excused from testifying

- **[[45.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  witness shall be excused from answering any question relating to a
  matter before a board of inquiry when required to do so by the board
  of inquiry on the ground that the answer to the question may tend to
  criminate the witness or subject the witness to any proceeding or
  penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer not receivable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No answer given or statement made by a witness in
  response to a question described in subsection (1) may be used or
  receivable against the witness in any disciplinary, criminal or civil
  proceeding, other than a hearing or proceeding in respect of an
  allegation that the witness gave the answer or made the statement
  knowing it to be false.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 14
:::

### [Cadet Organizations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375156 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formation

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may authorize the formation of cadet organizations under the
  control and supervision of the Canadian Forces to consist of persons
  of not less than twelve years of age who have not attained the age of
  nineteen years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training, administration, provision and
  command

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The cadet organizations referred to in subsection (1)
  shall be trained for such periods, administered in such manner and
  provided with materiel and accommodation under such conditions, and
  shall be subject to the authority and command of such officers, as the
  Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not part of Canadian Forces

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The cadet organizations referred to in subsection (1)
  are not comprised in the Canadian Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 43
- 1974-75-76, c. 36, Sch. (DND) vote 1d, c. 66, s. 21
:::

### [Educational Institutions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375166 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council, and any other authorities that are prescribed or
  appointed by the Governor in Council for that purpose, may in the
  interests of national defence establish institutions for the training
  and education of officers and non-commissioned members, officers and
  employees of the Department, candidates for enrolment in the Canadian
  Forces or for employment in the Department and other persons whose
  attendance has been authorized by or on behalf of the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Control and administration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The institutions referred to in subsection (1) shall
  be governed and administered in the manner prescribed by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 47
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 15
:::

### [Service Associations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375174 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may establish associations and organizations for
  purposes designed to further the defence of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accommodation, materiel and facilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may authorize the provision of
  accommodation, materiel and facilities for the training, practice and
  use of the associations and organizations mentioned in subsection (1)
  and other associations and organizations designed to further the
  defence of Canada, whether or not the members of such associations and
  organizations are officers or non-commissioned members.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 48
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Exercise of Authority]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375182 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of authority of officer or
non-commissioned member by another

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** Any power or jurisdiction
given to, and any act or thing to be done by, to or before any officer
or non-commissioned member may be exercised by, or done by, to or before
any other officer or non-commissioned member for the time being
authorized in that behalf by regulations or according to the custom of
the service.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 49
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method of signifying orders

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** Orders made under this
Act may be signified by an order, instruction or letter under the hand
of any officer whom the authority that made those orders has authorized
to issue orders on its behalf, and any order, instruction or letter
purporting to be signed by any officer appearing therein to be so
authorized is evidence that the officer is so authorized.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 47
:::

### [Notification of Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375191 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  regulations and all orders and instructions issued to the Canadian
  Forces shall be held to be sufficiently notified to any person whom
  they may concern by their publication, in the manner prescribed in
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, in the unit or other
  element in which that person is serving.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registered mail

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All regulations and all orders and instructions
  relating to or in any way affecting an officer or non-commissioned
  member of the reserve force who is not serving with a unit or other
  element shall, when sent to the officer or non-commissioned member by
  registered mail, addressed to the latest known place of abode or
  business of the officer or non-commissioned member, be held to be
  sufficiently notified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsections (1) and (2), all
  regulations and all orders and instructions referred to in those
  subsections shall be held to be sufficiently notified to any person
  whom they may concern by their publication in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 51
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60(E)
:::

### [Validity of Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375201 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authenticity of documents

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** A commission,
appointment, warrant, order or instruction in writing purported to be
granted, made or issued under this Act is evidence of its authenticity
without proof of the signature or seal affixed thereto or the authority
of the person granting, making or issuing it.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 49
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signature on commissions

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor General may cause the signature of the Governor General to be
  affixed to a commission granted to an officer of the Canadian Forces
  by stamping the signature on the commission with a stamp approved by,
  and used for the purpose by authority of, the Governor General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of signature

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A signature affixed in accordance with subsection (1)
  is as valid and effectual as if it were in the handwriting of the
  Governor General, and neither its authenticity nor the authority of
  the person by whom it was affixed shall be called in question, except
  on behalf of Her Majesty.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 50
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of bonds

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** Every bond to Her Majesty
entered into by any person before a judge, a justice of the peace or an
officer of the Canadian Forces, including a military judge, for the
purpose of securing the payment of a sum of money or the performance of
a duty or act required or authorized by this Act or by regulations is
valid and may be enforced accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 54
- 1998, c. 35, s. 16
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Code of Service Discipline]{.HTitleText1} {#h-375217 .Part}

### [Purpose]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1353039 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  purpose of the Code of Service Discipline is to maintain the
  discipline, efficiency and morale of the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the behaviour of persons who
  are subject to the Code of Service Discipline relates to the
  discipline, efficiency and morale of the Canadian Forces even when
  those persons are not on duty, in uniform or on a defence
  establishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 55
- 1998, c. 35, s. 17
- [2019, c. 15, s. 4](#1149713-1355895){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149713-1355895.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Disciplinary Jurisdiction of the Canadian Forces]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375218 .Subheading}

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375219 .Subheading}

**[[56 to 59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56_to_59 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
1998, c. 35, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons subject to Code of Service
Discipline

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons are subject to the Code of Service Discipline:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member of the
    regular force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member of the
    special force;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member of the
    reserve force when the officer or non-commissioned member is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} undergoing drill or training, whether in uniform
      or not,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in uniform,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on duty,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} called out under Part VI in aid of the civil
      power,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} called out on service,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} placed on active service,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} in or on any vessel, vehicle or aircraft of
      the Canadian Forces or in or on any defence establishment or work
      for defence,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} serving with any unit or other element of the
      regular force or the special force, or

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} present, whether in uniform or not, at any drill
      or training of a unit or other element of the Canadian Forces;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to such exceptions, adaptations and
    modifications as the Governor in Council may by regulations
    prescribe, a person who, pursuant to law or pursuant to an agreement
    between Canada and the state in whose armed forces the person is
    serving, is attached or seconded as an officer or non-commissioned
    member to the Canadian Forces;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person, not otherwise subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline, who is serving in the position of an officer or
    non-commissioned member of any force raised and maintained outside
    Canada by Her Majesty in right of Canada and commanded by an officer
    of the Canadian Forces;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person, not otherwise subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline, who accompanies any unit or other element of the
    Canadian Forces that is on service or active service in any place;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} subject to such exceptions, adaptations and
    modifications as the Governor in Council may by regulations
    prescribe, a person attending an institution established under
    section 47;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} an alleged spy for the enemy;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person, not otherwise subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline, who, in respect of any service offence committed
    or alleged to have been committed by the person, is in civil custody
    or in service custody; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a person, not otherwise subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline, while serving with the Canadian Forces under an
    engagement with the Minister whereby the person agreed to be subject
    to that Code.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person subject to the Code of Service
  Discipline under subsection (1) at the time of the alleged commission
  by the person of a service offence continues to be liable to be
  charged, dealt with and tried in respect of that offence under the
  Code of Service Discipline notwithstanding that the person may have,
  since the commission of that offence, ceased to be a person described
  in subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of status and rank

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, since allegedly committing a
  service offence, has ceased to be a person described in subsection
  (1), shall for the purposes of the Code of Service Discipline be
  deemed, for the period during which under that Code he is liable to be
  charged, dealt with and tried, to have the same status and rank that
  he held immediately before so ceasing to be a person described in
  subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 60
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons accompanying Canadian Forces

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section and sections 60, 62 and 65, but subject
  to any limitations prescribed by the Governor in Council, a person
  accompanies a unit or other element of the Canadian Forces that is on
  service or active service if the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} participates with that unit or other element in the
    carrying out of any of its movements, manoeuvres, duties in aid of
    the civil power, duties in a disaster or warlike operations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is accommodated or provided with rations at the
    person's own expense or otherwise by that unit or other element in
    any country or at any place designated by the Governor in Council;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a dependant outside Canada of an officer or
    non-commissioned member serving beyond Canada with that unit or
    other element; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is embarked on a vessel or aircraft of that unit or
    other element.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How persons accompanying Canadian
  Forces to be treated

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), every person mentioned in
  paragraph 60(1)(f) who, while accompanying any unit or other element
  of the Canadian Forces, is alleged to have committed a service
  offence, shall be treated as a non-commissioned member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate entitling person to
  treatment as officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person described in subsection (2) who holds, from
  the commanding officer of the unit or other element of the Canadian
  Forces that the person accompanies or from any other officer
  prescribed by the Minister for that purpose, a certificate entitling
  the person to be treated on the footing of an officer, revocable at
  the pleasure of the officer who issued it or of any other officer of
  equal or higher rank, shall be treated as an officer in respect of any
  offence alleged to have been committed by the person while holding
  that certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 61
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Command

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person to whom subsection 61(2) or (3) applies shall, for the purposes
  of the Code of Service Discipline, be deemed to be under the command
  of the commanding officer of the unit or other element of the Canadian
  Forces that the person accompanies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Spies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person described in paragraph 60(1)(h) shall,
  for the purposes of the Code of Service Discipline, be deemed to be
  under the command of the commanding officer of the unit or other
  element of the Canadian Forces that may be holding the person in
  custody from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Released persons serving sentence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person described in paragraph 60(1)(i) who is
  alleged to have committed, during the currency of the imprisonment or
  detention of that person, a service offence shall, for the purposes of
  the Code of Service Discipline, be deemed to be under the command of
  the commanding officer of the service prison or detention barrack, as
  the case may be, in which that person is imprisoned or detained.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 55
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons under special engagement

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  person mentioned in paragraph 60(1)(j) who, while serving with the
  Canadian Forces, is alleged to have committed a service offence shall
  be treated as a non-commissioned member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement entitling person to treatment
  as officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the terms of the agreement under which a person
  described in subsection (1) was engaged entitle the person to be
  treated as an officer, the person shall be treated as an officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Command where person under special
  engagement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person to whom subsection (1) or (2) applies
  shall, for the purposes of the Code of Service Discipline, be deemed
  to be under the command of the commanding officer of the unit or other
  element of the Canadian Forces in which that person is serving.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 63
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985,
c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 44\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons under command of officer deemed
their superior officer

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person subject to the Code of Service Discipline by virtue of
  paragraph 60(1)(f), (g), (i) or (j) shall, for the purposes of
  preparation, practice or execution of any plan, arrangement or
  manoeuvre for the defence or evacuation of any area in the event of
  attack, be under the command of the commanding officer of the unit or
  other element of the Canadian Forces that the person is accompanying
  or with which the person is serving or is in attendance and, for those
  purposes, the commanding officer shall be deemed to be a superior
  officer of the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibited interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) shall be construed as
  requiring any person described therein to bear arms or to participate
  in any active operations against the enemy.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 55
:::

#### [Plea in Bar of Trial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375289 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}*[Autrefois acquit]{.otherLang
lang="fr"}* and *[autrefois convict]{.otherLang lang="fr"}*

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may not be tried or tried again in respect of an offence or any
  other substantially similar offence arising out of the facts that gave
  rise to the offence if, while subject to the Code of Service
  Discipline in respect of that offence, or if, while liable to be
  charged, dealt with and tried under the Code in respect of that
  offence, the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been found not guilty by a court martial, civil
    court or court of a foreign state on a charge of having committed
    that offence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been found guilty by a court martial, civil
    court or court of a foreign state on a charge of having committed
    that offence and has been either punished in accordance with the
    sentence or discharged absolutely or on conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) affects the validity of a
  new trial held under section 249 or a new trial directed by a court
  having jurisdiction to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of other offences admitted at
  previous trial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who, under section 194, has been sentenced
  in respect of a service offence admitted by that person may not be
  tried by a court martial or civil court in respect of that offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 66
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 45
- 1998, c. 35, s. 20
- 2013, c. 24, s. 14
- [2019, c. 15, s. 5](#1149715-1353051){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149715-1353051.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149987-1355639){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149987-1355639.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Place of Commission of Offence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375301 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service offence, wherever committed, is
triable

**[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 70,
every person alleged to have committed a service offence may be charged,
dealt with and tried under the Code of Service Discipline, whether the
alleged offence was committed in Canada or outside Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 57
:::

#### [Place of Trial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375306 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No territorial limitation

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** Every person alleged to
have committed a service offence may be charged, dealt with and tried
under the Code of Service Discipline, either in Canada or outside
Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 58
:::

#### [Period of Liability]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375311 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When person is liable

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who is subject to the Code of Service Discipline at the time of
  the alleged commission of a service offence may be charged, dealt with
  and tried at any time under the Code.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sections 130 and 132

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if the service offence is
  punishable under section 130 or 132 and the act or omission that
  constitutes the service offence would have been subject to a
  limitation period had it been dealt with other than under the Code,
  then that limitation period applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 69
- 1990, c. 14, s. 7
- 1991, c. 43, s. 12
- 1993, c. 34, s. 92
- 1998, c. 35, s. 21
- 2008, c. 29, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-69-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 69"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Limitations with respect to Certain Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375319 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences not triable by courts martial

**[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** A court martial does not
have jurisdiction to try any person charged with any of the following
offences committed in Canada:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} murder;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} manslaughter; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence under any of sections 280 to 283 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [(d) to (f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 70
- 1998, c. 35, s. 22
- [2019, c. 15, s. 6](#1149724-1353058){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149724-1353058.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Jurisdiction of Civil Courts]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375328 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No interference with civil jurisdiction

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 66,
nothing in the Code of Service Discipline affects the jurisdiction of
any civil court to try a person for any offence triable by that court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 71
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 46
:::

### [DIVISION 1.1]{.HLabel2}[Declaration of Victims Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1353059 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353060 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[military justice
system]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[71.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.01 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, [[military justice system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the investigation of service offences, the processes
  for the laying and referral of charges of service offences and their
  prosecution;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the process for the carrying out of punishments in
  relation to service offences, except in respect of any service
  prisoners and service convicts who have been committed to a
  penitentiary or civil prison; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the proceedings of a court martial or a [[Review
  Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 197, in respect
  of an accused person who is found unfit to stand trial or not
  responsible on account of mental disorder.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353067){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353067.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Rights]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353068 .Subheading}

##### [Information]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1353069 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General information

**[[71.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.02 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right, on request, to information about

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the military justice system and the role of victims
  in it;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the services and programs available to them as a
  victim; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} their right to file a complaint for an infringement
  or denial of any of their rights under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353076){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353076.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation and proceedings

**[[71.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.03 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right, on request, to information about

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the status and outcome of the investigation into the
  service offence; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the location of proceedings in relation to the
  offence, when they will take place and their progress and outcome.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353082){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353082.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information about offender or accused

- **[[71.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every victim has the right, on
  request, to information about

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offender while they are in a service prison or
    detention barrack;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the release of the offender from a service prison
    or detention barrack;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} hearings held under section 202.161 to decide
    whether the accused person is a high-risk accused and the
    dispositions made at those hearings; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hearings held for the purpose of making
    dispositions under any of sections 201, 202 and 202.16 and the
    dispositions made at those hearings; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} hearings held by a Review Board under section
    202.25 and the dispositions made at those hearings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Information may be disclosed for the purposes of
  paragraphs (1)(a) and (b) subject to and in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353093){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353093.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 64](#1150074-1355910){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150074-1355910.html"
  role="button"}
:::

##### [Protection]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1353094 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

**[[71.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.05 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to have their security considered by the appropriate
authorities in the military justice system.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353224){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353224.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection from intimidation and
retaliation

**[[71.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.06 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to have reasonable and necessary measures taken by the
appropriate authorities in the military justice system to protect the
victim from intimidation and retaliation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353228){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353228.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Privacy

**[[71.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.07 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to have their privacy considered by the appropriate
authorities in the military justice system.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353232){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353232.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity protection

**[[71.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.08 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to request that their identity be protected if they are a
complainant in respect of the service offence or a witness in
proceedings relating to the service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353236){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353236.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testimonial aids

**[[71.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.09 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to request testimonial aids when appearing as a witness in
proceedings relating to the service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353240){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353240.html"
  role="button"}
:::

##### [Participation]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1353241 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Views to be considered

**[[71.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.1 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has the
right to convey their views about decisions to be made by appropriate
authorities in the military justice system that affect the victim's
rights under this Division and to have those views considered.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353245){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353245.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Victim impact statement

**[[71.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.11 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to present a victim impact statement to the appropriate
authorities in the military justice system and to have it considered.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353249){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353249.html"
  role="button"}
:::

##### [Restitution]{.HTitleText4} {#h-1353250 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restitution order

**[[71.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.12 .sectionLabel}** Every victim has
the right to have the court martial consider making a restitution order
against the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353262){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353262.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement

**[[71.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.13 .sectionLabel}** Every victim in
whose favour a restitution order is made has the right, if they are not
paid, to have the order entered as a civil court judgment that is
enforceable against the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353266){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353266.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [General Provisions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353267 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[71.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This Division applies in respect of
  a victim of a service offence in their interactions with the military
  justice system

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} while the offence is being investigated or
    prosecuted or the charge is being referred;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} while the offender is serving a punishment in
    relation to the offence, unless the offender is a service prisoner
    or service convict who has been committed to a penitentiary or civil
    prison; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} while the accused person is, in relation to the
    offence, under the jurisdiction of a court martial or a [[Review
    Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 197, if they
    are found unfit to stand trial or not responsible on account of
    mental disorder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting of offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), if an offence is
  reported to the appropriate authorities in the military justice
  system, the investigation of the offence is deemed to begin at the
  time of the reporting.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353277){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353277.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of rights

- **[[71.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The rights of victims under this
  Division are to be exercised through the mechanisms provided by law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Connection to Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A victim is entitled to exercise their rights under
  this Division only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are present in Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are a Canadian citizen or a [[permanent
    resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} within the meaning of subsection
    2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353286){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353286.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Victim's liaison officer

- **[[71.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless he or she is of the opinion
  that it is not possible to do so for operational reasons, a commanding
  officer shall, at the request of the victim, appoint an officer or
  non-commissioned member, who satisfies the conditions established in
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, to be a liaison officer
  to assist the victim as provided for in subsection (3). The commanding
  officer shall, to the extent possible, appoint the officer or
  non-commissioned member who has been requested by the victim to be
  their liaison officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  victim's liaison officer, a commanding officer shall appoint another
  officer or non-commissioned member to replace the liaison officer
  during that absence or incapacity, unless it is not possible to do so
  for operational reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of victim's liaison officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Assistance by a victim's liaison officer consists of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} explaining to the victim the manner in which
    service offences are charged, dealt with and tried under the Code of
    Service Discipline; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} obtaining and transmitting to the victim
    information relating to a service offence that the victim has
    requested and to which the victim has a right under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353297){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353297.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation of this Division

**[[71.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.17 .sectionLabel}** This Division is to
be construed and applied in a manner that is reasonable in the
circumstances and in a manner that is not likely to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} interfere with the proper administration of military
  justice, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by causing interference with investigative
    discretion or by causing excessive delay in, compromising or
    hindering the investigation of any service offence,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by causing interference with charge laying
    discretion in respect of any service offence, or by causing
    excessive delay in, compromising or hindering the laying or referral
    of a charge in respect of any service offence, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} by causing interference with prosecutorial
    discretion or by causing excessive delay in, compromising or
    hindering the prosecution of any service offence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} interfere with ministerial discretion in respect of
  any service offence;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} interfere with the discretion that may be exercised
  by any person or body authorized to release an accused person or
  offender into the community;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} endanger the life or safety of any individual; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} cause injury to international relations, national
  defence or national security.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353309){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353309.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation of other Acts,
regulations, etc.

**[[71.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.18 .sectionLabel}** To the extent that
it is possible to do so, every Act of Parliament enacted --- and every
order, rule or regulation made under such an Act --- before, on or after
the day on which this Division comes into force is to be construed and
applied in a manner that is compatible with the rights provided for
under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353313){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353313.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Primacy in event of inconsistency

- **[[71.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, after the application of
  sections 71.17 and 71.18, there is any inconsistency between any
  provision of this Division and any provision of any Act, order, rule
  or regulation referred to in section 71.18, the provision of this
  Division prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Acts, regulations, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of the
  [Canadian Bill of Rights](/eng/acts/C-12.3), the [Canadian Human
  Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6), the [Official Languages
  Act](/eng/acts/O-3.01), the [Access to Information
  Act](/eng/acts/A-1), the [Privacy Act](/eng/acts/P-21) and the
  [Canadian Victims Bill of Rights](/eng/acts/C-23.7) and in respect of
  any orders, rules and regulations made under any of those Acts.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353320){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353320.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

**[[71.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.2 .sectionLabel}** No adverse inference
is to be drawn against a person who is charged with a service offence
from the fact that an individual has been identified as a victim in
relation to the service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353324){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353324.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entering or remaining in Canada

**[[71.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.21 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division is to be construed so as to permit any individual to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter Canada or remain in Canada beyond the end of
  the period for which they are authorized to so remain;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} delay any removal proceedings or prevent the
  enforcement of any removal order; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} delay any extradition proceedings or prevent the
  extradition of any person to or from Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353331){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353331.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Remedies]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353332 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint

- **[[71.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every victim who is of the opinion
  that any of their rights under this Division have been infringed or
  denied by an authority within the military justice system has the
  right to file a complaint in accordance with regulations made by the
  Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints mechanism

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made by the Governor in Council may,
  among other things, provide for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the review of complaints involving alleged
    infringements or denials of rights under this Division;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the power to make recommendations to remedy such
    infringements and denials; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the obligation to notify victims of the result of
    those reviews and of any recommendations that were made.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353342){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353342.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status

**[[71.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.23 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division is to be construed as granting to, or removing from, any
victim, any individual acting on behalf of a victim or any victim's
liaison officer the status of a party, intervenor or observer in any
proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353346){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353346.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No cause of action

**[[71.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.24 .sectionLabel}** No cause of action
or right to damages arises from an infringement or denial of a right
under this Division. For greater certainty, nothing in this section is
to be construed as affecting any other cause of action or right to
damages.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353350){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353350.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal

**[[71.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71.25 .sectionLabel}** No appeal lies from
any decision or order solely on the grounds that a right under this
Division has been infringed or denied.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 7](#1149726-1353354){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149726-1353354.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Service Offences and Punishments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-375333 .Subheading}

#### [Responsibility for Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375334 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties to offences

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person is a party to and guilty of an offence who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} actually commits it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} does or omits to do anything for the purpose of
    aiding any person to commit it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} abets any person in committing it; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} counsels or procures any person to commit it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attempts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, having an intent to commit an
  offence, does or omits to do anything for the purpose of carrying out
  the intention is guilty of an attempt to commit the offence, whether
  or not it was possible under the circumstances to commit the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common intention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where two or more persons form an intention in common
  to carry out an unlawful purpose and to assist each other therein and
  any one of them, in carrying out the common purpose, commits an
  offence, each of them who knew or ought to have known that the
  commission of the offence would be a probable consequence of carrying
  out the common purpose is a party to and guilty of that offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 62
:::

#### [Civil Defences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375348 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules and principles of civil courts
applicable

**[[72.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.1 .sectionLabel}** All rules and
principles that are followed from time to time in the civil courts and
that would render any circumstance a justification or excuse for any act
or omission or a defence to any charge are applicable in any proceedings
under the Code of Service Discipline.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 15
:::

#### [Ignorance of the Law]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375353 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ignorance not to constitute excuse

**[[72.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72.2 .sectionLabel}** The fact that a
person is ignorant of the provisions of this Act, or of any regulations
or of any order or instruction duly notified under this Act, is no
excuse for any offence committed by the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 15
:::

#### [Misconduct of Commanders in Presence of Enemy]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375358 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences by commanders when in action

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** Every officer in command
of a vessel, aircraft, defence establishment, unit or other element of
the Canadian Forces who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} when under orders to carry out an operation of war or
  on coming into contact with an enemy that it is the duty of the
  officer to engage, does not use his utmost exertion to bring the
  officers and non-commissioned members under his command or his vessel,
  aircraft or other materiel into action,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} being in action, does not, during the action, in the
  officer's own person and according to the rank of the officer,
  encourage his officers and non-commissioned members to fight
  courageously,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} when capable of making a successful defence,
  surrenders his vessel, aircraft, defence establishment, materiel, unit
  or other element of the Canadian Forces to the enemy,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} being in action, improperly withdraws from the
  action,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} improperly fails to pursue an enemy or to consolidate
  a position gained,

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} improperly fails to relieve or assist a known friend
  to the utmost of his power, or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} when in action, improperly forsakes his station,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the officer acted
traitorously, shall be sentenced to imprisonment for life, if the
officer acted from cowardice, is liable to imprisonment for life or less
punishment, and in any other case, is liable to dismissal with disgrace
from Her Majesty's service or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 73
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 24
:::

#### [Misconduct of any Person in Presence of Enemy]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375371 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences by any person in presence of
enemy

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} improperly delays or discourages any action against
  the enemy,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} goes over to the enemy,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} when ordered to carry out an operation of war, fails
  to use his utmost exertion to carry the orders into effect,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} improperly abandons or delivers up any defence
  establishment, garrison, place, materiel, post or guard,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} assists the enemy with materiel,

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} improperly casts away or abandons any materiel in the
  presence of the enemy,

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} improperly does or omits to do anything that results
  in the capture by the enemy of persons or the capture or destruction
  by the enemy of materiel,

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} when on watch in the presence or vicinity of the
  enemy, leaves his post before he is regularly relieved or sleeps or is
  drunk,

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} behaves before the enemy in such manner as to show
  cowardice, or

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} does or omits to do anything with intent to imperil
  the success of any of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces
  cooperating therewith,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the person acted
traitorously, shall be sentenced to imprisonment for life, and in any
other case, is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 74
- 1998, c. 35, s. 25
:::

#### [Security]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375387 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences related to security

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} improperly holds communication with or gives
  intelligence to the enemy,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} without authority discloses in any manner whatever
  any information relating to the numbers, position, materiel,
  movements, preparations for movements, operations or preparations for
  operations of any of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces cooperating
  therewith,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} without authority discloses in any manner whatever
  any information relating to a cryptographic system, aid, process,
  procedure, publication or document of any of Her Majesty's Forces or
  of any forces cooperating therewith,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} makes known the parole, watchword, password,
  countersign or identification signal to any person not entitled to
  receive it,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} gives a parole, watchword, password, countersign or
  identification signal different from that which he received,

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} without authority alters or interferes with any
  identification or other signal,

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} improperly occasions false alarms,

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} when acting as sentry or lookout, leaves his post
  before he is regularly relieved or sleeps or is drunk,

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} forces a safeguard or forces or strikes a sentinel,
  or

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} does or omits to do anything with intent to prejudice
  the security of any of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces
  cooperating therewith,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the person acted
traitorously, shall be sentenced to imprisonment for life, and in any
other case, is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 75
- 1998, c. 35, s. 26
:::

#### [Prisoners of War]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375403 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences related to prisoners of war

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} by want of due precaution, or through disobedience of
  orders or wilful neglect of duty, is made a prisoner of war,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} having been made a prisoner of war, fails to rejoin
  Her Majesty's service when able to do so, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} having been made a prisoner of war, serves with or
  aids the enemy,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the person acted
traitorously, shall be sentenced to imprisonment for life, and in any
other case, is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 76
- 1998, c. 35, s. 27
:::

#### [Miscellaneous Operational Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375412 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences related to operations

**[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} does violence to any person bringing materiel to any
  of Her Majesty's Forces or to any forces cooperating therewith,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} irregularly detains any materiel being conveyed to
  any unit or other element of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces
  cooperating therewith,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} irregularly appropriates to the unit or other element
  of the Canadian Forces with which the person is serving any materiel
  being conveyed to any other unit or element of Her Majesty's Forces or
  of any forces cooperating therewith,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} without orders from the person's superior officer,
  improperly destroys or damages any property,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} breaks into any house or other place in search of
  plunder,

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} commits any offence against the property or person of
  any inhabitant or resident of a country in which he is serving,

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} steals from, or with intent to steal searches, the
  person of any person killed or wounded, in the course of warlike
  operations,

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} steals any money or property that has been left
  exposed or unprotected in consequence of warlike operations, or

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} takes otherwise than for the public service any money
  or property abandoned by the enemy,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the person committed the
offence on active service, is liable to imprisonment for life or to less
punishment and, in any other case, is liable to dismissal with disgrace
from Her Majesty's service or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 67
:::

#### [Spies for the Enemy]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375427 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence of being spy

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** Every person who spies
for the enemy is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 78
- 1998, c. 35, s. 28
:::

#### [Mutiny]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375432 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mutiny with violence

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** Every person who joins in
a mutiny that is accompanied by violence is guilty of an offence and on
conviction is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 79
- 1998, c. 35, s. 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mutiny without violence

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** Every person who joins in
a mutiny that is not accompanied by violence is guilty of an offence and
on conviction is liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
fourteen years or to less punishment or, in the case of a ringleader of
the mutiny, to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 80
- 1998, c. 35, s. 28
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences related to mutiny

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} causes or conspires with any other person to cause a
  mutiny,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} endeavours to persuade any person to join in a
  mutiny,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} being present, does not use his utmost endeavours to
  suppress a mutiny, or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} being aware of an actual or intended mutiny, does not
  without delay inform his superior officer thereof,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 71
:::

#### [Seditious Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375450 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advocating governmental change by force

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
publishes or circulates any writing, printing or document in which is
advocated, or who teaches or advocates, the use, without the authority
of law, of force as a means of accomplishing any governmental change
within Canada is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 72
:::

#### [Insubordination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375455 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disobedience of lawful command

**[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** Every person who disobeys
a lawful command of a superior officer is guilty of an offence and on
conviction is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 73
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Striking or offering violence to a
superior officer

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** Every person who strikes
or attempts to strike, or draws or lifts up a weapon against, or uses,
attempts to use or offers violence against, a superior officer is guilty
of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for life or to
less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 74
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insubordinate behaviour

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** Every person who uses
threatening or insulting language to, or behaves with contempt toward, a
superior officer is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
dismissal with disgrace from Her Majesty's service or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quarrels and disturbances

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} quarrels or fights with any other person who is
  subject to the Code of Service Discipline, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} uses provoking speeches or gestures toward a person
  so subject that tend to cause a quarrel or disturbance,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 76
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resisting or escaping from arrest or
custody

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} being concerned in a quarrel, fray or disorder,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} refuses to obey an officer, though of inferior
    rank, who orders the person into arrest, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} strikes or uses or offers violence to any such
    officer,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} strikes or uses or offers violence to any other
  person in whose custody he is placed, whether or not that other person
  is his superior officer and whether or not that other person is
  subject to the Code of Service Discipline,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} resists an escort whose duty it is to apprehend him
  or to have him in charge, or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} breaks out of barracks, station, camp, quarters or
  ship,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 77
:::

#### [Desertion]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375486 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who deserts or attempts to desert is guilty of an offence and
  on conviction, if the person committed the offence on active service
  or under orders for active service, is liable to imprisonment for life
  or to less punishment and, in any other case, is liable to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years or to less
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person deserts who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} being on or having been warned for active service,
    duty during an emergency or other important service, is absent
    without authority with the intention of avoiding that service;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} having been warned that his vessel is under sailing
    orders, is absent without authority with the intention of missing
    that vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} absents himself without authority from his place of
    duty with the intention of remaining absent from his place of duty;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is absent without authority from his place of duty
    and at any time during such absence forms the intention of remaining
    absent from his place of duty; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} while absent with authority from his place of duty,
    with the intention of remaining absent from his place of duty, does
    any act or omits to do anything the natural and probable consequence
    of which act or omission is to preclude the person from being at his
    place of duty at the time required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption of desertion

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been absent without authority for a
  continuous period of six months or more shall, unless the contrary is
  proved, be presumed to have had the intention of remaining absent from
  his place of duty.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 78
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Connivance at desertion

**[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} being aware of the desertion or intended desertion of
  a person from any of Her Majesty's Forces, does not without reasonable
  excuse inform his superior officer forthwith, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fails to take any steps in his power to cause the
  apprehension of a person whom he knows, or has reasonable grounds to
  believe, to be a deserter,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 79
:::

#### [Absence without Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375508 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who absents himself without leave is guilty of an offence and
  on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to
  less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person absents himself without leave who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} without authority leaves his place of duty;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} without authority is absent from his place of duty;
    or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} having been authorized to be absent from his place
    of duty, fails to return to his place of duty at the expiration of
    the period for which the absence of that person was authorized.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 80
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False statement in respect of leave

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
knowingly makes a false statement in respect of prolongation of leave of
absence is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 81
:::

#### [Disgraceful Conduct]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375523 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scandalous conduct by officers

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** Every officer who behaves
in a scandalous manner unbecoming an officer is guilty of an offence and
on conviction shall suffer dismissal with disgrace from Her Majesty's
service or dismissal from Her Majesty's service.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cruel or disgraceful conduct

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** Every person who behaves
in a cruel or disgraceful manner is guilty of an offence and on
conviction is liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years
or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 83
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Traitorous or disloyal utterances

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** Every person who uses
traitorous or disloyal words regarding Her Majesty is guilty of an
offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for a term not
exceeding seven years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 84
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abuse of subordinates

**[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** Every person who strikes
or otherwise ill-treats any person who by reason of rank or appointment
is subordinate to him is guilty of an offence and on conviction is
liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
