- R.S., c. N-4, s. 85
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Making false accusations or statements or
suppressing facts

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes a false accusation against an officer or
  non-commissioned member, knowing the accusation to be false, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} when seeking redress under section 29, knowingly
  makes a false statement affecting the character of an officer or
  non-commissioned member or knowingly, in respect of the redress so
  sought, suppresses any material fact

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 96
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drunkenness

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Drunkenness is an offence and every
  person convicted thereof is liable to imprisonment for less than two
  years or to less punishment, except that, where the offence is
  committed by a non-commissioned member who is not on active service or
  on duty or who has not been warned for duty, no punishment of
  imprisonment, and no punishment of detention for a term in excess of
  ninety days, shall be imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When committed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the offence of
  drunkenness is committed where a person, owing to the influence of
  alcohol or a drug,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is unfit to be entrusted with any duty that the
    person is or may be required to perform; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} behaves in a disorderly manner or in a manner
    likely to bring discredit on Her Majesty's service.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 97
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Malingering, aggravating disease or
infirmity or injuring self or another

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} malingers or feigns or produces disease or infirmity,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} aggravates, or delays the cure of, disease or
  infirmity by misconduct or wilful disobedience of orders, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} wilfully maims or injures himself or any other person
  who is a member of any of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces
  cooperating therewith, whether at the instance of that person or not,
  with intent thereby to render himself or that other person unfit for
  service, or causes himself to be maimed or injured by any person with
  intent thereby to render himself unfit for service,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if he commits the offence on
active service or when under orders for active service or in respect of
a person on active service or under orders for active service, is liable
to imprisonment for life or to less punishment and, in any other case,
is liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 88
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Service Arrest and Custody]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375564 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detaining unnecessarily or failing to
bring up for investigation

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
unnecessarily detains any other person in arrest or confinement without
bringing him to trial, or fails to bring that other person's case before
the proper authority for investigation, is guilty of an offence and on
conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 89
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Setting free without authority or
allowing or assisting escape

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} without authority sets free or authorizes or
  otherwise facilitates the setting free of any person in custody,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} negligently or wilfully allows to escape any person
  who is committed to his charge, or whom it is his duty to guard or
  keep in custody, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} assists any person in escaping or in attempting to
  escape from custody,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if he acted wilfully, is
liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven years or to less
punishment and, in any other case, is liable to imprisonment for less
than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 90
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Escape from custody

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** Every person who, being
in arrest or confinement or in prison or otherwise in lawful custody,
escapes or attempts to escape is guilty of an offence and on conviction
is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 91
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with conditions

**[[101.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101.1 .sectionLabel}** Every person who,
without lawful excuse, fails to comply with a condition imposed under
this Division or Division 3 or 8, or a condition of an undertaking given
under Division 3 or 10, is guilty of an offence and on conviction is
liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 29
- 2013, c. 24, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 101.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hindering arrest or confinement or
withholding assistance when called on

**[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} resists or wilfully obstructs an officer or
  non-commissioned member in the performance of any duty pertaining to
  the arrest, custody or confinement of a person subject to the Code of
  Service Discipline, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} when called on, refuses or neglects to assist an
  officer or non-commissioned member in the performance of any such duty

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 102
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withholding delivery over or assistance
to civil power

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
neglects or refuses to deliver over an officer or non-commissioned
member to the civil power, pursuant to a warrant in that behalf, or to
assist in the lawful apprehension of an officer or non-commissioned
member accused of an offence punishable by a civil court is guilty of an
offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two
years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 103
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Vessels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375596 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Losing, stranding or hazarding vessels

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
wilfully or negligently or through other default loses, strands or
hazards, or suffers to be lost, stranded or hazarded, any of Her
Majesty's Canadian ships or other vessels of the Canadian Forces is
guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to dismissal with
disgrace from Her Majesty's service or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 94
:::

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998, c.
35, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disobedience of captain's orders

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, when in a ship,
  disobeys any lawful command given by the captain of the ship in
  relation to the navigation or handling of the ship or affecting the
  safety of the ship, whether or not the captain is subject to the Code
  of Service Discipline, is guilty of an offence and on conviction is
  liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Command in ship

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, every person of
  whatever rank shall, when the person is in a ship, be under the
  command, in respect of all matters relating to the navigation or
  handling of the ship or affecting the safety of the ship, of the
  captain of the ship, whether or not the captain is subject to the Code
  of Service Discipline.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 96
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Aircraft]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375609 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wrongful acts in relation to aircraft or
aircraft material

**[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the use of or in relation to any aircraft or
  aircraft material, wilfully or negligently or by neglect of or
  contrary to regulations, orders or instructions, does any act or omits
  to do anything, which act or omission causes or is likely to cause
  loss of life or bodily injury to any person,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} wilfully or negligently or by neglect of or contrary
  to regulations, orders or instructions, does any act or omits to do
  anything, which act or omission results or is likely to result in
  damage to or destruction or loss of any of Her Majesty's aircraft or
  aircraft material or of aircraft or aircraft material of any forces
  cooperating with Her Majesty's Forces, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} during a state of war wilfully or negligently causes
  the sequestration by or under the authority of a neutral state or the
  destruction in a neutral state of any of Her Majesty's aircraft or of
  aircraft of any forces cooperating with Her Majesty's Forces,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if the person acted wilfully,
is liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment and, in any
other case, is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 97
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signing inaccurate certificate

**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** Every person who signs
an inaccurate certificate in relation to an aircraft or aircraft
material without taking reasonable steps to ensure that it was accurate,
the proof of taking which steps lies on that person, is guilty of an
offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two
years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 98
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Low flying

**[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** Every person who flies
an aircraft at a height less than the minimum height authorized in the
circumstances is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 99
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disobedience of captain's orders

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, when in an
  aircraft, disobeys any lawful command given by the captain of the
  aircraft in relation to the flying or handling of the aircraft or
  affecting the safety of the aircraft, whether or not the captain is
  subject to the Code of Service Discipline, is guilty of an offence and
  on conviction is liable to imprisonment for life or to less
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Command in aircraft

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every person of whatever rank shall, when the
    person is in an aircraft, be under the command, in respect of all
    matters relating to the flying or handling of the aircraft or
    affecting the safety of the aircraft, of the captain of the
    aircraft, whether or not the captain is subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the aircraft is a glider and is being towed by
    another aircraft, the captain of the glider shall, so long as the
    glider is being towed, be under the command, in respect of all
    matters relating to the flying or handling of the glider or
    affecting the safety of the glider, of the captain of the towing
    aircraft, whether or not the captain of the towing aircraft is
    subject to the Code of Service Discipline.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 100
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Vehicles]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375635 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Improper driving of vehicles

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} drives a vehicle of the Canadian Forces recklessly
    or in a manner that is dangerous to any person or property having
    regard to all the circumstances of the case, or, having charge of
    and being in or on such a vehicle, causes or by wilful neglect
    permits it to be so driven,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} while the person's ability to drive a vehicle of
    the Canadian Forces is impaired by alcohol or a drug, drives or
    attempts to drive such a vehicle, whether it is in motion or not, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} having charge of a vehicle of the Canadian Forces,
    knowingly permits it to be driven by a person whose ability to drive
    such a vehicle is impaired by alcohol or a drug,

  is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment
  for a term not exceeding five years or to less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Occupant of driver's seat deemed
  attempting to drive

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(b), a person who
  occupies the seat ordinarily occupied by a driver of a vehicle shall
  be deemed to have attempted to drive the vehicle, unless that person
  establishes that he did not enter or mount the vehicle for the purpose
  of setting it in motion.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 101
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Improper use of vehicles

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} uses a vehicle of the Canadian Forces for an
  unauthorized purpose,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} without authority uses a vehicle of the Canadian
  Forces for any purpose, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} uses a vehicle of the Canadian Forces contrary to any
  regulation, order or instruction,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 102
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Property]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375655 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Causing fires

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
wilfully or negligently or by neglect of or contrary to regulations,
orders or instructions, does any act or omits to do anything, which act
or omission causes or is likely to cause fire to occur in any materiel,
defence establishment or work for defence is guilty of an offence and on
conviction, if the person acted wilfully, is liable to imprisonment for
life or to less punishment and, in any other case, is liable to
imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 103
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stealing

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who steals is guilty of
  an offence and on conviction, if by reason of the person's rank,
  appointment or employment or as a result of any lawful command the
  person, at the time of the commission of the offence, was entrusted
  with the custody, control or distribution of the thing stolen, is
  liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding fourteen years or to
  less punishment and, in any other case, is liable to imprisonment for
  a term not exceeding seven years or to less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stealing is the act of fraudulently and without
    colour of right taking, or fraudulently and without colour of right
    converting to the use of any person, any thing capable of being
    stolen, with intent

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to deprive, temporarily or absolutely, the owner
      of it or a person who has a special property or interest in it, of
      the thing or of that property or interest,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to pledge it or deposit it as security,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to part with it under a condition with respect
      to its return that the person who parts with it may be unable to
      perform, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} to deal with it in such a manner that it cannot
      be restored in the condition in which it was at the time when it
      was taken and converted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} stealing is committed when the offender moves the
    thing or causes it to move or to be moved, or begins to cause it to
    become movable, with intent to steal it;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the taking or conversion may be fraudulent,
    although effected without secrecy or attempt at concealment; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it is immaterial whether the thing converted was
    taken for the purpose of conversion, or whether it was, at the time
    of the conversion, in the lawful possession of the person who
    converts it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When movable inanimate things capable
  of being stolen

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every inanimate thing that is the property of any
  person and that either is or may be made movable is capable of being
  stolen as soon as it becomes movable, although it is made movable in
  order that it may be stolen.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 104
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Receiving

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
receives or retains in his possession any property obtained by the
commission of any service offence, knowing the property to have been so
obtained, is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 105
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction, damage, loss or improper
disposal

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} wilfully destroys or damages, loses by neglect,
  improperly sells or wastefully expends any public property, non-public
  property or property of any of Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces
  cooperating therewith,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} wilfully destroys, damages or improperly sells any
  property belonging to another person who is subject to the Code of
  Service Discipline, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} sells, pawns or otherwise disposes of any cross,
  medal, insignia or other decoration granted by or with the approval of
  Her Majesty,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 106
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Miscellaneous offences

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} connives at the exaction of an exorbitant price for
  property purchased or rented by a person supplying property or
  services to the Canadian Forces,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} improperly demands or accepts compensation,
  consideration or personal advantage in respect of the performance of
  any military duty or in respect of any matter relating to the
  Department or the Canadian Forces,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} receives directly or indirectly, whether personally
  or by or through any member of his family or person under his control,
  or for his benefit, any gift, loan, promise, compensation or
  consideration, either in money or otherwise, from any person, for
  assisting or favouring any person in the transaction of any business
  relating to any of Her Majesty's Forces, or to any forces cooperating
  therewith or to any mess, institute or canteen operated for the use
  and benefit of members of those forces,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} demands or accepts compensation, consideration or
  personal advantage for convoying a vessel entrusted to his care,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} being in command of a vessel or aircraft, takes or
  receives on board goods or merchandise that he is not authorized to
  take or receive on board, or

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} commits any act of a fraudulent nature not
  particularly specified in sections 73 to 128,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 117
- 1998, c. 35, s. 31
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Tribunals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375700 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section and section 119,
  [[tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a court martial, a military
  judge, an officer conducting a summary hearing, the Grievances
  Committee, the Military Judges Inquiry Committee, the Military Police
  Complaints Commission, a board of inquiry, a commissioner taking
  evidence under this Act and any inquiry committee established under
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contempt

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} being duly summoned or ordered to attend as a
    witness before a tribunal, fails to attend or to remain in
    attendance,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} refuses to take an oath or make a solemn
    affirmation lawfully required by a tribunal to be taken or made,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} refuses to produce any document in the power or
    control of, and lawfully required by a tribunal to be produced by,
    that person,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} refuses when a witness to answer any question to
    which a tribunal may lawfully require an answer,

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} uses insulting or threatening language before, or
    causes any interruption or disturbance in the proceedings of, a
    tribunal, or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} commits any other contempt of a tribunal

  is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment
  for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 118
- 1998, c. 35, s. 32
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 17, 106(E)
- [2019, c. 15, s. 8](#1149728-1353356){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149728-1353356.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118-20131018.html "Link to previous version of section 118"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to appear or attend

**[[118.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118.1 .sectionLabel}** Every person who,
being duly summoned or ordered to appear before a court martial or a
military judge, as an accused, or before an officer conducting a summary
hearing, as a person charged with having committed a service infraction,
fails, without lawful excuse, the proof of which lies on the person, to
appear as summoned or ordered, or to remain in attendance, is guilty of
an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two
years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 32
- [2019, c. 15, s. 9](#1149732-1353359){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149732-1353359.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 118.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False evidence

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** Every person who, when
examined on oath or solemn affirmation before a tribunal, knowingly
gives false evidence is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable
to imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 119
- 1998, c. 35, s. 32
:::

#### [Offence in Relation to the [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7)]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375723 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with order or
obligation

- **[[119.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, without reasonable
  excuse, fails to comply with an order made under section 227.01 of
  this Act or section 490.012 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or
  with an obligation under section 227.06 of this Act, section 490.019
  or 490.02901 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 36.1 of
  the [International Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6) is
  guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
  less than two years or to less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable excuse

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a lawful command that prevents
  a person from complying with an order or obligation is a reasonable
  excuse.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of certain facts by certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings under subsection (1), a certificate of
  a person referred to in paragraph 16(2)(b.1) of the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) stating that the person
  named in the certificate failed to report under section 4, 4.1, 4.2 or
  4.3 --- or provide information under section 5 or notify a person
  under subsection 6(1) --- of that Act is evidence of the statements
  contained in it without proof of the signature or official character
  of the person appearing to have signed it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance and cross-examination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person named in the certificate may, with the
  leave of the court martial, require the attendance of the person who
  signed it for the purpose of cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of intention to produce

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A certificate is not to be received in evidence
  unless, before the commencement of the trial, the party who intends to
  produce it gives the person named in the certificate a copy of it and
  reasonable notice of their intention to produce it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 2
- 2010, c. 17, s. 45
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119.1-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 119.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Offence in Relation to DNA Identification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375737 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with order or summons

- **[[119.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, without reasonable
  excuse, fails to comply with an order made under subsection 196.14(4)
  or 196.24(4) of this Act or subsection 487.051(4) or 487.055(3.11) of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or with a summons referred to in
  subsection 487.055(4) or 487.091(3) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), is guilty of an offence and on conviction is
  liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a lawful command that prevents
  a person from complying with an order or summons is a reasonable
  excuse.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 22, ss. 34, 49
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Billeting]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ill-treatment or non-payment of occupant
or person on whom billeted

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ill-treats, by violence, extortion or making
  disturbance in billets or otherwise, any occupant of a house in which
  any person is billeted or of any premises in which accommodation for
  materiel has been provided, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fails to comply with regulations in respect of
  payment of the just demands of the person on whom he or any officer or
  non-commissioned member under his command is or has been billeted or
  the occupant of premises on which materiel is or has been
  accommodated,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 120
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

#### [Offences in relation to Enrolment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375753 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fraudulent enrolment

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** Every person who,
having been released from Her Majesty's Forces by reason of a sentence
of a court martial or by reason of misconduct, has afterwards been
enrolled in the Canadian Forces without declaring the circumstances of
that release is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to
imprisonment for less than two years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 121
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355615){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355615.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False answers or false information

**[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
knowingly

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes a false answer to any question set out in any
  document required to be completed, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} furnishes any false information or false document,

in relation to the enrolment of that person is guilty of an offence and
on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to
less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 112
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assisting unlawful enrolment

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** Every person who is
concerned in the enrolment of any other person and who knows or has
reasonable grounds to believe that by being enrolled that other person
commits an offence under this Act is guilty of an offence and on
conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 113
:::

#### [Miscellaneous Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375769 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negligent performance of duties

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
negligently performs a military duty imposed on that person is guilty of
an offence and on conviction is liable to dismissal with disgrace from
Her Majesty's service or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 114
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences in relation to documents

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} wilfully or negligently makes a false statement or
  entry in a document made or signed by that person and required for
  official purposes or who, being aware of the falsity of a statement or
  entry in a document so required, orders the making or signing thereof,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} when signing a document required for official
  purposes, leaves in blank any material part for which the signature is
  a voucher, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} with intent to injure any person or with intent to
  deceive, suppresses, defaces, alters or makes away with any document
  or file kept, made or issued for any military or departmental purpose,

is guilty of an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for
a term not exceeding three years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 115
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusing immunization, tests, blood
examination or treatment

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** Every person who, on
receiving an order to submit to inoculation, re-inoculation,
vaccination, re-vaccination, other immunization procedures, immunity
tests, blood examination or treatment against any infectious disease,
wilfully and without reasonable excuse disobeys that order is guilty of
an offence and on conviction is liable to imprisonment for less than two
years or to less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 116
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injurious or destructive handling of
dangerous substances

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
wilfully or negligently or by neglect of or contrary to regulations,
orders or instructions does any act or omits to do anything, in relation
to any thing or substance that may be dangerous to life or property,
which act or omission causes or is likely to cause loss of life or
bodily injury to any person or damage to or destruction of any property,
is guilty of an offence and on conviction, if he acted wilfully, is
liable to imprisonment for life or to less punishment and, in any other
case, is liable to imprisonment for less than two years or to less
punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 117
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conspiracy

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
conspires with any other person, whether or not that other person is
subject to the Code of Service Discipline, to commit an offence under
the Code of Service Discipline is guilty of an offence and on conviction
is liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven years or to
less punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 118
:::

#### [Conduct to the Prejudice of Good Order and Discipline]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375794 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prejudicing good order or discipline

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  act, conduct, disorder or neglect to the prejudice of good order and
  discipline is an offence and every person convicted thereof is liable
  to dismissal with disgrace from Her Majesty's service or to less
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and contraventions prejudicial
  to good order and discipline

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An act or omission constituting an offence under
  section 72 or a contravention by any person of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any of the provisions of this Act,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any regulations, orders or instructions published
    for the general information and guidance of the Canadian Forces or
    any part thereof, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any general, garrison, unit, station, standing,
    local or other orders,

  is an act, conduct, disorder or neglect to the prejudice of good order
  and discipline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attempts to commit offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An attempt to commit any of the offences prescribed
  in sections 73 to 128 is an act, conduct, disorder or neglect to the
  prejudice of good order and discipline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (2) or (3) affects the
  generality of subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not intended to cover offences
  elsewhere provided for

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person may be charged under this section with any
  offence for which special provision is made in sections 73 to 128 but
  the conviction of a person so charged is not invalid by reason only of
  the charge being in contravention of this subsection unless it appears
  that an injustice has been done to the person charged by reason of the
  contravention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer's responsibility not affected

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The responsibility of any officer for the
  contravention of subsection (5) is not affected by the validity of any
  conviction on the charge in contravention of that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 119
:::

#### [Offences Punishable by Ordinary Law]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375814 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service trial of civil offences

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  act or omission

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that takes place in Canada and is punishable under
    Part VII, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
    Parliament, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that takes place outside Canada and would, if it
    had taken place in Canada, be punishable under Part VII, the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of Parliament,

  is an offence under this Division and every person convicted thereof
  is liable to suffer punishment as provided in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), if a court martial
  convicts a person under subsection (1), it shall,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the conviction was in respect of an offence

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} committed in Canada under Part VII, the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of Parliament and for which
      a minimum punishment is prescribed, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} committed outside Canada under section 235 of
      the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

    impose a punishment in accordance with the enactment prescribing the
    minimum punishment for the offence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} impose the punishment prescribed for the offence
      by Part VII, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or that other
      Act, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} impose dismissal with disgrace from Her
      Majesty's service or less punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Code of Service Discipline applies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All provisions of the Code of Service Discipline in
  respect of a punishment of imprisonment for life, for two years or
  more or for less than two years, and a fine, apply in respect of
  punishments imposed under paragraph (2)(a) or subparagraph (2)(b)(i).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section is in derogation of the
  authority conferred by other sections of the Code of Service
  Discipline to charge, deal with and try a person alleged to have
  committed any offence set out in sections 73 to 129 and to impose the
  punishment for that offence described in the section prescribing that
  offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 130
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 33, 92
- [2019, c. 15, s. 10](#1149736-1353361){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149736-1353361.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to Attorney General

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Act, the reference in section 320.4 of the [Criminal
Code](/eng/acts/C-46) to the [[Attorney General]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
includes the Attorney General of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 131
- R.S., 1985, c. 27 (1st Supp.), s. 187
- 2018, c. 21, s. 43
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences under law applicable outside
Canada

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  act or omission that takes place outside Canada and would, under the
  law applicable in the place where the act or omission occurred, be an
  offence if committed by a person subject to that law is an offence
  under this Division, and every person who is found guilty thereof is
  liable to suffer punishment as provided in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment for offence under law
  applicable outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), where a court martial
  finds a person guilty of an offence under subsection (1), the court
  martial shall impose the punishment in the scale of punishments that
  it considers appropriate, having regard to the punishment prescribed
  by the law applicable in the place where the act or omission occurred
  and the punishment prescribed for the same or a similar offence in
  this Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
  Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Code of Service
  Discipline

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All provisions of the Code of Service Discipline in
  respect of a punishment of imprisonment for life, for two years or
  more or for less than two years, and a fine, apply in respect of
  punishments imposed under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section is in derogation of the
  authority conferred by other sections of the Code of Service
  Discipline to charge, deal with and try a person alleged to have
  committed any offence set out in sections 73 to 130 and to impose the
  punishment for that offence described in the section prescribing that
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of customs laws

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where an act or omission constituting an offence
  under subsection (1) contravenes the customs laws applicable in the
  place where the offence was committed, any officer appointed under the
  regulations for the purposes of this section may seize and detain any
  goods by means of or in relation to which the officer believes on
  reasonable grounds that the offence was committed and, if any person
  is convicted of the offence under subsection (1), the goods may, in
  accordance with regulations made by the Governor in Council, be
  forfeited to Her Majesty and may be disposed of as provided by those
  regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 132
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 34, 92
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355618){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355618.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-132-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 132"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Conviction of Cognate Offence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375853 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person charged with desertion

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person charged with desertion may be found guilty of attempting to
  desert or of being absent without leave.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person charged with attempt to desert

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person charged with attempting to desert may be
  found guilty of being absent without leave.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 122
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person charged with violent offence
against officer

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person charged with any one of the offences prescribed in section 84
  may be found guilty of any other offence prescribed in that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person charged with insubordinate
  behaviour

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person charged with any one of the offences
  prescribed in section 85 may be found guilty of any other offence
  prescribed in that section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 122
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conviction of offence in circumstances
involving lower punishment

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** A person charged with a
service offence may, on failure of proof of an offence having been
committed under circumstances involving a higher punishment, be found
guilty of the same offence as having been committed under circumstances
involving a lower punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 122
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on service trial of civil offences

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Where a person is
charged with an offence under section 130 and the charge is one on which
he might, in the event of trial by a civil court in Canada for that
offence, have been found guilty of any other offence, the person may be
found guilty of that other offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 122
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence charged, attempt proved

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the complete commission of an offence charged is not proved but the
  evidence establishes an attempt to commit the offence, the accused
  person may be found guilty of the attempt.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 137
- 1992, c. 16, s. 1
- 2013, c. 24, s. 18(E)
- [2019, c. 15, s. 11](#1149741-1353364){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149741-1353364.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special finding of guilty

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** A court martial may,
instead of making a finding of not guilty, make a special finding of
guilty if it concludes that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the facts proved in respect of an offence being tried
  by it differ materially from the facts alleged in the statement of
  particulars but are sufficient to establish the commission of the
  offence charged; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the difference between the facts proved and the facts
  alleged in the statement of particulars has not prejudiced the accused
  person in their defence.

If the court martial makes a special finding of guilty, it shall state
the differences between the facts proved and the facts alleged in the
statement of particulars.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 138
- [2019, c. 15, s. 12](#1149744-1353372){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149744-1353372.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Punishments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375892 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scale of punishments

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following punishments may be imposed in respect of service offences
  and each of those punishments is a punishment less than every
  punishment preceding it:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for life;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for two years or more;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} dismissal with disgrace from Her Majesty's service;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} imprisonment for less than two years;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} dismissal from Her Majesty's service;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} detention;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} reduction in rank;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} forfeiture of seniority;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} severe reprimand;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} reprimand;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} fine; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} minor punishments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[less
  punishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a punishment for an offence is specified by the
  Code of Service Discipline and it is further provided in the
  alternative that on conviction the offender is liable to less
  punishment, the expression [[less punishment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means any one or more of the punishments lower in the scale of
  punishments than the specified punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 139
- 1998, c. 35, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imprisonment for shorter term

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** Every person who, on
conviction of a service offence, is liable to imprisonment for life,
other than as a minimum punishment, or for a term of years or other term
may be sentenced to imprisonment for a shorter term.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 140
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 36
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal as accompanying punishment

- **[[140.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial imposes a
  punishment of imprisonment for life or for two years or more on an
  officer or a non-commissioned member, the court martial may in
  addition, notwithstanding any other provision of this Division, impose
  a punishment of dismissal with disgrace from Her Majesty's service or
  a punishment of dismissal from Her Majesty's service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal as accompanying punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial imposes a punishment of
  imprisonment for less than two years on an officer or a
  non-commissioned member, the court martial may in addition,
  notwithstanding any other provision of this Division, impose a
  punishment of dismissal from Her Majesty's service.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 36
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction in rank as accompanying
punishment

**[[140.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140.2 .sectionLabel}** Where a court
martial imposes a punishment of imprisonment on an officer or a
non-commissioned member, the court martial may in addition,
notwithstanding any other provision of this Division, impose a
punishment of reduction in rank, that may be

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an officer, to the lowest commissioned
  rank; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a non-commissioned member, to the
  lowest rank to which under the regulations the non-commissioned member
  can be reduced.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 36
:::

**[[140.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140.3 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2013,
c. 24, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-140.3-20111202.html "Link to previous version of section 140.3"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[140.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140.4 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2013,
c. 24, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-140.4-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 140.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal with disgrace

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  court martial imposes a punishment of dismissal with disgrace from Her
  Majesty's service on an officer or non-commissioned member, it may, in
  addition, despite any other provision of this Division, impose a
  punishment of imprisonment for less than two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of dismissal

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A punishment of dismissal with disgrace from Her
  Majesty's service or dismissal from Her Majesty's service is deemed to
  be carried out as of the date on which the release of an officer or a
  non-commissioned member from the Canadian Forces is effected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person on whom a punishment of dismissal with
  disgrace from Her Majesty's service has been carried out is not,
  except in an emergency or unless that punishment is subsequently set
  aside or altered, eligible to serve Her Majesty again in any military
  or civil capacity.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 141
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 37, 92
- [2019, c. 15, s. 13(E)](#1149746-1353374){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149746-1353374.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  punishment of detention is subject to the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} detention may not exceed ninety days and a person
    sentenced to detention may not be subject to detention for more than
    ninety days consecutively by reason of more than one conviction; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no officer may be sentenced to detention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction in rank during detention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A non-commissioned member above the rank of private
  who is sentenced to detention is deemed to be reduced to the rank of
  private until the sentence of detention is completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 142
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 38
- 2013, c. 24, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction in rank

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  punishment of reduction in rank applies to officers above the rank of
  second lieutenant and to non-commissioned members above the rank of
  private.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The punishment of reduction in rank does not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} involve reduction to a rank lower than that to
    which under regulations the offender can be reduced; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a commissioned officer, involve
    reduction to a rank lower than commissioned rank.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 143
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture of seniority

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** Where a court martial
imposes a punishment of forfeiture of seniority on an officer or
non-commissioned member, the court martial shall in passing sentence
specify the period for which seniority is to be forfeited.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 144
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 39
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fine

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fine must be imposed in a stated amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of payment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of payment of a fine are in the discretion
  of the court martial that imposes the fine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of terms of payment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The terms of payment of a fine may be varied by the
  military judge who imposed the fine or a military judge designated by
  the Chief Military Judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 145
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 39
- [2019, c. 15, s. 14](#1149750-1353377){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149750-1353377.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355621){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355621.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil enforcement of fines

- **[[145.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an offender is in default of
  payment of a fine, the Minister may, in addition to any other method
  provided by law for recovering the fine, by filing the conviction,
  enter as a judgment the amount of the fine and costs, if any, in any
  court in Canada that has jurisdiction to enter a judgment for that
  amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of filing order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judgment that is entered under this section is
  enforceable in the same manner as if it were a judgment obtained by
  the Minister in civil proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 21
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor punishments

**[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** Minor punishments shall
be such as are prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in
Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 125
:::

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019, c.
15, s. 15\]]{.Repealed}

Version](section-147-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 147"){.PITLink}

#### [Prohibition Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-375986 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition order

- **[[147.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial considers it
  desirable, in the interests of the safety of an offender or of any
  other person, it shall --- in addition to any other punishment that
  may be imposed for the offence --- make an order prohibiting the
  offender from possessing any firearm, cross-bow, prohibited weapon,
  restricted weapon, prohibited device, ammunition, prohibited
  ammunition or explosive substance, or all such things, on convicting
  or discharging absolutely the offender of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence in the commission of which violence
    against a person was used, threatened or attempted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence that involves, or the subject-matter of
    which is, a firearm, a cross-bow, a prohibited weapon, a restricted
    weapon, a prohibited device, any ammunition, any prohibited
    ammunition or an explosive substance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence relating to the contravention of any of
    sections 5 to 7 of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances
    Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8);

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} an offence relating to the contravention of any
    of sections 9 to 14 of the [Cannabis Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an offence that is punishable under section 130 and
    that is described in paragraph 109(1)(b) of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of prohibition order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) begins on the day
  the order is made and ends on the day specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless it specifies otherwise, an order made under
  subsection (1) does not prohibit an officer or a non-commissioned
  member from possessing any thing necessary for the performance of
  their duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A court martial that makes an order under
  subsection (1) shall without delay cause the Registrar of Firearms
  appointed under section 82 of the [Firearms Act](/eng/acts/F-11.6) to
  be notified of the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 39, s. 176
- 1996, c. 19, s. 83.1
- 2012, c. 1, s. 50
- 2013, c. 24, s. 22
- 2018, c. 16, ss. 168, 188(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147.1-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 147.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to surrender

**[[147.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.2 .sectionLabel}** A court martial
that makes an order under subsection 147.1(1) may, in the order, require
the offender against whom the order is made to surrender to a member of
the military police or to the offender's commanding officer

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any thing the possession of which is prohibited by
  the order that is in the possession of the offender on the
  commencement of the order; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} every authorization, licence and registration
  certificate relating to any thing the possession of which is
  prohibited by the order that is held by the offender on the
  commencement of the order.

The court martial shall specify in the order a reasonable period for
surrendering the thing or document, and during that period section
117.01 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) does not apply to the
offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 39, s. 176
- 2013, c. 24, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-147.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 147.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture

- **[[147.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless an order made under
  subsection 147.1(1) specifies otherwise, every thing the possession of
  which is prohibited by the order that, on the commencement of the
  order, is in the possession of the person against whom the order is
  made is forfeited to Her Majesty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every thing forfeited to Her Majesty under
  subsection (1) shall be disposed of or otherwise dealt with as the
  Minister directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 39, s. 176
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorizations revoked or amended

**[[147.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.4 .sectionLabel}** Every
authorization, licence and registration certificate relating to any
thing the possession of which is prohibited by an order made under
subsection 147.1(1) and issued to a person against whom the order is
made is, on the commencement of the order, revoked, or amended, as the
case may be, to the extent of the prohibitions in the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 39, s. 176
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to owner

**[[147.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.5 .sectionLabel}** Where the Minister
is, on application for an order under this section, satisfied that a
person, other than the person against whom an order under subsection
147.1(1) was made,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is the owner of any thing that is or may be forfeited
  to Her Majesty under subsection 147.3(1) and is lawfully entitled to
  possess it, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} had no reasonable grounds to believe that the thing
  would or might be used in the commission of the offence in respect of
  which the order was made,

the Minister shall order that the thing be returned to the owner or the
proceeds of any sale of the thing be paid to that owner, or, if the
thing was destroyed, that an amount equal to the value of the thing be
paid to the owner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 39, s. 176
:::

#### [Order to Abstain from Communicating]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353379 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If injury or damage feared

- **[[147.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An information may, in accordance
  with regulations made by the Governor in Council, be laid before a
  military judge by or on behalf of any victim who fears on reasonable
  grounds that a person who is subject to the Code of Service Discipline
  will cause physical or emotional harm to the victim, to the victim's
  spouse, to a person who is cohabiting with the victim in a conjugal
  relationship, having so cohabited for a period of at least one year,
  or to the victim's child or will cause damage to the victim's
  property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties to appear

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The military judge who receives the information shall
  cause the parties to appear before the military judge, either in
  person or otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may, if satisfied by the evidence
  that there are reasonable grounds for the victim's fears, order that
  the person who is subject to the Code of Service Discipline and who is
  referred to in the information

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} abstain from communicating, directly or indirectly,
    with any of the following individuals who are specified in the
    order:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the victim,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the victim's spouse,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a person who is cohabiting with the victim in a
      conjugal relationship, having so cohabited for a period of at
      least one year,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the victim's child;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} refrain from going to any place specified in the
    order; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} comply with any other condition specified in the
    order that the military judge considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of military judge

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, for operational reasons, no military judge is
  available, the information may be laid before a commanding officer
  and, if it is so laid, that commanding officer has all the powers of a
  military judge that are set out in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every decision of a commanding officer to make or not
  make an order under subsection (3) must be reviewed as soon as
  feasible by a military judge. The military judge may, at the
  conclusion of the review, amend any order that was made and, if none
  was made, make any order under that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 16](#1149755-1353399){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149755-1353399.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Intermittent Sentences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376028 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imprisonment or detention

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court martial that sentences an offender to imprisonment or detention
  for a period of 14 days or less may, on application of the offender
  and having regard to the offender's age and character, the nature of
  the offence and the circumstances surrounding its commission, and the
  availability of appropriate accommodation to ensure compliance with
  the sentence, order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the sentence be served intermittently at the
    times specified in the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the offender comply with any conditions
    prescribed in the order when the offender is not in confinement
    during the period during which the sentence is served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to vary intermittent
  sentence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An offender who is ordered to serve a sentence
  intermittently may apply to have the sentence served on consecutive
  days by applying to a military judge after giving notice to the
  Director of Military Prosecutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New sentence of imprisonment or
  detention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial imposes a sentence of imprisonment
  or detention on an offender who is subject to an intermittent sentence
  in respect of another offence, the unexpired portion of the
  intermittent sentence is to be served on consecutive days unless the
  court martial orders otherwise.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing into breach of conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On application by a representative of the Canadian
  Forces who is a member of a class designated for that purpose by
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, a determination of
  whether an offender has breached a condition imposed under paragraph
  (1)(b) may be made by a military judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of breach

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a military judge determines, after giving the
  offender and the applicant an opportunity to make representations,
  that the offender has breached a condition, the military judge may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} revoke the order made under subsection (1) and
    order that the offender serve the sentence on consecutive days; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vary any conditions imposed under paragraph (1)(b)
    and substitute or add other conditions as the military judge sees
    fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 148
- 2013, c. 24, s. 24
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149989-1355655){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149989-1355655.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-148-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 148"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Incarceration under more than one Sentence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376050 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrent punishment

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** Where a person is under
a sentence imposed by a court martial that includes a punishment
involving incarceration and another court martial subsequently passes a
new sentence that also includes a punishment involving incarceration,
both punishments of incarceration shall, subject to section 745.51 of
the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), after the date of the pronouncement
of the new sentence, run concurrently but the punishment higher in the
scale of punishments shall be served first.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 149
- 2011, c. 5, s. 7
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355624){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355624.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-149-20111202.html "Link to previous version of section 149"){.PITLink}
:::

coming into force, 2005, c. 22, s. 47\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Punishment for Certain Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376056 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment for certain offences

- **[[149.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding anything in this
  Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
  Parliament, a person convicted of an offence under this Act for which
  the maximum punishment is imprisonment for five years or more, or an
  offence punishable under section 130 that is an indictable offence
  under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or any other Act of
  Parliament, other than an offence for which a sentence of imprisonment
  for life is imposed as a minimum punishment, where the act or omission
  constituting the offence also constitutes a terrorist activity, is
  liable to imprisonment for life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offender must be notified

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply unless the Director of
  Military Prosecutions satisfies the court martial that the offender,
  before making a plea, was notified that the application of that
  subsection would be sought by reason of the act or omission
  constituting the offence also constituting a terrorist activity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 41, s. 99
:::

24, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S.,

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Arrest and Pre-Trial Custody]{.HTitleText2} {#h-376067 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376068 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Division.

[[custody review officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[custody review officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a
    person in custody, means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer who is the person's commanding
      officer, or an officer who is designated by that officer; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is not practical for an officer referred to
      in paragraph (a) to act as the custody review officer, the officer
      who is the commanding officer of the unit or element where the
      person is in custody or an officer who is designated by that
      officer. ([officier réviseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence that is punishable under section 130
      that is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} listed in section 469 of the [Criminal
        Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable by imprisonment for life
        under subsection 5(3), 6(3) or 7(2) of the [Controlled Drugs and
        Substances Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8), or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an offence of conspiring to commit an offence
        under any subsection referred to in subparagraph (ii);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act where the minimum
      punishment is imprisonment for life;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act for which a punishment
      higher in the scale of punishments than imprisonment for less than
      two years may be awarded that is alleged to have been committed
      while at large after having been released in respect of another
      offence pursuant to the provisions of this Division or Division
      10;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act that is a criminal
      organization offence; or

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act that is a terrorism
      offence. ([infraction désignée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 153
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 47
- 1998, c. 35, s. 40
- 2001, c. 41, s. 100
- 2012, c. 1, s. 48(E)
- 2013, c. 24, s. 26(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-153-20120313.html "Link to previous version of section 153"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Authority to Arrest]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376085 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General authority

- **[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who has committed, is
  found committing or is believed on reasonable grounds to have
  committed a service offence, or who is charged with having committed a
  service offence, may be placed under arrest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonably necessary force

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person authorized to effect arrest under this
  Division may use such force as is reasonably necessary for that
  purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 154
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 48
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of officers

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may, without a warrant, in the circumstances described in
  section 154, arrest or order the arrest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any non-commissioned member;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any officer of equal or lower rank; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any officer of higher rank who is engaged in a
    quarrel, fray or disorder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of non-commissioned members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A non-commissioned member may, without a warrant, in
  the circumstances described in section 154, arrest or order the arrest
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any non-commissioned member of lower rank; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any non-commissioned member of equal or higher rank
    who is engaged in a quarrel, fray or disorder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitations on power of arrest

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Unless ordered to do so by a superior officer, an
  officer or non-commissioned member shall not order the arrest of a
  person, nor arrest a person, without a warrant for an offence that is
  not a serious offence if:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have reasonable grounds to believe that the
    public interest may be satisfied without so arresting the person,
    having regard to all the circumstances including the need to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} establish the person's identity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} secure or preserve evidence of or relating to
      the offence, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} prevent the continuation or repetition of the
      offence or the commission of another offence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have no reasonable grounds to believe that, if
    the person is not so arrested, the person will fail to attend before
    a court martial in order to be dealt with according to law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest of persons other than officers
  or non-commissioned members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is not an officer or
  non-commissioned member but who was subject to the Code of Service
  Discipline at the time of the alleged commission by that person of a
  service offence may, without a warrant, be arrested or ordered to be
  arrested by such person as any commanding officer may designate for
  that purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 155
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 27
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149991-1355657){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149991-1355657.html"
  role="button"}
:::
