[Previous
Version](section-155-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 155"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of military police

- **[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Officers and non-commissioned
  members who are appointed as members of the military police under
  regulations made for the purposes of this section may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} detain or arrest without a warrant any person who
    is subject to the Code of Service Discipline, regardless of the
    person's rank or status, who has committed, is found committing, is
    believed on reasonable grounds to be about to commit or to have
    committed a service offence or who is charged with having committed
    a service offence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercise such other powers for carrying out the
    Code of Service Discipline as are prescribed in regulations made by
    the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest without warrant --- limitations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the military police shall not arrest a
  person without a warrant for an offence that is not a serious offence
  if paragraphs 155(2.1)(a) and (b) apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 156
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), ss. 49, 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 41
- 2013, c. 24, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-156-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 156"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issue of warrants

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  commanding officer, and every officer to whom a commanding officer
  has, under section 162.94, delegated the power to conduct a summary
  hearing, may by a warrant under his or her hand authorize any person
  to arrest any person subject to the Code of Service Discipline who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has committed a service offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is believed on reasonable grounds to have committed
    a service offence; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is charged under this Act with having committed a
    service offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer authorized to issue a warrant under this
  section shall not, unless the officer has certified on the face of the
  warrant that the exigencies of the service so require, issue a warrant
  for the arrest of any officer of rank higher than the rank held by the
  officer so authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An officer authorized to issue a warrant under this
  section shall not issue a warrant for the arrest of any person who is
  a member of, serving with, or attached or seconded to the same unit of
  the Canadian Forces as the officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of warrants

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In any warrant issued under this section, the offence
  in respect of which the warrant is issued shall be stated and the
  names of more persons than one in respect of the same offence, or
  several offences of the same nature, may be included.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section shall be deemed to be in
  derogation of the authority that any person, including an officer or
  non-commissioned member, may have under other sections of this Act or
  otherwise under the law of Canada to arrest any other person without a
  warrant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 157
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), ss. 50, 60
- [2019, c. 15, s. 17](#1149757-1353407){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149757-1353407.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-157-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 157"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Action following Arrest]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376138 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release from custody

- **[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person arrested under this Act shall, as soon as is practicable, be
  released from custody by the person making the arrest, unless the
  person making the arrest believes on reasonable grounds that it is
  necessary that the person under arrest be retained in custody having
  regard to all the circumstances, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the gravity of the offence alleged to have been
    committed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the need to establish the identity of the person
    under arrest;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the need to secure or preserve evidence of or
    relating to the offence alleged to have been committed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the need to ensure that the person under arrest
    will appear before a court martial or civil court to be dealt with
    according to law;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the need to prevent the continuation or repetition
    of the offence alleged to have been committed or the commission of
    any other offence; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the necessity to ensure the safety and security of
    the person under arrest, any victim of the offence, or any other
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention in custody

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an arrested person is to be retained in custody,
  the person shall be placed in service custody or civil custody. Such
  force as is reasonably necessary for the purpose may be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to receive into service custody

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The officer or non-commissioned member in charge of a
  guard or a guard-room or a member of the military police shall receive
  and keep a person under arrest who is committed to their custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Account in writing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person who commits a person under arrest to
  service custody shall, at the time of committal, deliver to the
  officer or non-commissioned member into whose custody the person under
  arrest is committed a signed account in writing setting out why the
  person under arrest is being committed to custody.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 158
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), ss. 51, 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 29
- [2019, c. 15, s. 18](#1149771-1353410){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149771-1353410.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 158"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of custody

- **[[158.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The officer or non-commissioned
  member into whose custody a person under arrest is committed shall, as
  soon as practicable, and in any case within twenty-four hours after
  the arrest of the person committed to custody, deliver a report of
  custody, in writing, to the custody review officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report of custody must set out the name of the
  person in custody, an account of the offence alleged to have been
  committed by that person so far as it is known and the name and rank,
  if any, of the person who committed the person into service custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations concerning release

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before the report of custody is delivered to the
  custody review officer,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the report and the account in writing
    must be provided to the person in custody; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person in custody must be given the opportunity
    to make representations concerning the person's release from
    custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations to be reduced to
  writing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Representations concerning release made by or on
  behalf of the person in custody must be reduced to writing or recorded
  by any other means.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accompanying documents

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When the report of custody is delivered, it must be
  accompanied by the account in writing and any representations made by
  or on behalf of the person in custody or a statement confirming that
  the person was given the opportunity to make representations but did
  not do so.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

#### [Initial Review]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376171 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of report of custody

- **[[158.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The custody review officer shall
  review the report of custody and the accompanying documents as soon as
  practicable after receiving them and in any case within forty-eight
  hours after the arrest of the person committed to custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to release

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After reviewing the report of custody and the
  accompanying documents, the custody review officer shall direct that
  the person committed to custody be released immediately unless the
  officer believes on reasonable grounds that it is necessary that the
  person be retained in custody, having regard to all the circumstances,
  including those set out in subsection 158(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing duty to release

**[[158.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.3 .sectionLabel}** If, at any time
after receiving the report of custody and before the person in custody
is brought before a military judge, the custody review officer no longer
believes that the grounds to retain the person in custody exist, the
custody review officer shall direct that the person be released from
custody.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to retain in custody if designated
offence

**[[158.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.4 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding
subsection 158.2(2) and section 158.3, if the person in custody is
charged with having committed a designated offence, the custody review
officer shall direct that the person be retained in custody.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to review where charge not laid

**[[158.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.5 .sectionLabel}** If a charge is not
laid within seventy-two hours after the person in custody was arrested,
the custody review officer shall determine why a charge has not been
laid and reconsider whether it remains necessary to retain the person in
custody.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release with or without conditions

- **[[158.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The custody review officer may
  direct that the person be released without conditions or that the
  person be released and, as a condition of release, direct the person
  to comply with any of the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} remain under military authority;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} report at specified times to a specified military
    authority;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} remain within the confines of a specified defence
    establishment or at a location within a geographical area;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} abstain from communicating with any witness or
    specified person, or refrain from going to any specified place; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} comply with such other reasonable conditions as are
    specified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of victim's safety and
  security

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the custody review officer directs that the
  person be released, with or without conditions, the custody review
  officer shall include in the direction a statement that he or she has
  considered the safety and security of every victim of the alleged
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to victim

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The custody review officer shall, on request by a
  victim of the alleged offence, cause a copy of the direction to be
  given to the victim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A direction to release a person with or without
  conditions may, on application, be reviewed by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the custody review officer is an officer
    designated by a commanding officer, that commanding officer; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the custody review officer is a commanding
    officer, the next superior officer to whom the commanding officer is
    responsible in matters of discipline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} After giving a representative of the Canadian Forces
  and the released person an opportunity to be heard, the officer
  conducting the review may make any direction that a custody review
  officer may make under subsection (1). If he or she makes a direction,
  subsections (1.1) and (1.2) apply with any necessary modifications.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 30(F)
- [2019, c. 15, s. 19](#1149780-1353417){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149780-1353417.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158.6-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 158.6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction --- no communication

**[[158.61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.61 .sectionLabel}** If a custody
review officer directs that a person be retained in custody, the custody
review officer may also direct that the person abstain from
communicating, directly or indirectly, with any victim, witness or other
person specified in the direction except in accordance with any
conditions specified in the direction that the officer considers
necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 20](#1149787-1353421){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149787-1353421.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Review by Military Judge]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376207 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of directions

- **[[158.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A military judge may, on application
  by counsel for the Canadian Forces or by a person released with
  conditions and after giving counsel and the released person an
  opportunity to be heard, review any of the following directions and
  make any direction that a custody review officer may make under
  subsection 158.6(1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a direction that was reviewed under subsection
    158.6(2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a direction that was made under subsection
    158.6(3); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a direction that was made under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A military judge shall not direct that a condition,
  other than the condition of keeping the peace and being of good
  behaviour, be imposed unless counsel for the Canadian Forces shows
  cause why it is necessary that the condition be imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further applications

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an application under this section has been heard,
  another application under this section may not be made with respect to
  the same person, except with leave of a military judge, before the
  expiry of 30 days from the day on which a decision was made in respect
  of the most recent application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing by military judge

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  custody review officer who does not direct the release of a person
  from custody shall, as soon as practicable, cause the person to be
  taken before a military judge for the purpose of a hearing to
  determine whether the person is to be retained in custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable operational considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining when it is practicable to cause the
  person to be taken before a military judge, the custody review officer
  may have regard to the constraints of military operations, including
  the location of the unit or element where the person is in custody and
  the circumstances under which it is deployed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 159
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 52
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Onus on Canadian Forces

**[[159.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.1 .sectionLabel}** When the person
retained in custody is taken before a military judge, the military judge
shall direct that the person be released from custody unless counsel for
the Canadian Forces, or in the absence of counsel a person appointed by
the custody review officer, shows cause why the continued retention of
the person in custody is justified or why any other direction under this
Division should be made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justification for retention in custody

**[[159.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.2 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 159.1 and 159.3, the retention of a person in custody is only
justified when one or more of the following grounds have been
established to the satisfaction of the military judge:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} custody is necessary to ensure the person's
  attendance before a court martial or civil court to be dealt with
  according to law;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} custody is necessary for the protection or the safety
  of the public, having regard to all the circumstances including any
  substantial likelihood that the person will, if released from custody,
  commit an offence or interfere with the administration of military
  justice; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} custody is necessary to maintain public trust in the
  administration of military justice, having regard to the circumstances
  including the apparent strength of the prosecution's case, the gravity
  of the nature of the offence, the circumstances surrounding its
  commission and the potential for a lengthy term of imprisonment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 32
- [2019, c. 15, s. 21](#1149789-1353423){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149789-1353423.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.2-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 159.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Onus on person in custody

- **[[159.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 159.1, if
  the person in custody is charged with having committed a designated
  offence, the military judge shall direct that the person be retained
  in custody until dealt with according to law, unless the person shows
  cause why the person's retention in custody is not justified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release on undertaking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person in custody shows cause why the person's
  retention in custody is not justified, the military judge shall direct
  that the person be released from custody on giving any undertaking to
  comply with any of the conditions referred to in section 158.6 that
  the military judge considers appropriate, unless the person in custody
  shows cause why the giving of an undertaking is not justified.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Direction --- no communication

**[[159.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.31 .sectionLabel}** If a military
judge directs that a person be retained in custody, the military judge
may also direct that the person abstain from communicating, directly or
indirectly, with any victim, witness or other person specified in the
direction, except in accordance with any conditions specified in the
direction that the military judge considers necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 22](#1149793-1353427){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149793-1353427.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release with or without undertaking

- **[[159.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may direct that
  the person be released without conditions or that the person be
  released on the giving of an undertaking to comply with any of the
  conditions referred to in section 158.6 that the military judge
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of undertaking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The undertaking under which a person is released may
  be varied

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by direction of a military judge on application
    with reasonable notice being given; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the written consent of the person and the
    Director of Military Prosecutions.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing may be adjourned

**[[159.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.5 .sectionLabel}** The military judge
may adjourn the hearing on the military judge's own motion or on
application, but the adjournment may not be for more than three clear
days except with the consent of the person in custody.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternate means of hearing

- **[[159.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may direct that
  the hearing be conducted wholly or in part by the means of a
  telecommunications device, including by telephone, if the military
  judge is satisfied that the benefit of a hearing by that device
  outweighs the potential prejudice to the person in custody of
  conducting a hearing by that device.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations and factors to be
  considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to make the direction, the
  military judge shall take into account

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the location of the person in custody;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gravity of the offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances under which the unit or element
    detaining the person in custody is deployed;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the availability of counsel for the Canadian Forces
    and the person in custody;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the limitations of available telecommunications
    devices;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the time required to bring the person in custody
    and the person's counsel before the military judge; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other matter that the military judge considers
    relevant.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

- **[[159.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall include in
  the minutes of any proceedings under this Division the reasons for any
  direction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of victim's safety and
  security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge directs that a person be
  released, with or without conditions, the military judge shall include
  in the direction a statement that he or she has considered the safety
  and security of every victim of the alleged offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to victim

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall, on request by a victim of
  the alleged offence, cause a copy of the direction to be given to the
  victim.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 23](#1149795-1353435){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149795-1353435.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159.7-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 159.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Duty of Director of Military Prosecutions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376276 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review after 90 days

**[[159.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.8 .sectionLabel}** If the trial of a
person who has been retained in custody has not commenced within ninety
days after the day that person was last taken before a military judge,
the Director of Military Prosecutions shall cause the person to be
brought before a military judge to determine whether the continued
retention of the person in custody is justified under section 159.2.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

#### [Review by Court Martial Appeal Court]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376281 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of direction

- **[[159.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At any time before the commencement
  of a person's trial, a judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court may, on
  application, review any direction of a military judge under this
  Division to release the person from custody with or without an
  undertaking or to retain the person in custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of provisions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of this Division apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, to any review under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

#### [Direction Cancelled]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376289 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[159.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.91 .sectionLabel}** A direction to
retain a person in custody or impose conditions on their release is
cancelled in the circumstances prescribed in regulations made by the
Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 33
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Commencement of Proceedings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-376294 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376295 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[commanding
officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[commanding officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a person
charged with having committed a service offence or a service infraction,
means the commanding officer of the person and includes an officer who
is empowered by regulations made by the Governor in Council to act as
the commanding officer of the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 160
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 24](#1149797-1353444){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149797-1353444.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-160-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 160"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Laying of Charge]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376300 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Laying of charge

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings against a person who is
  alleged to have committed a service offence or a service infraction
  are commenced by the laying of a charge in accordance with regulations
  made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to act expeditiously

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person is retained in custody or released from
  custody with conditions, a charge must be laid as expeditiously as the
  circumstances permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 161
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 34
- [2019, c. 15, s. 24](#1149797-1353443){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149797-1353443.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 161"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral of charge --- service offence

- **[[161.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After a person is charged with
  having committed a service offence, the charge must be referred, in
  accordance with regulations made by the Governor in Council, to the
  Director of Military Prosecutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral of charge --- service
  infraction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After a person is charged with having committed a
  service infraction, the charge must be referred, in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, to an officer who is a
  commanding officer in respect of the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 24](#1149797-1353450){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149797-1353450.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 161.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Duty to Act Expeditiously]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376312 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to act expeditiously

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** Charges laid under the
Code of Service Discipline shall be dealt with as expeditiously as the
circumstances permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 162
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2008, c. 29, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-162-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 162"){.PITLink}
:::

c. 15, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

Version](section-162.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 162.1"){.PITLink}

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Summary Hearings]{.HTitleText2} {#h-376326 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376327 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[162.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.3 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[commanding officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[commanding officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a person
    charged with having committed a service infraction, means an officer
    who is a [[commanding officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
    section 160. ([commandant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[delegated officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[delegated officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an officer to whom a
    commanding officer has, under section 162.94, delegated the power to
    conduct a summary hearing. ([officier délégué]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[scale of sanctions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[scale of sanctions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the scale of
    sanctions set out in subsection 162.7. ([échelle des
    sanctions]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[superior commander]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[superior commander]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an officer of or
    above the rank of colonel, or any other officer appointed by the
    Chief of the Defence Staff as a superior commander. ([commandant
    supérieur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353461){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353461.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-162.3-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 162.3"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Service Infractions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353462 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary hearing

**[[162.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.4 .sectionLabel}** Service infractions
may be dealt with only by summary hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353466){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353466.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not offence

**[[162.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.5 .sectionLabel}** A service
infraction is not an offence under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353470){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353470.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prior trial --- offence

- **[[162.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person has been tried in
  respect of an offence, the person may not be charged with having
  committed a service infraction arising from the same facts, regardless
  of whether the person was found guilty or not guilty of the offence by
  a court martial, by a civil court or by a court of a foreign state.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prior summary hearing --- service
  infraction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a summary hearing has been conducted in respect of
  a service infraction that a person is alleged to have committed, the
  person may be charged, dealt with and tried in respect of an offence
  arising from the same facts, regardless of whether or not the person
  was found to have committed the service infraction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer or statement --- restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No answer given or statement made by a person at
  their summary hearing may be used or be receivable against them in any
  disciplinary, criminal or civil proceeding, other than at a hearing or
  proceeding in respect of an allegation that they gave the answer or
  made the statement knowing it to be false.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353479){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353479.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scale of sanctions

**[[162.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.7 .sectionLabel}** The following
sanctions may be imposed in respect of a service infraction, and each is
a sanction less than every sanction preceding it:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} reduction in rank;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} severe reprimand;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} reprimand;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} deprivation of pay, and of any allowance prescribed
  in regulations made by the Governor in Council, for not more than 18
  days; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} minor sanctions prescribed in regulations made by the
  Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353488){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353488.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction in rank

- **[[162.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The sanction of reduction in rank
  applies to officers above the rank of second lieutenant and to
  non-commissioned members above the rank of private.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The sanction of reduction in rank is not to involve

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reduction to a rank lower than that to which the
    person who has committed a service infraction can be reduced under
    regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a commissioned officer, reduction to
    a rank lower than commissioned rank.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353497){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353497.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives of sanctions

**[[162.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.9 .sectionLabel}** The imposition of
sanctions is intended to achieve one or more of the following
objectives:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to promote a habit of obedience to lawful commands
  and orders;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to maintain public trust in the Canadian Forces as a
  disciplined armed force;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to denounce indisciplined conduct;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to deter persons from committing service infractions;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} to assist in rehabilitating persons who have
  committed service infractions;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} to promote a sense of responsibility in persons who
  have committed service infractions.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353507){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353507.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fundamental principle

**[[162.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.91 .sectionLabel}** Sanctions must be
proportionate to the gravity of the service infraction and the degree of
responsibility of the person who committed it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353511){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353511.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other principles

**[[162.92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.92 .sectionLabel}** Sanctions must be
imposed in accordance with the following other principles:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a sanction should be increased or reduced to account
  for any relevant aggravating or mitigating circumstances relating to
  the commission of the service infraction or the person who committed
  it, and aggravating circumstances include evidence establishing that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person, in committing the service infraction,
    abused their rank or other position of trust or authority,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the service infraction was motivated by bias,
    prejudice or hate based on race, national or ethnic origin,
    language, colour, religion, sex, age, mental or physical disability,
    sexual orientation, or gender identity or expression, or on any
    other similar factor, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the service infraction resulted
    in harm to the conduct of a military operation or any military
    training;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a sanction should be similar to sanctions imposed on
  persons for similar service infractions committed in similar
  circumstances; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a sanction should be the least severe sanction
  required to maintain the discipline, efficiency and morale of the
  Canadian Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353521){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353521.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of indirect consequences

**[[162.93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.93 .sectionLabel}** In determining
the sanction to be imposed against a person, a superior commander,
commanding officer or delegated officer may take into account any
indirect consequences of the finding that the person committed a service
infraction, or of the sanction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353525){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353525.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Summary Hearings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353526 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

**[[162.94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.94 .sectionLabel}** A commanding
officer may, subject to regulations made by the Governor in Council and
to the extent that the commanding officer considers appropriate,
delegate his or her power to conduct a summary hearing to any officer
under his or her command.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353541){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353541.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commanding officer's obligation

**[[162.95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162.95 .sectionLabel}** A commanding
officer to whom a charge alleging the commission of a service infraction
is referred under subsection 161.1(2) shall, taking into account the
conditions set out in section 163,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct a summary hearing in respect of the charge;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} decide to not proceed with the charge if, in his or
  her opinion, it should not be proceeded with; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} refer the charge, subject to and in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, to another commanding
  officer, a superior commander or a delegated officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353548){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353548.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  superior commander, commanding officer or delegated officer may
  conduct a hearing in respect of a charge alleging the commission of a
  service infraction if all of the following conditions are satisfied:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person charged is an officer who is at least
    one rank below the rank of the superior commander, commanding
    officer or delegated officer, or is a non-commissioned member;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} having regard to the gravity of the facts that gave
    rise to the charge, the powers of the superior commander, commanding
    officer or delegated officer to impose a sanction are adequate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there are no reasonable grounds to believe that the
    person charged is unable on account of mental disorder to understand
    the nature, object or possible consequences of the proceedings; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} having regard to the interests of discipline,
    efficiency and morale of the Canadian Forces, it would be
    appropriate to conduct the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition on conducting hearing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A superior commander, commanding officer or delegated
  officer may not conduct a hearing if he or she

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} carried out or directly supervised the
    investigation of the service infraction;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issued a warrant under section 273.3 in relation to
    anything referred to in any of paragraphs 273.3(a) to (c) that
    relates to the service infraction; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} laid the charge or caused it to be laid.

  However, he or she may conduct such a hearing if, having regard to all
  the circumstances, it is not practicable for any other superior
  commander, commanding officer or delegated officer to conduct the
  hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 163
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), ss. 53, 60
- 1991, c. 43, s. 14
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2008, c. 29, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 35
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353563){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353563.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 163"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions imposed by superior commander

- **[[163.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A superior commander who finds on a
  balance of probabilities, at a summary hearing, that a person has
  committed one or more service infractions, may impose one or more of
  the sanctions referred to in section 162.7.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions imposed by commanding officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A commanding officer who finds on a balance of
  probabilities, at a summary hearing, that a person has committed one
  or more service infractions, may impose one or more of the sanctions
  referred to in paragraphs 162.7(c) to (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions imposed by delegated officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A delegated officer who finds on a balance of
  probabilities, at a summary hearing, that a person has committed one
  or more service infractions, may impose one or more of the following
  sanctions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a sanction referred to in paragraph 162.7(d) for
    not more than seven days;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} minor sanctions referred to in paragraph 162.7(e).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353573){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353573.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 163.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation after referral

**[[163.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.2 .sectionLabel}** A superior
commander, commanding officer or delegated officer to whom a charge is
referred under paragraph 162.95(c) or under this section shall, taking
into account the conditions set out in section 163,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct a summary hearing in respect of the charge;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} decide not to proceed with the charge if, in his or
  her opinion, it should not be proceeded with; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} refer the charge, subject to and in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council, to a superior commander,
  commanding officer or delegated officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353580){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353580.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent summary hearing proceedings
not precluded

**[[163.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.3 .sectionLabel}** A decision that a
charge should not be proceeded with by summary hearing does not
preclude, subject to section 163.4, proceeding with the charge by
summary hearing at any subsequent time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353584){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353584.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[163.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.4 .sectionLabel}** A summary hearing
in respect of a charge alleging the commission of a service infraction
may not be conducted unless it commences within six months after the day
on which the service infraction is alleged to have been committed.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353588){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353588.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No territorial limitation

**[[163.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.5 .sectionLabel}** Every person
alleged to have committed a service infraction may be charged under the
Code of Service Discipline, regardless of whether the alleged service
infraction was committed in Canada or outside Canada, and the summary
hearing in respect of that charge may be conducted in Canada or outside
Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353592){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353592.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Review Authorities]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353593 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief of the Defence Staff and other
military authorities

- **[[163.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The review authorities in respect of
  a finding that a person has committed a service infraction and in
  respect of a sanction imposed by an officer who conducted a summary
  hearing are the Chief of the Defence Staff and any other military
  authorities that are prescribed by the Governor in Council in
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When authorities may act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A review authority in respect of a finding that a
  person has committed a service infraction and in respect of a sanction
  imposed by an officer who conducted a summary hearing may act on its
  own initiative or on application, made in accordance with regulations
  made by the Governor in Council, of the person found to have committed
  the service infraction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353607){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353607.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Quashing of Findings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353608 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to quash

- **[[163.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A review authority may quash a
  finding, by an officer who conducted a summary hearing, that a person
  has committed a service infraction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of complete quashing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a finding that a person has committed a service
  infraction is quashed and no other such finding was made at the
  summary hearing, every sanction imposed as a result of the quashed
  finding is also quashed and a new summary hearing may be held in
  relation to the service infraction as if no previous summary hearing
  had been held.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of partial quashing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case where more than one finding has been made
  that a person has committed a service infraction and a review
  authority quashes one or more but not all of them, if a sanction
  imposed is in excess of any that may be imposed in respect of the
  remaining findings or is, in the opinion of the review authority,
  unduly severe, the review authority shall substitute for that sanction
  any new sanction or sanctions that it considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353626){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353626.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Substitution of Findings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353627 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution of invalid or
unsubstantiated findings

- **[[163.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A review authority may substitute a
  new finding for any finding that a person has committed a service
  infraction that was invalidly made or that cannot be supported by the
  evidence if the new finding could validly have been made on the charge
  to which the new finding relates and it appears to the review
  authority that the officer who conducted the summary hearing was
  satisfied of the facts that establish the service infraction specified
  or involved in the new finding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect on sanction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a new finding is substituted and a sanction
  imposed in respect of the original finding is in excess of a sanction
  that may be imposed in respect of the new finding or is, in the
  opinion of the review authority, unduly severe, the review authority
  shall substitute for that sanction any new sanction or sanctions that
  it considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353634){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353634.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Substitution of Sanctions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353635 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to substitute

- **[[163.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A review authority may substitute
  for any invalid sanction imposed by an officer who conducted a summary
  hearing any new sanction or sanctions that it considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition applicable to new sanction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a new sanction is substituted, the new sanction
  may not be higher in the scale of sanctions than that other sanction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353642){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353642.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Commutation, Mitigation and Remission of Sanctions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353643 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to commute, mitigate or remit
sanctions

- **[[163.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A review authority may commute,
  mitigate or remit any or all of the sanctions imposed by an officer
  who conducted a summary hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in subsection (1).

  [[commute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[commute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to replace a sanction with
      another sanction that is lower in the scale of
      sanctions. ([commuer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[mitigate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[mitigate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to impose a lesser amount
      of the same sanction. ([mitiger]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[remit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[remit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to exempt a person from the
      requirement to undergo the whole or any part of a
      sanction. ([remettre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 25](#1149801-1353653){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149801-1353653.html"
  role="button"}
:::

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019, c.
15, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-164-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 164"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[164.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019,
c. 15, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-164.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 164.1"){.PITLink}
:::

c. 15, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}
