:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Trial by Court Martial]{.HTitleText2} {#h-376431 .Subheading}

#### [Charge must be Preferred]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376432 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Charge must be preferred

- **[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may be tried by court martial only if a charge against the
  person is preferred by the Director of Military Prosecutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[prefer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, a charge is preferred
  when the charge sheet in respect of the charge is signed by the
  Director of Military Prosecutions, or an officer authorized by the
  Director of Military Prosecutions to do so, and filed with the Court
  Martial Administrator.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 165
- 1992, c. 16, s. 2
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 37
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Director of Military Prosecutions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376440 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[165.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may appoint an officer
  who is a barrister or advocate with at least ten years standing at the
  bar of a province to be the Director of Military Prosecutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office and removal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Military Prosecutions holds office
  during good behaviour for a term of not more than four years. The
  Minister may remove the Director of Military Prosecutions from office
  for cause on the recommendation of an inquiry committee established
  under regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of inquiry committee

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An inquiry committee has the same powers, rights
  and privileges --- other than the power to punish for contempt --- as
  are vested in a superior court of criminal jurisdiction with respect
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the attendance, swearing and examination of
    witnesses;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and inspection of documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of its orders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all other matters necessary or proper for the due
    exercise of its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Military Prosecutions is eligible to
  be re-appointed on the expiry of a first or subsequent term of office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 16, s. 2
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and functions

**[[165.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.11 .sectionLabel}** The Director of
Military Prosecutions is responsible for the preferring of all charges
to be tried by court martial and for the conduct of all prosecutions at
courts martial. The Director of Military Prosecutions also acts as
counsel for the Minister in respect of appeals when instructed to do so.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preferring charges

- **[[165.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a charge is referred to the
  Director of Military Prosecutions, the Director of Military
  Prosecutions may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prefer the charge; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prefer any other charge that is founded on facts
    disclosed by evidence in addition to or in substitution for the
    charge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Irregularity, informality or defect

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The validity of a charge preferred by the Director
  of Military Prosecutions is not affected by any irregularity,
  informality or defect in the charge referred to the Director.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawing charges

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Military Prosecutions may withdraw a
  charge that has been preferred, but if a trial by court martial has
  commenced, the Director of Military Prosecutions may do so only with
  leave of the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of withdrawing charge

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Withdrawing a charge does not preclude it from being
  proceeded with at any subsequent time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of not preferring charge

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A decision not to prefer a charge does not preclude
  the charge from being preferred at any subsequent time.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 39
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.12-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for not proceeding

**[[165.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.13 .sectionLabel}** If the Director
of Military Prosecutions decides that a charge should not be proceeded
with by a trial by court martial, he or she shall communicate the
decision and the reasons for it, in writing, to the officer or
non-commissioned member who referred the charge to him or her, and to
the commanding officer of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- [2019, c. 15, s. 26](#1149805-1355164){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149805-1355164.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.13-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.13"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[165.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.14 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
2008, c. 29, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.14-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barristers and advocates to assist

**[[165.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.15 .sectionLabel}** The Director of
Military Prosecutions may be assisted and represented, to the extent
determined by the Director of Military Prosecutions, by officers who are
barristers or advocates with standing at the bar of a province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Director of Military Prosecutions

**[[165.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.16 .sectionLabel}** The powers of the
Director of Military Prosecutions may be exercised, and the duties and
functions of the Director of Military Prosecutions may be performed, by
any officer who is a barrister or advocate with standing at the bar of a
province and who is authorized by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relationship to Judge Advocate General

- **[[165.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Military
  Prosecutions acts under the general supervision of the Judge Advocate
  General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General instructions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General may issue general
  instructions or guidelines in writing in respect of prosecutions. The
  Director of Military Prosecutions shall ensure that they are available
  to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific instructions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General may issue instructions or
  guidelines in writing in respect of a particular prosecution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Availability to public

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Military Prosecutions shall ensure
  that instructions and guidelines issued under subsection (3) are
  available to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply where the Director of
  Military Prosecutions considers that it would not be in the best
  interests of the administration of military justice for any
  instruction or guideline, or any part of it, to be available to the
  public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies to Minister

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General shall provide the Minister
  with a copy of every instruction and guideline made under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

#### [Court Martial Administrator]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376503 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

**[[165.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.18 .sectionLabel}** There shall be a
person appointed to be the Court Martial Administrator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties

- **[[165.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator
  performs the duties specified in sections 165.191 to 165.193 and, if
  he or she convenes a General Court Martial, shall appoint its members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summoning of accused person

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator shall summon the
  accused person to appear at the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator performs such other
  duties as may be specified by this Act or prescribed by the Governor
  in Council in regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relationship to Chief Military Judge

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator acts under the
  general supervision of the Chief Military Judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2008, c. 29, s. 7
- 2013, c. 24, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.19-20080718.html "Link to previous version of section 165.19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convening General Court Martial

- **[[165.191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator
  shall convene a General Court Martial if any charge preferred against
  an accused person on a charge sheet is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act, other than under section
    130 or 132, that is punishable by imprisonment for life;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is
    punishable by imprisonment for life; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is
    referred to in section 469 of the Criminal Code.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent to be tried by Standing Court
  Martial

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An accused person who is charged with an offence
  referred to in subsection (1) may, with the written consent of the
  accused person and that of the Director of Military Prosecutions, be
  tried by Standing Court Martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The consent given under subsection (2) may not be
  withdrawn unless both the accused and the Director of Military
  Prosecutions agree in writing to the withdrawal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 29, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convening Standing Court Martial

**[[165.192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.192 .sectionLabel}** The Court
Martial Administrator shall convene a Standing Court Martial if every
charge preferred against an accused person on a charge sheet is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence under this Act, other than under section
  130, that is punishable by imprisonment for less than two years or by
  a punishment that is lower in the scale of punishments; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence that is punishable under section 130 and
  is punishable on summary conviction under any Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 29, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Choice of accused

- **[[165.193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.193
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An accused person may choose to be
  tried by General Court Martial or Standing Court Martial if a charge
  is preferred and sections 165.191 and 165.192 do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator shall cause the
  accused person to be notified in writing that he or she may make a
  choice under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to make choice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person fails to notify the Court
  Martial Administrator in writing of his or her choice within 14 days
  after the day on which the accused person is notified under subsection
  (2), the accused person is deemed to have chosen to be tried by
  General Court Martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New choice --- as of right

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The accused person may, not later than 30 days before
  the date set for the commencement of the trial, make a new choice once
  as of right, in which case he or she shall notify the Court Martial
  Administrator in writing of the new choice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New choice --- with consent

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The accused person may also, with the written consent
  of the Director of Military Prosecutions, make a new choice at any
  time, in which case he or she shall notify the Court Martial
  Administrator in writing of the new choice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Two or more accused

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If charges are preferred jointly and all of the
  accused persons do not choose --- or are not deemed to have chosen ---
  to be tried by the same type of court martial, they must be tried by a
  General Court Martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convening of court martial

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator shall convene a
  General Court Martial or Standing Court Martial in accordance with
  this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 29, s. 8
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Court Martial Administrator

**[[165.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.2 .sectionLabel}** The duties and
functions of the Court Martial Administrator may be performed by any
person authorized by the Court Martial Administrator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

#### [Military Judges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376558 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[165.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may appoint
  any officer who is a barrister or advocate of at least 10 years'
  standing at the bar of a province and who has been an officer for at
  least 10 years to be a military judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every military judge shall, before commencing the
  duties of office, take the following oath of office:

  I ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot} solemnly and
  sincerely promise and swear (or affirm) that I will impartially,
  honestly and faithfully, and to the best of my skill and knowledge,
  execute the powers and trusts reposed in me as a military judge. (*And
  in the case of an oath:* So help me God.)

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal for cause

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A military judge holds office during good behaviour
  and may be removed by the Governor in Council for cause on the
  recommendation of the Military Judges Inquiry Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ceasing to hold office

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A military judge ceases to hold office on being
  released at his or her request from the Canadian Forces or on
  attaining the age of 60 years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resignation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A military judge may resign from office by giving
  notice in writing to the Minister. The resignation takes effect on the
  day on which the Minister receives the notice or on a later day that
  may be specified in the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2011, c. 22, s. 2
- 2013, c. 24, s. 41
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.21-20111129.html "Link to previous version of section 165.21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Reserve Force Military Judges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376573 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panel established

- **[[165.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is established a Reserve Force
  Military Judges Panel to which the Governor in Council may name any
  officer of the reserve force who has been an officer for at least 10
  years and who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a barrister or advocate of at least 10 years'
    standing at the bar of a province;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been a military judge;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has presided at a Standing Court Martial or a
    Special General Court Martial; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} has been a judge advocate at a court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reserve force military judge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer named to the panel is referred to in this
  Act as a "reserve force military judge".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every reserve force military judge shall, before
  commencing the duties of office, take the following oath of office:

  I ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot} solemnly and
  sincerely promise and swear (or affirm) that I will impartially,
  honestly and faithfully, and to the best of my skill and knowledge,
  execute the powers and trusts reposed in me as a military judge. (*And
  in the case of an oath:* So help me God.)

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 41
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.22-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal from panel

- **[[165.221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.221
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may for
  cause remove the name of a reserve force military judge from the
  Reserve Force Military Judges Panel on the recommendation of the
  Military Judges Inquiry Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic removal from panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The name of a reserve force military judge shall be
  removed from the panel on the judge's release, at his or her request,
  from the Canadian Forces or on the judge attaining the age of 60
  years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary removal from panel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A reserve force military judge may request that their
  name be removed from the panel by giving notice in writing to the
  Minister. The removal takes effect on the day on which the Minister
  receives the notice or on a later day that may be specified in the
  notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 41
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Military Judge

- **[[165.222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.222
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Military Judge may select
  any reserve force military judge to perform any duties referred to in
  section 165.23 that may be specified by the Chief Military Judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Military Judge may request a reserve force
  military judge to undergo any training that may be specified by the
  Chief Military Judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 41
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on activities

**[[165.223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.223 .sectionLabel}** A reserve force
military judge shall not engage in any business or professional activity
that is incompatible with the duties that they may be required to
perform under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 41
:::

#### [Duties and Immunity of Military Judges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376608 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial duties and functions

- **[[165.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Military judges shall preside at
  courts martial and shall perform other judicial duties under this Act
  that are required to be performed by military judges.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other duties and functions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to their judicial duties, military judges
  shall perform any other duties that the Chief Military Judge may
  direct, but those other duties may not be incompatible with their
  judicial duties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boards of inquiry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Military judges may, with the concurrence of the
  Chief Military Judge, be appointed as a board of inquiry.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[165.231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.231 .sectionLabel}** A military
judge has the same immunity from liability as a judge of a superior
court of criminal jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 42
:::

#### [Chief Military Judge]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376622 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Military Judge

- **[[165.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may
  designate a military judge, other than a reserve force military judge,
  to be the Chief Military Judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Military Judge holds a rank that is not
  less than colonel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 43
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.24-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and functions

**[[165.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.25 .sectionLabel}** The Chief
Military Judge assigns military judges to preside at courts martial and
to perform other judicial duties under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

**[[165.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.26 .sectionLabel}** The Chief
Military Judge may authorize any military judge, other than a reserve
force military judge, to exercise and perform any of the powers, duties
and functions of the Chief Military Judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165.26-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165.26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

**[[165.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.27 .sectionLabel}** The Chief
Military Judge may delegate any of the Chief Military Judge's duties and
functions to a military judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deputy Chief Military Judge

**[[165.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.28 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may designate a military judge, other than a reserve force
military judge, to be the Deputy Chief Military Judge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power, duties and functions

**[[165.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.29 .sectionLabel}** In the event that
the Chief Military Judge is absent or unable to act or the office of
Chief Military Judge is vacant, the Deputy Chief Military Judge shall
exercise and perform the powers, duties and functions of the Chief
Military Judge that are not otherwise authorized to be exercised or
performed by a military judge under section 165.26.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of practice and procedure

**[[165.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.3 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Military
Judge may, with the Governor in Council's approval and after consulting
with a rules committee established under regulations made by the
Governor in Council, make rules governing the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} pre-trial conferences and other preliminary
  proceedings;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the making of applications under section 158.7;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the bringing of persons before a military judge under
  section 159;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the scheduling of trials by court martial;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the minutes of proceedings of courts martial and
  other proceedings;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} documents, exhibits or other things connected with
  any proceeding, including public access to them; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other aspects of practice and procedure that are
  prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

#### [Military Judges Inquiry Committee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376661 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of Committee

- **[[165.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is established a Military
  Judges Inquiry Committee to which the Chief Justice of the Court
  Martial Appeal Court shall appoint three judges of the Court Martial
  Appeal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice shall appoint one of the judges to
  act as Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of inquiry committee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The inquiry committee has the same powers, rights and
  privileges --- including the power to punish for contempt --- as are
  vested in a superior court of criminal jurisdiction with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the attendance, swearing and examination of
    witnesses;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and inspection of documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of its orders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all other matters necessary or proper for the due
    exercise of its jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry required

- **[[165.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Military Judges Inquiry
  Committee shall, on receipt of a request in writing made by the
  Minister, commence an inquiry as to whether a military judge should be
  removed from office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other inquiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The inquiry committee may, on receipt of any
  complaint or allegation in writing made in respect of a military
  judge, commence an inquiry as to whether the military judge should be
  removed from office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination and recommendation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the inquiry committee may
  designate a judge appointed to the committee to examine a complaint or
  allegation referred to in subsection (2) and to recommend whether an
  inquiry should be commenced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to military judge

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The military judge in respect of whom an inquiry is
  held shall be given reasonable notice of the inquiry's subject matter
  and of its time and place and shall be given an opportunity, in person
  or by counsel, to be heard at the inquiry, to cross-examine witnesses
  and to adduce evidence on his or her own behalf.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry held in public or private

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The inquiry committee may hold an inquiry either in
  public or in private unless the Minister, having regard to the
  interests of the persons participating in the inquiry and the
  interests of the public, directs that the inquiry be held in public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counsel

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the inquiry committee may engage
  on a temporary basis the services of counsel to assist the committee
  and may, subject to any applicable Treasury Board directives,
  establish the terms and conditions of the counsel's engagement and fix
  their remuneration and expenses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendation to the Governor in
  Council

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The inquiry committee may recommend to the Governor
  in Council that the military judge be removed if, in its opinion,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the military judge has become incapacitated or
    disabled from the due execution of his or her judicial duties by
    reason of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} infirmity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} having been guilty of misconduct,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} having failed in the due execution of his or
      her judicial duties, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} having been placed, by his or her conduct or
      otherwise, in a position incompatible with the due execution of
      his or her judicial duties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the military judge does not satisfy the physical
    and medical fitness standards applicable to officers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The inquiry committee shall provide to the Minister a
  record of each inquiry and a report of its conclusions. If the inquiry
  was held in public, the inquiry committee shall make its report
  available to the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

#### [Military Judges Compensation Committee]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376700 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of Committee

- **[[165.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is established a Military
  Judges Compensation Committee consisting of three part-time members to
  be appointed by the Governor in Council as follows:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one person nominated by the military judges;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one person nominated by the Minister; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} one person, who shall act as chairperson, nominated
    by the members who are nominated under paragraphs (a) and (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure and removal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each member holds office during good behaviour for a
  term of four years, and may be removed for cause at any time by the
  Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A member is eligible to be reappointed for one
  further term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of a
  member, the Governor in Council may appoint, as a substitute temporary
  member, a person nominated in accordance with subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the office of a member becomes vacant during the
  member's term, the Governor in Council shall appoint a person
  nominated in accordance with subsection (1) to hold office for the
  remainder of the term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} All three members of the compensation committee
  together constitute a quorum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The members of the compensation committee shall be
  paid the remuneration fixed by the Governor in Council and, subject to
  any applicable Treasury Board directives, the reasonable travel and
  living expenses incurred by them in the course of their duties while
  absent from their ordinary place of residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandate

- **[[165.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Military Judges Compensation
  Committee shall inquire into the adequacy of the remuneration of
  military judges.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In conducting its inquiry, the compensation committee
  shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the prevailing economic conditions in Canada,
    including the cost of living, and the overall economic and current
    financial position of the federal government;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the role of financial security of the judiciary in
    ensuring judicial independence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the need to attract outstanding candidates to the
    judiciary; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other objective criteria that the committee
    considers relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quadrennial inquiry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The compensation committee shall commence an inquiry
  on September 1, 2015, and on September 1 of every fourth year after
  2015, and shall submit a report containing its recommendations to the
  Minister within nine months after the day on which the inquiry
  commenced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Postponement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The compensation committee may, with the consent of
  the Minister and the military judges, postpone the commencement of a
  quadrennial inquiry.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other inquiries

- **[[165.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may at any time refer
  to the Military Judges Compensation Committee for its inquiry the
  matter, or any aspect of the matter, mentioned in subsection
  165.34(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The compensation committee shall submit to the
  Minister a report containing its recommendations within a period fixed
  by the Minister after consultation with the compensation committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuance of duties

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who ceases to hold office as a member for
  any reason other than their removal may carry out and complete their
  duties in respect of a matter that was referred to the compensation
  committee under subsection (1) before the person ceased to hold
  office. While completing those duties, the person is deemed to be a
  member of the compensation committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

**[[165.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.36 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may, on the request of the Military Judges Compensation
Committee, extend the time for the submission of a report.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's duties

- **[[165.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after receiving a
  report, the Minister shall notify the public and facilitate public
  access to the report in any manner that the Minister considers
  appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Response

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall respond to a report within six
  months after receiving it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 45
:::

#### [General Courts Martial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376756 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** A General Court Martial
may try any person who is liable to be charged, dealt with and tried on
a charge of having committed a service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 166
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment limitation

**[[166.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166.1 .sectionLabel}** A General Court
Martial that tries a person other than an officer or a non-commissioned
member may only pass a sentence that includes a punishment of
imprisonment or a fine.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 29, s. 9
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  General Court Martial is composed of a military judge and a panel of
  five members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank of senior member

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The senior member of the panel must be an officer of
  or above the rank of lieutenant-colonel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2013, c. 24, s. 47\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ranks for trial of brigadier-general or
  above

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is of or above the rank of
  brigadier-general, the senior member of the panel must be an officer
  of or above the rank of the accused person and the other members of
  the panel must be of or above the rank of colonel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank for trial of colonel

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is of the rank of colonel, the
  senior member of the panel must be an officer of or above the rank of
  the accused person and the other members of the panel must be of or
  above the rank of lieutenant-colonel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank for trial of lieutenant-colonel or
  lower-ranked officer

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is an officer of or below the
  rank of lieutenant-colonel, the members of the panel other than the
  senior member must be of or above the rank of the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank for trial of non-commissioned
  member

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is a non-commissioned member,
  the panel is composed of the senior member, one other officer and
  three non-commissioned members who are of or above both the rank of
  the accused person and the rank of sergeant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 167
- 1992, c. 16, s. 3
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 47
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-167-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 167"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ineligibility to serve

**[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** None of the following
persons may sit as a member of the panel of a General Court Martial:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member who is a lawyer
  or notary;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a witness for the prosecution or the accused person;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the commanding officer of the accused person;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a member of the military police;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an officer below the rank of captain;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} any person who, before the court martial,
  participated in any investigation respecting the matters on which a
  charge against the accused person is founded; or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} an officer or non-commissioned member of any armed
  force who is attached, seconded or on loan to the Canadian Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 168
- 1992, c. 16, s. 4
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 48
:::

#### [Standing Courts Martial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376796 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** A Standing Court
Martial may try any person who is liable to be charged, dealt with and
tried on a charge of having committed a service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 173
- 1992, c. 16, s. 6
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2008, c. 29, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-173-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 173"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** Every military judge is
authorized to preside at a Standing Court Martial, and a military judge
who does so constitutes the Standing Court Martial.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 174
- 1992, c. 16, s. 6
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment limitation

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** A Standing Court
Martial that tries a person other than an officer or a non-commissioned
member may only pass a sentence that includes a punishment of
imprisonment or a fine.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 175
- 1991, c. 43, s. 16
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2008, c. 29, s. 12
:::

::: PITLink

#### [Powers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376812 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Courts martial

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court martial has the same powers, rights and privileges --- including
  the power to punish for contempt --- as are vested in a superior court
  of criminal jurisdiction with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the attendance, swearing and examination of
    witnesses;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and inspection of documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of its orders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all other matters necessary or proper for the due
    exercise of its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military judges

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to a military judge performing
  a judicial duty under this Act other than presiding at a court
  martial.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 179
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 56
- 1998, c. 35, s. 42
- 2013, c. 24, s. 49(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Admission to Courts Martial and Certain Proceedings Before Military Judges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376824 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings public

- **[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless this Act provides otherwise,
  court martial proceedings, and proceedings before military judges
  under any of sections 147.6, 148, 158.7, 159, 187, 215.2 and 248.81,
  must be public and, to the extent that accommodation permits, the
  public must be admitted to the proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A military judge or, if a court martial has been
  convened, the military judge assigned to preside at the court martial
  may, on application of the prosecutor or a witness or on the military
  judge's own motion, order that the public be excluded during the whole
  or any part of the proceedings or order that the witness testify
  behind a screen or other device that would allow the witness not to be
  seen by members of the public, if the military judge considers that it
  is necessary in the interests of public safety, public morals, the
  maintenance of order or the proper administration of military justice,
  or to prevent injury to international relations, national defence or
  national security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether the order is in the interest
  of the proper administration of military justice, the military judge
  shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims and witnesses in
    the military justice process;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safeguarding of the interests of witnesses
    under the age of 18 years in all proceedings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the ability of any witness, if the order were not
    made, to give a full and candid account of the acts complained of;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether any witness needs the order for their
    security or to protect them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the protection of military justice system
    participants who are involved in the proceedings;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether effective alternatives to the making of the
    order are available in the circumstances;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the salutary and deleterious effects of the order;
    and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  an order is, or is not, made under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons to be stated

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a person is charged with an offence punishable
  under section 130 that is an offence under section 151, 152, 153,
  153.1 or 155, subsection 160(2) or (3) or section 163.1, 170, 171,
  171.1, 172, 172.1, 172.2, 173, 271, 272, 273, 279.01, 279.011, 279.02,
  279.03, 286.1, 286.2 or 286.3 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  and the prosecutor or the person applies for an order under subsection
  (2), the military judge shall, if no such order is made, state, by
  reference to the circumstances of the case, the reasons for not making
  an order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witnesses

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Witnesses are not to be admitted to the proceedings
  except when under examination or by leave of the military judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clearing court

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of any deliberation, the military
  judge may cause the place where the proceedings are being held to be
  cleared.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 180
- 1992, c. 16, s. 8
- 1998, c. 35, s. 43
- 2001, c. 41, s. 101
- 2013, c. 24, s. 50
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355189){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355189.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150042-1355798){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150042-1355798.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-180-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 180"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Production of certain records]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1355190 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[record]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[180.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.01 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes
of sections 180.02 to 180.08, [[record]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
form of record that contains personal information for which there is a
reasonable expectation of privacy and includes medical, psychiatric,
therapeutic, counselling, education, employment, child welfare, adoption
and social services records, personal journals and diaries, and records
containing personal information the production or disclosure of which is
protected by any other Act of Parliament or of a provincial legislature,
but does not include records made by persons responsible for the
investigation or prosecution of the service offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355194){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355194.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of record to accused

- **[[180.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except in accordance with sections
  180.03 to 180.08, no record relating to a complainant or a witness
  shall be produced to an accused person in any proceedings in respect
  of any of the following offences or in any proceedings in respect of
  two or more offences at least one of which is any of the following
  offences:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is an
    offence under section 151, 152, 153, 153.1, 155, 160, 170, 171, 172,
    173, 210, 211, 213, 271, 272, 273, 279.01, 279.011, 279.02, 279.03,
    286.1, 286.2 or 286.3 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any offence under the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), as it read at any time before the day on
    which this paragraph comes into force, if the conduct alleged
    involved a violation of the complainant's sexual integrity and would
    be an offence referred to in paragraph (a) if it had occurred on or
    after that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of provisions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 180.01, this section and sections 180.03 to
  180.08 and 303 apply in respect of a record relating to the
  complainant or a witness that is in the possession or control of any
  person, including the prosecutor in the proceedings, unless, in the
  case of a record in the possession or control of the prosecutor, the
  complainant or witness, as the case may be, has expressly waived the
  application of those sections.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of prosecutor to give notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a record in respect of which this
  section applies that is in the possession or control of the
  prosecutor, the prosecutor shall notify the accused person that the
  record is in the prosecutor's possession or control, but, in doing so,
  the prosecutor shall not disclose the record's contents.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355205){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355205.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for production

- **[[180.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An accused person who seeks the
  production of a record may make an application for its production to a
  military judge or, if the court martial has been convened, to the
  military judge assigned to preside at the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and content of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be made in writing and set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} particulars identifying the record that the accused
    person seeks to have produced and the name of the person who has
    possession or control of the record; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the grounds on which the accused person relies to
    establish that the record is likely relevant to an issue at trial or
    to the competence of a witness to testify.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insufficient grounds

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any one or more of the following assertions by the
  accused person are not sufficient on their own to establish that the
  record is likely relevant to an issue at trial or to the competence of
  a witness to testify:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the record exists;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the record relates to medical or psychiatric
    treatment, therapy or counselling that the complainant or witness
    has received or is receiving;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the record relates to the incident that is the
    subject matter of the proceedings;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the record may disclose a prior inconsistent
    statement of the complainant or witness;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the record may relate to the credibility of the
    complainant or witness;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the record may relate to the reliability of the
    testimony of the complainant or witness merely because the
    complainant or witness has received or is receiving psychiatric
    treatment, therapy or counselling;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the record may reveal allegations of sexual abuse
    of the complainant by a person other than the accused person;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the record relates to the sexual activity of the
    complainant with any person, including the accused person;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the record relates to the presence or absence of a
    recent complaint;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the record relates to the complainant's sexual
    reputation;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the record was made close in time to the complaint
    or to the activity that forms the subject matter of the charge
    against the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The accused person shall serve the application on the
  prosecutor, on the person who has possession or control of the record,
  on the complainant or witness, as the case may be, and on any other
  person to whom, to the knowledge of the accused person, the record
  relates, at least 14 days before the hearing referred to in subsection
  180.04(1) begins or any shorter interval that the military judge may
  allow in the interests of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service on other persons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may, at any time, order that the
  application be served on any person to whom he or she considers the
  record may relate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355231){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355231.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing in private

- **[[180.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall hold a
  hearing in private to determine whether to order the person who has
  possession or control of the record to produce it to the military
  judge for review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons who may appear at hearing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who has possession or control of the
  record, the complainant or witness, as the case may be, and any other
  person to whom the record relates may appear and make submissions at
  the hearing, but they are not compellable as witnesses at the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to counsel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall, as soon as feasible, inform
  any person referred to in subsection (2) who participates in the
  hearing of their right to be represented by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No order for costs may be made against a person
  referred to in subsection (2) in respect of their participation in the
  hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355242){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355242.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to produce record for review

- **[[180.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may order the
  person who has possession or control of the record to produce the
  record or a part of the record to the military judge for review if,
  after the hearing, the military judge is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application was made in accordance with
    subsections 180.03(2) to (5);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the accused person has established that the record
    is likely relevant to an issue at trial or to the competence of a
    witness to testify; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the production of the record is necessary in the
    interests of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make the order, the
  military judge shall consider the salutary and deleterious effects of
  the determination on the accused person's right to make a full answer
  and defence and on the right to privacy, personal security and
  equality of the complainant or witness, as the case may be, and of any
  other person to whom the record relates. In particular, the military
  judge shall take the following factors into account:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which the record is necessary for the
    accused person to make a full answer and defence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the probative value of the record;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature and extent of the reasonable expectation
    of privacy with respect to the record;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether production of the record is based on a
    discriminatory belief or bias;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the potential prejudice to the personal dignity and
    right to privacy of any person to whom the record relates;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    sexual offences;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the obtaining of
    treatment by complainants of sexual offences; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the effect of the determination on the integrity of
    the trial process.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355260){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355260.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of record by military judge

- **[[180.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.06
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge has ordered
  the production of the record or a part of the record for review, the
  military judge shall review it in the absence of the parties to
  determine whether the record or the part of the record should be
  produced to the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing in private

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may hold a hearing in private if
  he or she considers that it will assist in making the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions re hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 180.04(2) to (4) apply in the case of a
  hearing held under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355269){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355269.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to produce

- **[[180.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge is satisfied
  that the record or a part of the record is likely relevant to an issue
  at trial or to the competence of a witness to testify and that its
  production is necessary in the interests of military justice, the
  military judge may order that the record or the part of the record, as
  the case may be, be produced to the accused person, subject to any
  conditions that may be imposed under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make the order, the
  military judge shall consider the salutary and deleterious effects of
  the determination on the accused person's right to make a full answer
  and defence and on the right to privacy, personal security and
  equality of the complainant or witness, as the case may be, and of any
  other person to whom the record relates and, in particular, the
  military judge shall take the factors specified in paragraphs
  180.05(2)(a) to (h) into account.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions on production

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The military judge who orders the production of the
  record or a part of the record to the accused person, may impose
  conditions on the production to protect the interests of military
  justice and, to the greatest extent possible, the privacy, personal
  security and equality interests of the complainant or witness, as the
  case may be, and of any other person to whom the record relates,
  including the following conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the record is to be edited as directed by the
    military judge;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the record, rather than the original, is
    to be produced;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the accused person and counsel for the accused
    person are not to disclose the contents of the record to any other
    person, except with the approval of the military judge;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the record is to be viewed only at a location
    specified by the military judge;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} no copies of the record are to be made or only the
    number of copies specified by the military judge may be made; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} information regarding any person named in the
    record, such as their address, telephone number and place of
    employment, is to be severed from the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to prosecutor

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge orders the production of the
  record or a part of the record to the accused person, the military
  judge shall direct that a copy of the record or the part of the
  record, as the case may be, be provided to the prosecutor, unless the
  military judge determines that it is not in the interests of military
  justice to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record not used in other proceedings

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The record or the part of the record that is produced
  to the accused person under an order made under subsection (1) must
  not be used in any other disciplinary, criminal, civil or
  administrative proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retention of record by court

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge refuses to order the production
  of the record or a part of the record to the accused person, the
  record or the part of the record, as the case may be, must, unless the
  military judge orders otherwise, be kept by the military judge in a
  sealed package until the later of the expiry of the time for any
  appeal and the completion of any appeal in the proceedings against the
  accused person, at which time the record or the part of the record
  must be returned to the person lawfully entitled to possession or
  control of it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355290){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355290.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for decision

**[[180.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180.08 .sectionLabel}** The military
judge shall provide reasons in writing for ordering or not ordering the
production of the record or a part of the record under subsection
180.05(1) or 180.07(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 27](#1149809-1355294){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149809-1355294.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Rules of Evidence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376839 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of evidence

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act, the Governor in
  Council may make rules of evidence to be applicable at trials by court
  martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No rule made under this section is effective until it
  has been published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/),
  and every rule shall be laid before each House of Parliament on any of
  the first 15 days on which that House is sitting after the day on
  which it is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 181
- 2013, c. 24, s. 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-181-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 181"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admission of documents and records

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Documents and records of the classes
  that are prescribed in rules made under section 181 may be admitted,
  as evidence of the facts stated in them, at trials by court martial or
  in any proceedings before civil courts arising out of those trials,
  and the conditions governing the admissibility of the documents and
  records --- or copies of them --- in those classes shall be as
  prescribed in those rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statutory declarations admissible,
  subject to conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A court martial may receive, as evidence of the facts
  stated in them, statutory declarations made in the manner prescribed
  by the [Canada Evidence Act](/eng/acts/C-5), subject to the following
  conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the declaration is one that the prosecutor
    wishes to introduce, a copy shall be served on the accused person at
    least seven days before the trial;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the declaration is one that the accused person
    wishes to introduce, a copy shall be served on the prosecutor at
    least three days before the trial; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at any time before the trial, the party served with
    a copy of the declaration under paragraph (a) or (b) may notify the
    opposite party that the party so served will not consent to the
    declaration being received by the court martial, and in that event
    the declaration shall not be received.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 182
- 2013, c. 24, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-182-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 182"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Witnesses at Courts Martial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376857 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procurement of attendance of witnesses

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  commanding officer of an accused person shall take all necessary
  action to procure the attendance of the witnesses whom the prosecutor
  and the accused person request to be called and whose attendance can,
  having regard to the exigencies of the service, reasonably be
  procured.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) requires the procurement
  of the attendance of any witness, the request for whose attendance is
  considered by the commanding officer to be frivolous or vexatious.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procurement of attendance in
  exceptional cases

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a commanding officer considers to be frivolous
  or vexatious a request by the accused person for the attendance of a
  witness whose attendance, having regard to the exigencies of the
  service, can reasonably be procured, the attendance of that witness
  shall be procured if the accused person pays in advance the fees and
  expenses of the witness in accordance with section 251.2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement of accused for fees and
  expenses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the evidence of a witness whose attendance is
  procured under subsection (2) proves to be relevant and material at
  the trial, the court martial shall order that the accused person be
  reimbursed in the amount of the fees and expenses paid to the witness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of accused preserved

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section limits the right of an
  accused person to procure and produce at the trial, at the expense of
  the accused person, if the exigencies of the service permit, such
  witnesses as that person may desire.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 183
- 1998, c. 35, s. 44
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Support person --- witnesses under 18 or
with disability

- **[[183.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused
  person in respect of a service offence, a military judge or, if the
  court martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to
  preside at the court martial shall, on application of the prosecutor
  in respect of a witness who is under the age of 18 years or who has a
  mental or physical disability, or on application of such a witness,
  order that a support person of the witness's choice be permitted to be
  present and to be close to the witness while the witness testifies,
  unless the military judge is of the opinion that the order would
  interfere with the proper administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other witnesses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused person in respect
  of a service offence, a military judge or, if the court martial has
  been convened, the military judge assigned to preside at the court
  martial may, on application of the prosecutor in respect of a witness,
  or on application of a witness, order that a support person of the
  witness's choice be permitted to be present and to be close to the
  witness while the witness testifies, if the military judge is of the
  opinion that the order would facilitate the giving of a full and
  candid account by the witness of the acts complained of or would
  otherwise be in the interest of the proper administration of military
  justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make an order under
  subsection (2), the military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the witness's age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the witness's mental or physical disabilities, if
    any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the offence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the nature of any relationship between the witness
    and the accused person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the witness needs the order for their
    security or to protect them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims and witnesses in
    the military justice process; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness not to be support person

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall not permit a witness to be a
  support person unless the military judge is of the opinion that doing
  so is necessary for the proper administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No communication while testifying

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may order that the support person
  and the witness not communicate with each other while the witness
  testifies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  an order is, or is not, made under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355316){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355316.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testimony outside courtroom --- witnesses
under 18 or with disability

- **[[183.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused
  person in respect of a service offence, a military judge or, if the
  court martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to
  preside at the court martial shall, on application of the prosecutor
  in respect of a witness who is under the age of 18 years or who is
  able to communicate evidence but may have difficulty doing so by
  reason of a mental or physical disability, or on application of such a
  witness, order that the witness testify outside the courtroom or
  behind a screen or other device that would allow the witness not to
  see the accused person, unless the military judge is of the opinion
  that the order would interfere with the proper administration of
  military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other witnesses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused person in respect
  of a service offence, a military judge or, if the court martial has
  been convened, the military judge assigned to preside at the court
  martial may, on application of the prosecutor in respect of a witness,
  or on application of a witness, order that the witness testify outside
  the courtroom or behind a screen or other device that would allow the
  witness not to see the accused person, if the military judge is of the
  opinion that the order would facilitate the giving of a full and
  candid account by the witness of the acts complained of or would
  otherwise be in the interest of the proper administration of military
  justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make an order under
  subsection (2), the military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the witness's age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the witness's mental or physical disabilities, if
    any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the offence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the nature of any relationship between the witness
    and the accused person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the witness needs the order for their
    security or to protect them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the identity
    of a [[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section
    2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), who has acted, is acting
    or will be acting in an undercover capacity or of a person who has
    acted, is acting or will be acting covertly under the direction of a
    [[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in that section;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the
    witness's identity if they have had, have or will have
    responsibilities relating to national security or intelligence;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims and witnesses in
    the military justice process; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Same procedure for determination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge is of the opinion that it is
  necessary for a witness to testify in order to determine whether an
  order under subsection (2) should be made in respect of the witness,
  the military judge shall order that the witness testify in accordance
  with that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of exclusion

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A witness shall not testify outside the courtroom in
  accordance with an order made under subsection (1) or (2) unless
  arrangements are made for the accused person, the military judge and,
  if a General Court Martial has been convened, its panel to watch the
  testimony of the witness by means of closed-circuit television or
  otherwise and the accused person is permitted to communicate with
  counsel while watching the testimony.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  an order is, or is not, made under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355340){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355340.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused not to cross-examine witnesses
under 18

- **[[183.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused
  person in respect of a service offence, a military judge or, if the
  court martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to
  preside at the court martial shall, on application of the prosecutor
  in respect of a witness who is under the age of 18 years, or on
  application of such a witness, order that the accused person not
  personally cross-examine the witness, unless the military judge is of
  the opinion that the proper administration of military justice
  requires the accused person to personally conduct the
  cross-examination. If such an order is made, the military judge shall
  direct the Director of Defence Counsel Services to provide counsel to
  conduct the cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused not to cross-examine
  complainant --- certain offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused person in respect
  of an offence punishable under section 130 that is an offence under
  section 264, 271, 272 or 273 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), a
  military judge shall, on application of the prosecutor in respect of a
  witness who is a victim, or on application of such a witness, order
  that the accused person not personally cross-examine the witness,
  unless the military judge is of the opinion that the proper
  administration of military justice requires the accused person to
  personally conduct the cross-examination. If such an order is made,
  the military judge shall direct the Director of Defence Counsel
  Services to provide counsel to conduct the cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other witnesses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused person in respect
  of a service offence, a military judge or, if the court martial has
  been convened, the military judge assigned to preside at the court
  martial may, on application of the prosecutor in respect of a witness
  who is not entitled to make an application under subsection (1) or
  (2), or on application of such a witness, order that the accused
  person not personally cross-examine the witness, if the military judge
  is of the opinion that the order would facilitate the giving of a full
  and candid account by the witness of the acts complained of or would
  otherwise be in the interest of the proper administration of military
  justice. If such an order is made, the military judge shall direct the
  Director of Defence Counsel Services to provide counsel to conduct the
  cross-examination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make an order under
  subsection (3), the military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the witness's age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the witness's mental or physical disabilities, if
    any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the offence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the nature of any relationship between the witness
    and the accused person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the witness needs the order for their
    security or to protect them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims and witnesses in
    the military justice process; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  counsel is, or is not, provided under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355360){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355360.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-disclosure of witness's identity

- **[[183.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused
  person in respect of a service offence, a military judge or, if the
  court martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to
  preside at the court martial may, on application of the prosecutor in
  respect of a witness, or on application of a witness, make an order
  directing that any information that could identify the witness not be
  disclosed in the course of the proceedings, if the military judge is
  of the opinion that the order is in the interest of the proper
  administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing may be held

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may hold a hearing to determine
  whether the order should be made, and the hearing may be in private.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make the order, the
  military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the right to a fair and public hearing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the witness needs the order for their
    security or to protect them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the security
    of anyone known to the witness;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the identity
    of a [[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section
    2 of the Criminal Code, who has acted, is acting or will be acting
    in an undercover capacity, or of a person who has acted, is acting
    or will be acting covertly under the direction of a [[peace
    officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in that section;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the
    witness's identity if they have had, have or will have
    responsibilities relating to national security or intelligence;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims and witnesses in
    the military justice process;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the importance of the witness's testimony to the
    case;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether effective alternatives to the making of the
    order are available in the circumstances;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the salutary and deleterious effects of the order;
    and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  an order is, or is not, made under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355382){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355382.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order restricting publication --- sexual
offences

- **[[183.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a
  military judge or, if the court martial has been convened, the
  military judge assigned to preside at the court martial may make an
  order directing that any information that could identify a victim or a
  witness not be published in any document, or broadcast or transmitted
  in any way, if the proceedings are in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any of the following offences:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is
      an offence under section 151, 152, 153, 153.1, 155, 160, 162,
      163.1, 170, 171, 171.1, 172, 172.1, 172.2, 173, 210, 211, 213,
      271, 272, 273, 279.01, 279.011, 279.02, 279.03, 280, 281, 286.1,
      286.2, 286.3, 346 or 347 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any offence under the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), as it read at any time before the day on
      which this subparagraph comes into force, if the conduct alleged
      would be an offence referred to in subparagraph (i) if it had
      occurred on or after that day; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two or more offences being dealt with in the same
    proceeding, at least one of which is an offence referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory order on application
