  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings in respect of any offence referred to
  in subsection (1), the military judge shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as feasible, inform the victim and any
    witness under the age of 18 years of their right to make an
    application for the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on application of the victim, the prosecutor or any
    such witness, make the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Victim under 18 --- other offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), in proceedings in respect
  of a service offence other than an offence referred to in subsection
  (1), if the victim is under the age of 18 years, the military judge
  or, if the court martial has been convened, the military judge
  assigned to preside at the court martial may make an order directing
  that any information that could identify the victim not be published
  in any document or broadcast or transmitted in any way.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory order on application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings in respect of a service offence other
  than an offence referred to in subsection (1), if the victim is under
  the age of 18 years, the military judge or, if the court martial has
  been convened, the military judge assigned to preside at the court
  martial shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as feasible, inform the victim of their
    right to make an application for the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on application of the victim or the prosecutor,
    make the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Child pornography

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings in respect of an offence punishable
  under section 130 that is an offence under section 163.1 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the military judge or, if the court
  martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to preside at
  the court martial shall make an order directing that any information
  that could identify a witness who is under the age of 18 years or any
  person who is the subject of any representation, written material or
  recording that constitutes [[child
  pornography]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in that section 163.1,
  not be published in any document or broadcast or transmitted in any
  way.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An order made under this section does not apply in
  respect of the disclosure of information if the disclosure is made in
  the course of the administration of military justice and it is made
  for a purpose other than to make the information known in the
  community.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355405){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355405.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order restricting publication --- victims
and witnesses

- **[[183.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless an order is made under
  section 183.5, on application of the prosecutor in respect of a victim
  or a witness, or on application of a victim or a witness, a military
  judge or, if the court martial has been convened, the military judge
  assigned to preside at the court martial may make an order directing
  that any information that could identify the victim or witness not be
  published in any document or broadcast or transmitted in any way, if
  the military judge is of the opinion that the order is in the interest
  of the proper administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military justice system participants

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application of the prosecutor in respect of a
  military justice system participant who is involved in proceedings in
  respect of an offence referred to in subsection (3) or on application
  of the military justice system participant themself, a military judge
  or, if the court martial has been convened, the military judge
  assigned to preside at the court martial may make an order directing
  that any information that could identify the military justice system
  participant not be published in any document or broadcast or
  transmitted in any way, if the military judge is of the opinion that
  the order is in the interest of the proper administration of military
  justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (2), an offence is any
  of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is an
    offence under section 423.1, 467.11, 467.111, 467.12 or 467.13 of
    the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or that is a serious offence
    committed for the benefit of, at the direction of, or in association
    with a criminal organization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a terrorism offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is an
    offence under subsection 16(1) or (2), 17(1), 19(1), 20(1), 20.1(1),
    20.2(1), 20.3(1), 20.4(1) or 22(1) of the [Foreign Interference and
    Security of Information Act](/eng/acts/O-5);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an offence punishable under section 130 that is an
    offence under subsection 21(1) or section 23 of the [Foreign
    Interference and Security of Information Act](/eng/acts/O-5) and
    that is committed in relation to an offence referred to in paragraph
    (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order made under this section does not apply in
  respect of the disclosure of information if the disclosure is made in
  the course of the administration of military justice and it is made
  for a purpose other than to make the information known in the
  community.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Making of application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An application for an order under this section must
  be made in accordance with regulations made by the Governor in
  Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The application must set out the grounds on which the
  applicant relies to establish that the order is necessary for the
  proper administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing may be held

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may hold a hearing to determine
  whether an order under this section should be made, and the hearing
  may be held in private.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make an order under this
  section, the military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the right to a fair and public hearing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether there is a real and substantial risk that
    the victim, witness or military justice system participant would
    suffer harm if their identity were disclosed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the victim, witness or military justice
    system participant needs the order for their security or to protect
    them from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims, witnesses and
    military justice system participants;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether effective alternatives are available to
    protect the identity of the victim, witness or military justice
    system participant;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the salutary and deleterious effects of the order;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the impact of the order on the freedom of
    expression of those affected by it; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} An order made under this section may be subject to
  any conditions that the military judge thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication prohibited

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Unless the military judge refuses to make an order
  under this section, no person shall publish in any document or
  broadcast or transmit in any way

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the contents of the application for the order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any evidence taken, information given or
    submissions made at a hearing held under subsection (7); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other information that could identify the
    person to whom the application relates as a victim, witness or
    military justice system participant in the proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355443){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355443.html"
  role="button"}
- [2024, c. 16, s. 57](#1460885-1487736){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1460885-1487736.html"
  role="button"}
- [2024, c. 16, s. 58](#1460934-1487738){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1460934-1487738.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-183.6-20220620.html "Link to previous version of section 183.6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security of witnesses

- **[[183.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings against an accused
  person, a military judge or, if the court martial has been convened,
  the military judge assigned to preside at the court martial may, on
  application of the prosecutor or a witness or on his or her own
  motion, make any order other than one that may be made under section
  180, if the military judge is of the opinion that the order is
  necessary to protect the security of any witness and is otherwise in
  the interest of the proper administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make the order, the
  military judge shall consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the witness's age;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the witness's mental or physical disabilities, if
    any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the right to a fair and public hearing;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the service offence;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} whether the witness needs the order to protect them
    from intimidation or retaliation;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} whether the order is needed to protect the security
    of anyone known to the witness;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} society's interest in encouraging the reporting of
    service offences and the participation of victims, witnesses and
    military justice system participants;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the importance of the witness's testimony to the
    case;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether effective alternatives to the making of the
    order are available in the circumstances;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the salutary and deleterious effects of the order;
    and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that the military judge considers
    relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adverse inference

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No adverse inference may be drawn from the fact that
  an order is, or is not, made under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 28](#1149813-1355463){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149813-1355463.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Evidence on Commission]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376871 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of commissioner to take
evidence

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Military Judge, or any military judge designated by the Chief
  Military Judge, may appoint any officer or other qualified person, in
  this section referred to as a "commissioner", to take, under oath, the
  evidence of any person required as a witness at a court martial

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} who is, by reason of physical disability arising
    out of illness, not likely to be able to attend at the time the
    trial is held;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} who is absent from the country in which the trial
    is held; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whose attendance is not readily obtainable for a
    good and sufficient reason.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of commission evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The document containing the evidence of a witness,
  taken under subsection (1) and duly certified by the commissioner is
  admissible in evidence at a trial by court martial to the same extent
  and subject to the same objections as if the evidence were given by
  the witness in person at the trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to require personal attendance of
  witness

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, in the opinion of a court martial, a witness
  whose evidence has been taken on commission should, in the interests
  of military justice, appear and give evidence before the court
  martial, and the witness is not too ill to attend the trial and is not
  outside the country in which the trial is held, the court martial may
  require the attendance of that witness.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation, examination and
  cross-examination before commissioner

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} At any proceedings before a commissioner, the accused
  person and the prosecutor are entitled to be represented and the
  persons representing them have the right to examine and cross-examine
  any witness.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 184
- 1998, c. 35, s. 45
- 2013, c. 24, s. 53
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-184-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 184"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to accused

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** The accused person
shall, at least twenty-four hours before it is admitted at the court
martial, be furnished without charge with a copy of the document
referred to in subsection 184(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 161
:::

#### [Objections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376890 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objections

- **[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a court martial is assembled, the names of the military judge and the
  members, if any, must be read to the accused person and the
  prosecutor, who shall then be asked if they object to the constitution
  of the court martial and, in the event of an objection, the decision
  as to whether to allow the objection is to be made in accordance with
  the procedure prescribed in regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The procedure for the replacement of a person in
  respect of whom an objection has been allowed shall be as prescribed
  in regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 186
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
:::

#### [Preliminary Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376898 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary proceedings

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** At any time after a
charge has been preferred but before the commencement of the trial, any
question, matter or objection in respect of the charge may, on
application, be heard and determined by a military judge or, if the
court martial has been convened, the military judge assigned to preside
at the court martial.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 187
- 1992, c. 16, s. 9
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
- 2008, c. 29, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-187-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 187"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Amendment of Charges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376903 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment if defence not prejudiced

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where it appears to a court martial
  that there is a technical defect in a charge that does not affect the
  substance of the charge, the court martial, if of the opinion that the
  conduct of the accused person's defence will not be prejudiced by an
  amendment of the charge, shall make the order for the amendment of the
  charge that it considers necessary to meet the circumstances of the
  case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment on amendment of charge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a charge is amended by a court martial, the
  court martial shall, if the accused person so requests, adjourn its
  proceedings for any period that it considers necessary to enable the
  accused person to meet the charge so amended.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minute of amendment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a charge is amended by a court martial, a
  minute of the amendment shall be endorsed on the charge sheet.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 188
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
:::

#### [Adjournments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376913 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** A court martial may
adjourn its proceedings whenever the court martial considers adjournment
desirable.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 189
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
:::

#### [Pleas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1355464 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pleas permitted

- **[[189.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An accused person who makes an
  application under subsection (2) or who, after the commencement of the
  trial, is called on to plead may plead guilty or not guilty, or any
  other plea authorized by regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plea of guilty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At any time after a court martial is convened but
  before the commencement of the trial, the military judge assigned to
  preside at the court martial may, on application, receive the accused
  person's plea of guilty in respect of any charge and, if there are no
  other charges remaining before the court martial to which pleas of not
  guilty have been recorded, determine the sentence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for accepting guilty plea

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The military judge may accept a plea of guilty only
  if he or she is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accused person is making the plea voluntarily;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the accused person

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} understands that the plea is an admission of the
      essential elements of the service offence,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} understands the nature and consequences of the
      plea, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} understands that the military judge is not
      bound by any agreement made between the accused person and the
      prosecutor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of plea

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The failure of the military judge to fully inquire
  whether the conditions set out in subsection (3) are met does not
  affect the validity of the plea.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to plead

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an accused person refuses to plead or does not
  answer directly, he or she is deemed to have made a plea of not
  guilty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allowing time

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An accused person is not entitled as of right to have
  their trial postponed, but the military judge may, if the military
  judge considers that the accused person should be allowed further time
  to plead or prepare for their defence or for any other reason, adjourn
  the trial to a later time, on any terms that the military judge
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Included or other offence

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other provision of this Act, if an
  accused person pleads not guilty of the service offence charged but
  guilty of any other service offence arising out of the same
  transaction, whether or not it is an included offence, the military
  judge may, with the consent of the prosecutor, accept that plea of
  guilty and, if the plea is accepted, the military judge shall find the
  accused person not guilty of the offence charged and find him or her
  guilty of the offence in respect of which the plea of guilty was
  accepted and enter those findings in the record of the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry of court --- serious personal
  injury offence

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is charged with a service
  offence that is a serious personal injury offence and the accused
  person and the prosecutor have entered into an agreement under which
  the accused person will enter a plea of guilty of the service offence
  charged --- or a plea of not guilty of the service offence charged but
  guilty of any other service offence arising out of the same
  transaction, whether or not it is an included offence --- the military
  judge shall, after accepting the plea of guilty, inquire of the
  prosecutor whether reasonable steps were taken to inform the victims
  of the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry of court --- certain offences

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is charged with a serious
  offence that is not a serious personal injury offence and the accused
  person and the prosecutor have entered into an agreement referred to
  in subsection (8), the military judge shall, after accepting the plea
  of guilty, inquire of the prosecutor whether any of the victims had
  advised the prosecutor of their desire to be informed if such an
  agreement were entered into and, if so, whether reasonable steps were
  taken to inform that victim of the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to inform

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (8) or (9) applies, and any victim was
  not informed of the agreement before the plea of guilty was accepted,
  the prosecutor shall, as soon as feasible, take reasonable steps to
  inform the victim of the agreement and the acceptance of the plea.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity of plea

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Neither the failure of the military judge to inquire
  of the prosecutor as required under subsection (8) or (9) nor the
  failure of the prosecutor to take reasonable steps to inform the
  victims of the agreement affects the validity of the plea.

- [(12)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 64\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 29](#1149815-1355500){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149815-1355500.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 64](#1150074-1355845){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150074-1355845.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Views]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376918 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority for viewing

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** A court martial may
view any place, thing or person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 190
- 1991, c. 43, s. 17
- 1992, c. 16, s. 10
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
:::

#### [Decisions of General Court Martial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376923 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Questions of law

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** The military judge
presiding at a General Court Martial determines all questions of law or
mixed law and fact arising before or after the commencement of the
trial.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 191
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
- 2008, c. 29, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[191.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019,
c. 15, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191.1-20080718.html "Link to previous version of section 191.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of panel

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  members of the panel determine the court martial's finding and its
  decision in respect of any other matter or question arising after the
  commencement of the trial that is not a question of law or mixed law
  and fact.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A decision of the panel in respect of a finding of
  guilty or not guilty, of unfitness to stand trial or of not
  responsible on account of mental disorder is determined by the
  unanimous vote of its members. A decision in respect of any other
  matter is determined by a majority vote.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 192
- 1992, c. 16, s. 11
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
- 2008, c. 29, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-192-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 192"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disagreement of panel

- **[[192.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the military judge presiding at a
  General Court Martial is satisfied that the members of the panel are
  unable to agree on a finding and that further retention of the panel
  would be useless, the military judge may in his or her discretion
  discharge the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution of court martial

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a panel is discharged under subsection (1), the
  court martial is dissolved and the accused person may be dealt with as
  if the trial had never commenced.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 29, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** The military judge
presiding at a General Court Martial determines the sentence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 193
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
- 2008, c. 29, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Similar Offences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376950 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Similar offences may be considered in
imposing sentence

- **[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court martial may, on the request of a person who is found guilty and
  who admits to having committed service offences similar in character
  to an offence of which the person is found guilty, take those service
  offences into consideration for the purposes of the sentence as if the
  person had been charged with, tried for and found guilty of those
  service offences.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial takes an admitted service offence
  into consideration for the purposes of the sentence, the sentence may
  not include any punishment higher in the scale of punishments than the
  punishment that might be imposed in respect of any offence of which
  the person is found guilty.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 194
- 1998, c. 35, s. 46
:::

#### [Absconding Accused]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376958 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused absconding during court martial

- **[[194.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An accused person who absconds
  during the course of their trial by court martial, whether or not the
  person is charged jointly with another person, is deemed to have
  waived their right to be present at their trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing or adjourning court martial

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A military judge presiding at the court martial of an
  accused person who absconds may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} continue the trial and proceed to a judgment or
    verdict and, if the accused person is found guilty, impose a
    sentence in their absence; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a warrant is issued under section 249.23,
    adjourn the trial to await the appearance of the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing court martial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A military judge who adjourns a court martial may at
  any time continue the court martial if he or she is satisfied that it
  is no longer in the interests of military justice to await the
  appearance of the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adverse inference

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A court martial may draw an inference adverse to the
  accused person from the fact that the accused person has absconded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused not entitled to reopening

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An accused person who reappears at their trial is not
  entitled to have any part of the proceedings that were conducted in
  their absence reopened unless the court martial is satisfied that
  because of exceptional circumstances it is in the interests of
  military justice to reopen the proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counsel for accused person may continue
  to act

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Counsel for an accused person who absconds is not
  deprived, as result of the absconding, of any authority he or she may
  have to continue to represent the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 54
:::

#### [Pronouncement of Findings and Sentence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376976 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner and effective date of
pronouncement

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** The finding and
sentence of a court martial shall, at the conclusion of the trial of the
offender so sentenced, be pronounced in open court to the offender, who
shall be under the sentence as of the date of the pronouncement thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 170
:::

#### [Death or Incapacity]{.HTitleText3} {#h-376981 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Death or incapacity to continue of judge

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the military judge presiding
  at a court martial dies or is for any reason unable to continue, the
  proceedings of the court martial are deemed to be adjourned. The
  proceedings may be continued with another military judge, in this
  section referred to as the "replacement judge", assigned by the Chief
  Military Judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When finding not pronounced

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial has not pronounced its finding
  before the presiding military judge dies or becomes unable to
  continue, the replacement judge

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a General Court Martial, may order
    that the court martial

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} continue from the stage at which it was when it
      was deemed to be adjourned, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} commence again, at the stage immediately
      following the plea of the accused person, as if no evidence had
      been introduced; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Standing Court Martial, shall
    commence the court martial again at the stage immediately following
    the plea of the accused person, as if no evidence had been
    introduced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjudications and evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a court martial continued under
  subparagraph (2)(a)(i),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if an adjudication was made before the adjournment
    but no order was made, the replacement judge shall make any order
    that is authorized by law and required in the circumstances; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if any evidence was adduced before the adjournment,
    the evidence is deemed to have been adduced before the replacement
    judge but, if the prosecutor and the accused so agree, any part of
    that evidence may be adduced again.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When finding pronounced

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial pronounced its finding before
  the presiding military judge died or became unable to continue, the
  replacement judge shall determine the sentence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 196
- 1998, c. 35, s. 47
- 2008, c. 29, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution

- **[[196.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, after an accused person has made
  a plea but before the court martial pronounces its finding, two or
  more members of the panel die or are for any reason unable to continue
  to act, the court martial is dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Illness of accused

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, on account of the illness of an accused
  person, it is impossible to continue the trial of that person, the
  court martial is dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unfit to stand trial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial finds, under subsection 198(2),
  that an accused person is unfit to stand trial and it completes the
  proceedings under subsection 200(2), the court martial is dissolved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of dissolution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial is dissolved pursuant to this
  section, the accused person may be dealt with as if the trial had
  never commenced.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 47
- 2008, c. 29, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 196.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 6.1]{.HLabel2}[Forensic DNA Analysis]{.HTitleText2} {#h-377010 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[196.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.11 .sectionLabel}** The definitions
in this section apply in this Division.

[[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Commissioner of the
    Royal Canadian Mounted Police. ([commissaire]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a primary
    designated offence or a secondary designated offence. ([infraction
    désignée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[DNA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[DNA]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means deoxyribonucleic
    acid. ([ADN]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[DNA profile]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[DNA profile]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the results of forensic
    DNA analysis. ([profil d'identification génétique]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[forensic DNA analysis]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[forensic DNA analysis]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a bodily substance that is taken
      in execution of a warrant under section 196.12, means forensic DNA
      analysis of the bodily substance and the comparison of the results
      of that analysis with the results of the analysis of the DNA in
      the bodily substance referred to in paragraph 196.12(1)(b), and
      includes any incidental tests associated with that analysis; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a bodily substance that is
      provided voluntarily in the course of an investigation of a
      designated offence or is taken from a person in execution of an
      order made under section 196.14 or under an authorization granted
      under section 196.24, or to a bodily substance referred to in
      paragraph 196.12(1)(b), means forensic DNA analysis of the bodily
      substance. ([analyse génétique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[peace officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a police officer, police constable, bailiff,
      constable or other person employed for the preservation and
      maintenance of the public peace or for the service or execution of
      civil process; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer or a non-commissioned member of the
      Canadian Forces who is

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a member of the military police, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} employed on duties that the Governor in
        Council has prescribed in the regulations to be of such a kind
        as to necessitate that the officer or non-commissioned member
        performing them has the powers of a peace officer. ([agent de la
        paix]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[prescribed form]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[prescribed form]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a form prescribed in
    the regulations made by the Governor in Council. ([formulaire
    réglementaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[primary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[primary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence within the meaning of paragraphs (a)
      and (c.02) of the definition [[primary designated
      offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 487.04 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is punishable under section 130;

    - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} an offence within the meaning of any of
      paragraphs (a.1) to (c.01), (c.03) and (c.1) of the definition
      [[primary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section
      487.04 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is punishable
      under section 130; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an attempt to commit or, other than for the
      purpose of subsection 196.12(1), a conspiracy to commit an offence
      within the meaning of any of paragraphs (a) to (c.03) of the
      definition [[primary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
      section 487.04 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is
      punishable under section 130. ([infraction
      primaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[secondary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[secondary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence within the meaning of any of
      paragraphs (a) to (d.2) of the definition [[secondary designated
      offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 487.04 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is punishable under section 130;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence under any of the following provisions
      of this Act:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 77(a) (violence to person bringing
        materiel to forces),

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 79 (mutiny with violence),

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 84 (striking a superior officer),

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 87(b) (violence while in custody),

      - [(v)]{.lawlabel} section 95 (striking a subordinate),

      - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 107(a) (endangering a person on an
        aircraft), or

      - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} section 127 (handling of dangerous
        substances); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an attempt to commit or, other than for the
      purpose of subsection 196.12(1), a conspiracy to commit any
      offence referred to in paragraph (a) or (b). ([infraction
      secondaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 10, s. 1
- 2005, c. 25, s. 23
- 2007, c. 22, ss. 35, 48
- 2010, c. 17, s. 46
- 2013, c. 24, s. 55
- 2014, c. 25, s. 36
- 2018, c. 21, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196.11-20141206.html "Link to previous version of section 196.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information for warrant to take bodily
substances for forensic DNA analysis

- **[[196.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A military judge, on [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application in the prescribed form, may
  issue a warrant in the prescribed form authorizing the taking for the
  purpose of forensic DNA analysis, from a person subject to the Code of
  Service Discipline, of any number of samples of bodily substances that
  is reasonably required for that purpose, if the military judge is
  satisfied by information on oath that it is in the best interests of
  the administration of military justice to do so and that there are
  reasonable grounds to believe

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that a designated offence has been committed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that a bodily substance has been found or obtained

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the place where the offence was committed,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on or within the body of the victim of the
      offence,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on anything worn or carried by the victim at
      the time the offence was committed, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} on or within the body of any person or thing or
      at any place associated with the commission of the offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the person subject to the Code of Service
    Discipline was a party to the offence; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that forensic DNA analysis of a bodily substance
    from the person will provide evidence about whether the bodily
    substance referred to in paragraph (b) was from that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In considering whether to issue the warrant, the
  military judge shall have regard to all relevant matters, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the offence and the circumstances
    surrounding its commission; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether there is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a peace officer who is able, by virtue of
      training or experience, to obtain a bodily substance from the
      person, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} another person who is able, by virtue of
      training or experience, to obtain under the direction of a peace
      officer a bodily substance from the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 10, s. 1
- 2013, c. 24, s. 56
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196.12-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 196.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Telewarrants

- **[[196.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a peace officer believes that it
  would be impracticable to appear personally before a military judge to
  apply for a warrant, the peace officer may submit an information on
  oath to the judge by telephone or other means of telecommunication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An information submitted by telephone or other means
  of telecommunication shall include, in addition to the information
  described in subsection 196.12(1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the circumstances that make it
    impracticable for the peace officer to appear personally before a
    military judge; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a statement as to any prior application for a
    warrant under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath in writing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the telecommunication is in written form, a
  written statement by the peace officer that all matters contained in
  the information are true to the officer's knowledge and belief is
  deemed to be a statement made under oath.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information filed with Court Martial
  Administrator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The military judge shall, as soon as practicable,
  certify the information as to time and date of receipt, and cause it
  to be filed with the Court Martial Administrator. If the
  telecommunication is not in written form, the information that is to
  be filed is the verbatim record, or a transcription of it, certified
  by the judge as to its contents and time and date of receipt.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formalities respecting warrant and
  facsimiles

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When a military judge issues a warrant by telephone
  or other means of telecommunication that is not in written form,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall complete and sign the warrant in
    the prescribed form, noting on its face the time, date and place of
    issuance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the peace officer, on the direction of the judge,
    shall complete, in duplicate, a facsimile of the warrant in the
    prescribed form, noting on its face the name of the judge and the
    time, date and place of issuance; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall, as soon as practicable after the
    warrant has been issued, cause the warrant to be filed with the
    Court Martial Administrator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of warrant ---
  telecommunication in written form

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} When a military judge issues a warrant by a means of
  telecommunication in written form,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall complete and sign the warrant in
    the prescribed form, noting on its face the time, date and place of
    issuance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall transmit the warrant by the means
    of telecommunication to the peace officer who submitted the
    information, and the copy of the warrant received by the peace
    officer is deemed to be a facsimile within the meaning of paragraph
    (5)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the peace officer shall procure another facsimile
    of the warrant; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall, as soon as practicable after the
    warrant has been issued, cause the warrant to be filed with Court
    Martial Administrator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of authorization

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In any proceeding in which it is material for a court
  to be satisfied that the taking of samples of a bodily substance was
  authorized by a warrant issued by telephone or other means of
  telecommunication, the absence of the information or warrant, signed
  by the military judge and carrying on its face a notation of the time,
  date and place of issuance, is, in the absence of evidence to the
  contrary, proof that the taking of the samples was not authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duplicates and facsimiles acceptable

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A duplicate or a facsimile of an information or a
  warrant has the same probative force as the original for the purpose
  of subsection (7).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 10, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- primary designated offences

- **[[196.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A court martial shall make an order
  in the prescribed form authorizing the taking of the number of samples
  of bodily substances that is reasonably required for the purpose of
  forensic DNA analysis from a person who is found guilty of an offence
  committed at any time, including before June 30, 2000, if that offence
  is a primary designated offence within the meaning of paragraph (a) of
  the definition [[primary designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
  section 196.11 when the person is sentenced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- primary designated offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A court martial shall make such an order in the
  prescribed form in relation to a person who is found guilty of an
  offence committed at any time, including before June 30, 2000, if that
  offence is a primary designated offence within the meaning of
  paragraph (a.1) or (b) of the definition [[primary designated
  offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 196.11 when the person is
  sentenced. However, the court martial is not required to make the
  order if it is satisfied that the person has established that the
  impact of such an order on their privacy and security of the person
  would be grossly disproportionate to the public interest in the
  protection of society and the proper administration of military
  justice, to be achieved through the early detection, arrest and
  conviction of offenders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- persons found not responsible
  and secondary designated offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A court martial may, on application by the prosecutor
  and if it is satisfied that it is in the best interests of the
  administration of military justice to do so, make such an order in the
  prescribed form in relation to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who is found not responsible on account of
    mental disorder for an offence committed at any time, including
    before June 30, 2000, if that offence is a designated offence when
    the finding is made; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who is found guilty of an offence
    committed at any time, including before June 30, 2000, if that
    offence is a secondary designated offence when the person is
    sentenced.

  In deciding whether to make the order, the court martial shall
  consider the nature of the offence and the circumstances surrounding
  its commission, any previous convictions, any previous finding of not
  responsible on account of mental disorder for a designated offence and
  the impact that such an order would have on the person's privacy and
  security and shall give reasons for the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to offender

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial makes an order authorizing the
  taking of samples of bodily substances, it may make an order in the
  prescribed form to require the person to report at the place, day and
  time set out in the order and submit to the taking of the samples.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 10, s. 1
- 2005, c. 25, s. 24
- 2007, c. 22, ss. 36, 48
- [2019, c. 15, s. 31](#1149820-1355503){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149820-1355503.html"
  role="button"}
:::
