[Previous
Version](section-202.121-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 202.121"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Mental Disorder When Offence Committed]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377444 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence of mental disorder

- **[[202.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No accused person shall be held
  responsible under this Act for a service offence in respect of an act
  committed or an omission made while suffering from a mental disorder
  that rendered the person incapable of appreciating the nature and
  quality of the act or omission or of knowing that it was wrong.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person is presumed not to suffer from a mental
  disorder so as to be exempt from responsibility by virtue of
  subsection (1), until the contrary is proved on the balance of
  probabilities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proof that an accused person was
  suffering from a mental disorder so as to be exempt from
  responsibility is on the party raising the issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, where a court martial has
  reasonable grounds to believe that evidence of the mental condition of
  an accused person is necessary for the purpose of determining whether
  the accused person was, at the time of the commission of the alleged
  offence, suffering from a mental disorder so as to be exempt from
  responsibility, the court martial may make an order for an assessment
  of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Finding of not responsible on account of
mental disorder

- **[[202.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial finds that an
  accused person committed the act or made the omission that forms the
  basis of the offence charged but was suffering at the time from a
  mental disorder so as to be exempt from responsibility, the court
  martial shall make a finding that the accused person committed the act
  or made the omission but is not responsible on account of mental
  disorder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a finding of not responsible on account of
  mental disorder is made, the accused person shall not be found guilty
  or convicted of the offence, but

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accused person may not be tried or tried again
    in respect of that offence or any other substantially similar
    offence arising out of the facts that gave rise to that offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any civil court may take into account the finding
    in considering any application for judicial interim release or in
    considering the dispositions to make or sentence to impose against
    that person for any other offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any court martial or the Court Martial Appeal Court
    may consider the finding in considering an application for release
    pending appeal under Division 10 or in considering the dispositions
    to make or sentence to impose against that person for any other
    offence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 51\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the finding may be considered in making an order
    under Division 3 in respect of that person;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 33\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the finding does not include a finding or
    determination respecting civil liability; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the Parole Board of Canada or any provincial parole
    board may take the finding into account in considering an
    application by that person for parole or for a record suspension
    under the [Criminal Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47) in respect of any
    other offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Finding not previous conviction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A finding of not responsible on account of mental
  disorder is not a previous conviction for the purposes of any offence
  under any Act for which a greater punishment is prescribed by reason
  of previous convictions.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1998, c. 35, s. 51
- 2005, c. 25, s. 30
- 2007, c. 5, s. 3
- 2012, c. 1, ss. 152, 160
- [2019, c. 15, s. 33](#1149828-1355508){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149828-1355508.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355626){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355626.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.14-20130228.html "Link to previous version of section 202.14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition hearing

- **[[202.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial makes a
  finding of not responsible on account of mental disorder in respect of
  an accused person, the court martial shall hold a hearing and make a
  disposition under section 202.16, where the court martial is satisfied
  that it can readily make a disposition in respect of the accused
  person and that a disposition should be made without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, where a court martial has
  reasonable grounds to believe that evidence of the mental condition of
  an accused person is necessary for the purpose of determining the
  appropriate disposition to be made under this section, the court
  martial may make an order for an assessment of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, s. 61(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.15-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition

- **[[202.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial makes a
  disposition by virtue of subsection 202.15(1), it shall, taking into
  account the safety of the public, which is the paramount
  consideration, the mental condition of the accused person, the
  reintegration of the accused person into society and the other needs
  of the accused person, make one of the following dispositions that is
  necessary and appropriate in the circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the accused person be
    released from custody without conditions if, in the opinion of the
    court martial, the accused person is not a significant threat to the
    safety of the public;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the accused person be
    released from custody subject to such conditions as the court
    martial considers appropriate; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the accused person be
    detained in custody in a hospital or other appropriate place
    determined by the court martial, subject to such conditions as the
    court martial considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treatment not a condition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No order made under subsection (1) shall direct that
  any psychiatric or other treatment of the accused person be carried
  out or direct that the accused person submit to such treatment, except
  that the order may include a condition regarding psychiatric or other
  treatment where the accused person has consented to the condition and
  the court martial considers the condition to be reasonable and
  necessary in the interests of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1997, c. 18, s. 133
- [2014, c. 6, s. 24](#1034497-1353104){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034497-1353104.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.16-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.16"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [High-Risk Accused]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1353105 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to court martial

- **[[202.161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial makes a finding
  under subsection 202.14(1) that an accused person is not responsible
  on account of mental disorder and it has not terminated its
  proceedings in respect of the accused person, the Director of Military
  Prosecutions may make an application to the court martial for a
  finding that the accused person is a high-risk accused.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to Chief Military Judge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial has terminated its proceedings
  in respect of the accused person, the Director of Military
  Prosecutions may make the application to the Chief Military Judge. On
  receipt of the application, the Chief Military Judge shall cause the
  Court Martial Administrator to convene a Standing Court Martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No application shall be made under subsection (1)
  or (2) if a disposition has been made to release the accused person
  from custody without conditions or to discharge the accused person
  absolutely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Finding

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court martial referred to in subsection (1)
  or (2) may, at the conclusion of a hearing, find the accused person to
  be a high-risk accused if the accused person has been found not
  responsible on account of mental disorder for a serious personal
  injury offence, the accused person was 18 years of age or more at the
  time of the commission of the offence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court martial is satisfied that there is a
    substantial likelihood that the accused person will use violence
    that could endanger the life or safety of another person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the court martial is of the opinion that the acts
    that constitute the offence were of such a brutal nature as to
    indicate a risk of grave physical or psychological harm to another
    person.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 64\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to find that the accused person
  is a high-risk accused, the court martial shall consider all relevant
  evidence, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature and circumstances of the offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any pattern of repetitive behaviour of which the
    offence forms a part;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the accused person's current mental condition;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the past and expected course of the accused
    person's treatment, including the accused person's willingness to
    follow treatment; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the opinions of experts who have examined the
    accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention of high-risk accused person

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial finds the accused person to be a
  high-risk accused, the court martial shall make a disposition under
  paragraph 202.16(1)(c), but the accused person's detention must not be
  subject to any condition that would permit the accused person to be
  absent from the hospital or other appropriate place unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is appropriate, in the opinion of the person in
    charge of the hospital or other appropriate place, for the accused
    person to be absent from the hospital or place for medical reasons
    or for any purpose that is necessary for the accused person's
    treatment, if the accused person is escorted by a person who is
    authorized by the person in charge of the hospital or place; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a structured plan has been prepared to address any
    risk related to the accused person's absence and, as a result, that
    absence will not present an undue risk to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, if the court martial has
  reasonable grounds to believe that evidence of the mental condition of
  an accused person is necessary for the purpose of determining the
  appropriate disposition to be made under this section, the court
  martial may make an order for an assessment of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2014, c. 6, s. 25](#1034506-1353137){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034506-1353137.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 64](#1150074-1355847){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150074-1355847.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to court martial for review

- **[[202.162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.162
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a Review Board, in exercising a
  power under section 202.25, decides to refer to a court martial for
  review under subsection 672.84(1) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) a finding that an accused person is a high-risk
  accused, the Review Board shall, immediately after making the
  decision, cause a copy of it to be sent to the Chief Military Judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convening court martial

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a copy of the decision, the Chief
  Military Judge shall cause the Court Martial Administrator to convene
  a Standing Court Martial to review the finding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of finding by court martial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, at the conclusion of a
  hearing, revoke the finding if the court martial is satisfied that
  there is not a substantial likelihood that the accused person will use
  violence that could endanger the life or safety of another person, in
  which case sections 202.15 and 202.21 apply as if the court martial
  has made a finding of not responsible on account of mental disorder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Finding not revoked

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial does not revoke the finding, it
  shall immediately send to the Review Board, in original or copied
  form, a transcript of the hearing, any other document or information
  related to the hearing, and all exhibits filed with it, if the
  transcript, document, information or exhibits are in its possession.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, if the court martial has
  reasonable grounds to believe that evidence of the mental condition of
  the accused person is necessary for the purpose of determining whether
  to revoke the finding, the court martial may make an order for an
  assessment of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2014, c. 6, s. 25](#1034506-1353150){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034506-1353150.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [General Provisions Respecting Assessment Orders, Dispositions and Assessment Reports]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377490 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for custody

- **[[202.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An accused person shall not be
  placed in custody under an assessment order made by a court martial
  under this Division unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court martial is satisfied that on the evidence
    custody is necessary to assess the accused person, or that on the
    evidence of a medical practitioner custody is desirable to assess
    the accused person and the accused person consents to custody;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} custody of the accused person is required in
    respect of any other matter or by virtue of any other provision of
    this Act or the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the prosecutor, having been given a reasonable
    opportunity to do so, shows that the detention of the accused person
    in custody is justified having regard to all the circumstances,
    including those set out in paragraphs 158(1)(a) to (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of medical practitioner in
  writing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraph (1)(a), where the
  prosecutor and the accused person agree, the evidence of a medical
  practitioner may be in the form of a report in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No treatment order on assessment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No assessment order made under this Division shall
  direct that any psychiatric or other treatment of an accused person be
  carried out or direct that the accused person submit to such
  treatment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 52(E), 92
- 2005, c. 22, s. 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.17-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No custody or release orders during
assessment

- **[[202.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} During the period that an assessment
  order made by a court martial under this Division is in force, no
  order may be made for custody or release from custody of the accused
  person under any provision of Division 3 or for release from detention
  or imprisonment under any provision of Division 10 in respect of that
  offence or an included offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of assessment order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 202.17(1), a court martial may,
  at any time while an assessment order made by the court martial under
  this Division is in force, if it is established to the satisfaction of
  the court martial on a balance of probabilities that it is necessary
  to do so, vary the terms and conditions respecting the custody or
  release from custody of the accused person specified in the order in
  such manner as the court martial considers appropriate in the
  circumstances.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 53, 92
- 2005, c. 22, s. 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.18-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment report

- **[[202.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An assessment order made by a court
  martial under this Division may require the person who makes the
  assessment to submit in writing an assessment report on the mental
  condition of the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment report to be filed with
  court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An assessment report shall be filed with the court
  martial that ordered it at the place and within the period specified
  by the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distribution of assessment report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, where an assessment report is
  filed pursuant to subsection (2), the court martial shall cause copies
  of it to be sent to the prosecutor, the accused person and any counsel
  representing the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment report part of record

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations, an assessment report shall
  form part of the record of the proceedings in respect of which it was
  prepared.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.19-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of disposition

**[[202.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.2 .sectionLabel}** A disposition made
in respect of an accused person under section 201, 202 or 202.16 shall
come into force on the day that it is made or on any later day that the
court martial specifies in it, and shall remain in force until the
Review Board of the appropriate province holds a hearing and makes a
disposition under section 672.83 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, s. 53
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure at disposition hearing

- **[[202.201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.201
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A hearing by a court martial to make
  or review a disposition in respect of an accused person shall be held
  in accordance with this section and the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing to be informal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The hearing may be conducted in as informal a manner
  as is appropriate in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interested person may be party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial may designate as a party any person
  who has a substantial interest in protecting the accused person's
  interests, if the court martial is of the opinion that it is just to
  do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of hearing --- parties

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall give notice of the hearing to
  the parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of hearing --- victim

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, at the request of a victim
  of the offence, give the victim notice of the hearing and of the
  relevant provisions of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of release from custody and
  intended place of residence

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person is released from custody
  without conditions under paragraph 202.16(1)(a) or with conditions
  under paragraph 201(1)(a) or 202.16(1)(b), a notice of the release and
  the accused person's intended place of residence shall, at the
  victim's request, be given to the victim within the time and in the
  manner fixed by regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order excluding public

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial considers it to be in the
  accused person's best interests and not contrary to the public
  interest, it may order the public or any members of the public to be
  excluded from the hearing or any part of it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to counsel

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The accused person or any other party has the right
  to be represented by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assigning counsel

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A court martial shall, either before or at the time
  of the hearing of an accused person who is not represented by counsel,
  direct that counsel be provided by the Director of Defence Counsel
  Services if the accused person has been found unfit to stand trial or
  the interests of military justice require that counsel be provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of accused person to be present

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (11), the accused person has
  the right to be present during the entire hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal or absence of accused person

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The court martial may permit the accused person to
  be absent during the entire hearing or any part of it on any
  conditions that the court martial considers appropriate. The court
  martial may also cause the accused person to be removed and barred
  from re-entry for the entire hearing or any part of it for any of the
  following reasons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the accused person is interrupting the hearing and
    it is not feasible to continue it in the accused person's presence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the court martial is satisfied that the accused
    person's presence would likely endanger the life or safety of
    another person or would seriously impair the treatment or recovery
    of the accused person; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the court martial is satisfied that the accused
    person should not be present for the hearing of evidence, oral or
    written submissions, or the cross-examination of any witness
    respecting the existence of grounds for removing the accused person
    under paragraph (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of parties at hearing

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Any party may adduce evidence, make oral or written
  submissions, call witnesses and cross-examine any witness called by
  any other party and, on application, cross-examine any person who made
  an assessment report that was submitted in writing to the court
  martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witnesses

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} A party may not compel the attendance of witnesses,
  but may request the court martial to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Video links

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} If the accused person agrees, the court martial may
  permit them to appear by closed-circuit television or any other means
  that allows the court martial and the accused person to engage in
  simultaneous visual and oral communication, for any part of the
  hearing, so long as the accused person is given the opportunity to
  communicate privately with counsel if they are represented by counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of mental condition of
  accused person

  [(15)]{.lawlabel} A court martial that reviews a disposition shall, on
  receipt of an assessment report, determine if there has been any
  change in the accused person's mental condition since the disposition
  was made or last reviewed that may provide grounds for the accused
  person's release from custody under section 202.16. If the court
  martial determines that there has been such a change, it shall notify
  every victim of the offence that they may prepare a statement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Victim impact statement

  [(16)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of making or reviewing a disposition
  in respect of an accused person, a court martial shall consider the
  statement of any victim of the offence describing the physical or
  emotional harm done to, or property damage or economic loss suffered
  by, the victim as a result of the commission of the offence and the
  impact of the offence on the victim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(17)]{.lawlabel} A victim's statement must be prepared in the form,
  and filed in accordance with the procedures, provided for by
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation of victim statement

  [(18)]{.lawlabel} Unless the court martial considers that it would not
  be in the best interests of the administration of military justice,
  the court martial shall, at the victim's request, permit the victim to
  read their statement or to present the statement in any other manner
  that the court martial considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration by court martial

  [(19)]{.lawlabel} Whether or not a statement has been prepared and
  filed, the court martial may consider any other evidence concerning
  any victim of the offence for the purpose of making or reviewing the
  disposition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of statement

  [(20)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Administrator shall, as soon as
  feasible after receiving a victim's statement, ensure that a copy is
  provided to the prosecutor and to the accused person or their counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry by court martial

  [(21)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after a finding of not
  responsible on account of mental disorder is made and before making a
  disposition, the court martial shall inquire of the prosecutor or a
  victim of the offence, or any person representing a victim of the
  offence, whether the victim has been advised that they may prepare a
  statement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(22)]{.lawlabel} On application of the prosecutor or a victim or on
  its own motion, the court martial may adjourn the hearing to permit a
  victim to prepare a statement or to present evidence referred to in
  subsection (19) if it is satisfied that the adjournment would not
  interfere with the proper administration of military justice.

- [(23)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 65\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 59
- [2014, c. 6, ss. 26, 32](#1034508-1353201){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034508-1353201.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 65](#1150091-1355851){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150091-1355851.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.201-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 202.201"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional conditions --- safety and
security

**[[202.202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.202 .sectionLabel}** If a court
martial holds a hearing referred to in section 202.201, the court
martial shall consider whether it is desirable, in the interests of the
safety and security of any person, particularly a victim of or witness
to the offence or a justice system participant, to include as a
condition of the disposition that the accused

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} abstain from communicating, directly or indirectly,
  with any victim, witness or other person identified in the
  disposition, or refrain from going to any place specified in the
  disposition; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} comply with any other condition specified in the
  disposition that the court martial considers necessary to ensure the
  safety and security of those persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2014, c. 6, ss. 26, 32](#1034508-1353207){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034508-1353207.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status quo pending Review Board's hearing

- **[[202.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial makes a
  finding of unfit to stand trial or not responsible on account of
  mental disorder in respect of an accused person and does not make a
  disposition in respect of the accused person under section 201, 202 or
  202.16, any order or direction for the custody or release from custody
  of the accused person that is in force at the time the finding is made
  continues in force, subject to its terms, until a disposition in
  respect of the accused person is made by the Review Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), a court martial may,
  on cause being shown, cancel any order or direction referred to in
  subsection (1) and make any other order or direction for the custody
  or release from custody of the accused person that the court martial
  considers to be appropriate in the circumstances, including an order
  directing that the accused person be detained in custody in a hospital
  or other appropriate place pending a disposition in respect of the
  accused person made by the Review Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent court martial order takes
  precedence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Pending a disposition by the Review Board, where a
  court martial

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes a disposition under paragraph 201(1)(b) or
    202.16(1)(c) in respect of an accused person, that disposition takes
    precedence over any prior sentence of imprisonment or detention of
    the accused person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} imposes a sentence of imprisonment or detention on
    the accused person, that sentence takes precedence over any prior
    disposition made under paragraph 201(1)(b) or 202.16(1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition takes precedence over
  probation order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a disposition is made under paragraph 201(1)(b)
  or 202.16(1)(c) and the accused person is convicted or discharged
  conditionally under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) by a civil
  court in respect of another offence but is not sentenced to a term of
  imprisonment in respect of that other offence, the disposition shall
  come into force and, notwithstanding any provision of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), takes precedence over any probation order made
  in respect of the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, s. 54
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedural irregularities

- **[[202.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any procedural irregularity in
  relation to a hearing held by a court martial or Review Board does not
  affect the validity of the proceedings unless the accused person
  suffers substantial prejudice thereby.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for disposition and copies to
  be provided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After making a disposition in respect of an accused
  person under section 201, 202 or 202.16, a court martial shall state
  its reasons for making the disposition in the record of the
  proceedings, and shall provide or cause to be provided to the accused
  person, the prosecutor and the person in charge of the hospital or
  other appropriate place where the accused person is detained in
  custody or is to attend pursuant to the disposition a copy of the
  disposition and those reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmittal of transcript to Review
  Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial holds a hearing under subsection
  200(2) or 202.15(1), whether or not it makes a disposition, it shall
  send without delay to the Review Board of the appropriate province, in
  original or copied form, a transcript of the hearing, any document or
  information relating to the hearing and all exhibits filed with it, if
  the transcript, document, information or exhibits are in its
  possession.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmittal of transcript to Review
  Board

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial does not hold a hearing
  referred to in subsection (3), it shall send without delay to the
  Review Board of the appropriate province, following a verdict of unfit
  to stand trial or not responsible on account of mental disorder, in
  original or copied form, any transcript of the proceedings in respect
  of the accused, any document or information relating to the
  proceedings and all exhibits filed with it, if the transcript,
  document, information or exhibits are in its possession.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of committal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a court martial makes a disposition in respect
  of an accused person under section 201 or 202.16 directing that the
  accused person be placed and detained in custody in a hospital or
  other appropriate place, a committing authority referred to in
  subsection 219(1) shall issue a committal order in such form as is
  prescribed by regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, ss. 55, 61(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.22-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[202.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
  [[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a justice as defined in section
  2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest without warrant for
  contravention of disposition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A member of the military police or any other peace
  officer within the meaning of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) may
  arrest an accused person without a warrant if he or she has reasonable
  grounds to believe that the accused person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is at large contrary to the terms of a disposition
    made by a court martial under section 201, 202 or 202.16 or by a
    Review Board; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has contravened or wilfully failed to comply with
    the disposition or any condition of a disposition or assessment
    order, or is about to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused person released subject to
  conditions

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The member of the military police or other peace
  officer who makes an arrest under subsection (2) may release an
  accused person arrested under that subsection who is subject to a
  disposition made by a court martial under paragraph 201(1)(a) or
  202.16(1)(b), a disposition made by a Review Board under paragraph
  672.54(b) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or an assessment
  order and deliver the accused person to the place specified in the
  disposition or assessment order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued detention

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The member of the military police or other peace
  officer shall not release the accused person if he or she has
  reasonable grounds to believe

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that it is necessary in the public interest that
    the accused person be detained in custody having regard to all the
    circumstances, including the need to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} establish the identity of the accused person,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} establish the terms and conditions of the
      disposition or assessment order referred to in subsection (2.1),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} prevent the commission of an offence, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} prevent the accused person from doing anything
      referred to in paragraph (2)(a) or (b); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the accused person is subject to a disposition
    or an assessment order of a Review Board of another province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused person brought before justice
  or commanding officer

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} An accused person referred to in subsection (2.1)
  who is not released or an accused person arrested under subsection (2)
  who is subject to a disposition of a court martial made under
  paragraph 201(1)(b), subsection 202(1) or paragraph 202.16(1)(c) or a
  disposition of a Review Board made under paragraph 672.54(c) of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) shall be taken to a justice having
  jurisdiction in the territorial division in which the accused person
  is arrested or a commanding officer without unreasonable delay and in
  any event within a period of twenty-four hours after the arrest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justice or commanding officer not
  available

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a justice having jurisdiction in the territorial
  division in which the accused person is arrested or a commanding
  officer is not available within a period of twenty-four hours after
  the arrest, the accused person shall be taken before a justice or
  commanding officer as soon as practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release of accused person

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A justice or commanding officer shall release an
  accused who is brought before them unless they are satisfied that
  there are reasonable grounds to believe that the circumstances
  referred to in paragraph (2)(a) or (b) exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} If the justice or commanding officer releases the
  accused, notice shall be given to the Review Board that made the
  disposition or to the court martial or Review Board that made the
  assessment order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order pending decision of Review Board

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a justice or commanding officer before whom an
  accused person is taken is satisfied that there are reasonable grounds
  to believe that the circumstances referred to in paragraph (2)(a)
  or (b) exist, the justice or commanding officer may, pending a hearing
  of a Review Board with respect to the disposition or a hearing of a
  court martial or Review Board with respect to the assessment order,
  make an order that is appropriate in the circumstances in relation to
  the accused person, including an order that the accused person be
  delivered to a place that is specified in the disposition or
  assessment order. If the justice or commanding officer makes an order
  under this subsection, notice shall be given to the Review Board that
  made the disposition or to the court martial or Review Board that made
  the assessment order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Review Board

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a Review Board receives a notice pursuant to
  subsection (4), it may exercise the powers and shall perform the
  duties referred to in the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in respect
  of the accused person as if the Review Board were conducting a review
  of a disposition.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, ss. 56, 61(F)
- 2013, c. 24, s. 60
:::

[Previous
Version](section-202.23-20060102.html "Link to previous version of section 202.23"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Protected Statements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377632 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[protected
statement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[202.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[protected
  statement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a statement made by the accused
  person, during the course and for the purposes of an assessment
  ordered under this Division or treatment directed by a disposition
  made under section 202, to the person specified in the assessment
  order or the disposition, or to anyone acting under that person's
  direction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protected statements not admissible
  against accused

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No protected statement or reference to a protected
  statement made by an accused person is admissible in evidence, without
  the consent of the accused person, in any proceeding before a court,
  court martial, tribunal, body or person with jurisdiction to compel
  the production of evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2), evidence of a
  protected statement is admissible for the purpose of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determining whether the accused person is unfit to
    stand trial;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} making a disposition or placement decision
    respecting the accused person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} determining under subsection 202.162(3) whether to
    revoke a finding that an accused person is a high-risk accused;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} determining whether the balance of the mind of the
    accused person was disturbed at the time of commission of the
    alleged offence, where the accused person is a female charged with
    an offence arising out of the death of her newly-born child;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} determining whether the accused person was, at the
    time of the commission of an alleged offence, suffering from
    automatism or a mental disorder so as to be exempt from
    responsibility by virtue of subsection 202.13(1), if the accused
    person puts his or her mental capacity to form the requisite intent
    into issue or if the prosecutor raises the issue after a finding is
    made of not responsible on account of mental disorder;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} challenging the credibility of an accused person in
    any proceeding where the testimony of the accused person is
    inconsistent in a material particular with a protected statement
    that the accused person made previously; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} establishing the perjury of an accused person who
    is charged with perjury in respect of a statement made in any
    proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
- 2005, c. 22, s. 57
- [2014, c. 6, s. 27](#1034510-1353209){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034510-1353209.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-202.24-20060102.html "Link to previous version of section 202.24"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Provisions of Criminal Code Applicable]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377649 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Review Board

- **[[202.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Review Boards and their chairpersons
  may exercise the powers and shall perform the duties assigned to them
  under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), with any modifications that
  the circumstances require and unless the context otherwise requires,
  in relation to findings made by courts martial of unfit to stand trial
  or not responsible on account of mental disorder, and in relation to
  dispositions made under section 201 or 202.16 or subsection
  202.161(4), except for the powers and duties referred to in
  subsections 672.5(8.1) and (8.2) and sections 672.851 and 672.86 to
  672.89 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the application of subsection (1), a reference
  to the attorney general of a province in which a hearing is held under
  subsection 672.5(3) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) shall be
  read as a reference to the Director of Military Prosecutions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to Attorney General

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a reference to
  the Attorney General in subsections 672.81(1.1) and (1.31) of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) shall be read as a reference to the
  Director of Military Prosecutions. **\[2014, c. 6, s. 28\]**

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to Attorney General

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a reference to
  the Attorney General of a province in which a hearing is held under
  subsection 672.5(3) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) and a
  reference to the Attorney General in subsections 672.81(1.1) and
  (1.31) of that Act shall be read as a reference to the Director of
  Military Prosecutions. **\[2014, c. 6, s. 32\]**

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of paragraph 672.121(a) of
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), the reference to
  subsection 672.851(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in
  paragraph 672.121(a) of that Act shall be read as a reference to
  subsection 202.121(1) of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 2005, c. 22, s. 58
- 2013, c. 24, s. 61
- [2014, c. 6, s. 28](#1034512-1355913){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034512-1355913.html"
  role="button"}
- [2014, c. 6, s. 32](#1034538-1355921){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034538-1355921.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202.25-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 202.25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of ss. 672.67 to 672.71 of
[Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) to findings

**[[202.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202.26 .sectionLabel}** Sections 672.67
to 672.71 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply, with any
modifications that the circumstances require, to findings made by courts
martial of unfit to stand trial or not responsible on account of mental
disorder, and a reference in any of those sections to a Review Board is
deemed to be a reference to the Review Board of the appropriate
province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 18
- 1998, c. 35, s. 54
- 2005, c. 22, s. 58
:::

[Previous
Version](section-202.26-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 202.26"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 7.1]{.HLabel2}[Sentencing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-377663 .Subheading}

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2019, c.
15, s. 65\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Purpose and Principles of Sentencing by Courts Martial]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377673 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fundamental purpose of sentencing

- **[[203.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The fundamental purpose of
  sentencing is to maintain the discipline, efficiency and morale of the
  Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The fundamental purpose of sentencing is to be
  achieved by imposing just punishments that have one or more of the
  following objectives:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to promote a habit of obedience to lawful commands
    and orders;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to maintain public trust in the Canadian Forces as
    a disciplined armed force;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to denounce unlawful conduct and the harm done to
    victims or to the community that is caused by unlawful conduct;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to deter offenders and other persons from
    committing offences;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to assist in rehabilitating offenders;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} to assist in reintegrating offenders into military
    service;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} to separate offenders, if necessary, from other
    officers or non-commissioned members or from society generally;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} to provide reparations for harm done to victims or
    to the community; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to promote a sense of responsibility in offenders
    and an acknowledgment of the harm done to victims or to the
    community.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355670){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355670.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.1-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fundamental principle of sentencing

**[[203.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.2 .sectionLabel}** A sentence must be
proportionate to the gravity of the offence and the degree of
responsibility of the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other sentencing principles

**[[203.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.3 .sectionLabel}** Sentences must be
imposed in accordance with the following other principles:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a sentence should be increased or reduced to account
  for any relevant aggravating or mitigating circumstances relating to
  the offence or the offender, and aggravating circumstances include,
  but are not restricted to, evidence establishing that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the offender, in committing the offence, abused
    their rank or other position of trust or authority,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the offence was motivated by bias, prejudice or
    hate based on race, national or ethnic origin, language, colour,
    religion, sex, age, mental or physical disability, sexual
    orientation, or gender identity or expression, or on any other
    similar factor,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the offender, in committing the offence, abused
    their spouse or common-law partner,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the offender, in committing the offence, abused a
    person under the age of 18 years,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence resulted in
    substantial harm to the conduct of a military operation,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the offence was committed in a theatre of
    hostilities,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the offence was committed for the benefit of, at
    the direction of or in association with a criminal organization, or

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the offence was a terrorism offence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a sentence should be similar to sentences imposed on
  similar offenders for similar offences committed in similar
  circumstances;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offender should not be deprived of liberty by
  imprisonment or detention if less restrictive punishments may be
  appropriate in the circumstances;

- [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} all available punishments, other than imprisonment
  and detention, that are reasonable in the circumstances and consistent
  with the harm done to victims or to the community should be considered
  for all offenders, with particular attention to the circumstances of
  Aboriginal offenders;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a sentence should be the least severe sentence
  required to maintain the discipline, efficiency and morale of the
  Canadian Forces; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any indirect consequences of the finding of guilty or
  the sentence should be taken into consideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149981-1175611){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149981-1175611.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.3-20190621.html "Link to previous version of section 203.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abuse of persons under age of 18

**[[203.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.4 .sectionLabel}** When a court
martial imposes a sentence for an offence that involved the abuse of a
person under the age of 18 years, it shall give primary consideration to
the objectives of denunciation and deterrence of such conduct.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355678){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355678.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.4-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.4"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Facts Relevant to the Determination of a Sentence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377717 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disputed facts

- **[[203.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a dispute with respect
  to any fact that is relevant to the determination of a sentence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court martial shall request that evidence be
    adduced as to the existence of the fact unless it is satisfied that
    sufficient evidence was adduced at trial;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (c), the court martial shall
    be satisfied on a balance of probabilities of the existence of the
    disputed fact before relying on it in determining the sentence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the prosecutor shall establish, by proof beyond a
    reasonable doubt, the existence of any aggravating fact or any
    previous conviction of the accused person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a General Court Martial, the court
  martial

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall accept as proven all facts, express or
    implied, that are essential to the court martial panel's finding of
    guilty; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may find any other relevant fact that was disclosed
    by evidence at the trial to be proven, or hear evidence presented by
    either party with respect to that fact.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
:::

#### [Victim Impact Statement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377730 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to consider victim impact statement

- **[[203.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of determining the
  sentence to be imposed on an offender or whether the offender should
  be discharged absolutely in respect of any offence, a court martial
  shall consider the statement of any victim of the offence describing
  the physical or emotional harm done to, or property damage or economic
  loss suffered by, the victim as a result of the commission of the
  offence and the impact of the offence on the victim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of statement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The victim's statement must be filed in accordance
  with regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation of statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Unless the court martial considers that it would not
  be in the best interests of the administration of military justice,
  the court martial shall, at the victim's request, permit the victim to
  present the statement by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reading it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reading it in the presence and close proximity of
    any support person of the victim's choice;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 203.7(4), reading it outside
    the courtroom or behind a screen or other device that would allow
    the victim not to see the offender; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} presenting it in any other manner that the court
    martial considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence concerning victim admissible

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Whether or not a statement has been prepared and
  filed in accordance with this section, the court martial may consider
  any other evidence concerning any victim of the offence for the
  purpose of determining the sentence to be imposed on the offender or
  determining whether the offender should be discharged absolutely.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355692){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355692.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.6-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry by court martial

- **[[203.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after a finding
  of guilt and in any event before imposing sentence, the court martial
  shall inquire of the prosecutor whether reasonable steps have been
  taken to provide the victim with an opportunity to prepare a statement
  referred to in subsection 203.6(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application of the prosecutor or a victim or on
  its own motion, the court martial may adjourn the proceedings to
  permit a victim to prepare a statement or to present evidence in
  accordance with subsection 203.6(4), if the court martial is satisfied
  that the adjournment would not interfere with the proper
  administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Photograph

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} During the presentation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the victim may have with them a photograph of
    themselves taken before the commission of the offence if it would
    not, in the opinion of the court martial, disrupt the proceedings;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the statement is presented by an individual
    acting on the victim's behalf, that individual may have with them a
    photograph of the victim taken before the commission of the offence
    if it would not, in the opinion of the court martial, disrupt the
    proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of exclusion

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The victim shall not present the statement outside
  the courtroom unless arrangements are made for the offender and the
  court martial to watch the presentation by means of closed-circuit
  television or otherwise and the offender is permitted to communicate
  with counsel while watching the presentation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of statement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In considering the statement, the court martial shall
  take into account the portions of the statement that it considers
  relevant to the determination referred to in subsection 203.6(1) and
  disregard any other portion.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355706){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355706.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.7-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.7"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Military Impact Statement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1355707 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military impact statement

- **[[203.71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.71
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When determining the sentence to be
  imposed on an offender or determining whether the offender should be
  discharged absolutely in respect of any service offence, the court
  martial shall consider any statement made on the behalf of the
  Canadian Forces describing the harm done to discipline, efficiency or
  morale as a result of the commission of the offence and the impact of
  the offence on discipline, efficiency or morale.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of statement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The statement must be prepared by an officer or
  non-commissioned member who is authorized to do so by regulations made
  by the Governor in Council and it must be filed in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation of statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, at the request of the person
  who made the statement, permit the person to present the statement by
  reading it or by presenting it in any other manner that the court
  martial considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of statement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, as soon as feasible after a
  finding of guilt, cause a copy of the statement to be provided to the
  prosecutor and to the offender or counsel for the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355718){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355718.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Community Impact Statement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1355719 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Community impact statement

- **[[203.72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.72
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When determining the sentence to be
  imposed on an offender or determining whether the offender should be
  discharged absolutely in respect of any service offence, the court
  martial shall consider any statement made by an individual on a
  community's behalf, describing the harm or loss suffered by the
  community as a result of the commission of the offence and the impact
  of the offence on the community.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of statement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The statement must be filed in accordance with
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presentation of statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, at the request of the
  individual who made the statement, permit the individual to present
  the statement by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reading it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reading it in the presence and close proximity of
    any support person of the individual's choice;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4), reading it outside the
    courtroom or behind a screen or other device that would allow the
    individual not to see the offender; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} presenting it in any other manner that the court
    martial considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of exclusion

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The individual making the statement shall not present
  it outside the courtroom unless arrangements are made for the offender
  and the court martial to watch the presentation by means of
  closed-circuit television or otherwise and the offender is permitted
  to communicate with counsel while watching the presentation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of statement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall, as soon as feasible after a
  finding of guilt, cause a copy of the statement to be provided to the
  prosecutor and to the offender or counsel for the offender.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355736){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355736.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Absolute Discharge]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377749 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absolute discharge

- **[[203.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an accused person pleads guilty
  to or is found guilty of an offence, other than an offence for which a
  minimum punishment is prescribed by law or an offence punishable by
  imprisonment for 14 years or for life, the court martial before which
  the accused appears may, if it considers it to be in the accused
  person's best interests and not contrary to the public interest,
  instead of convicting the accused person, direct that they be
  discharged absolutely.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of discharge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a court martial directs that an offender be
  discharged absolutely of an offence, the offender is deemed not to
  have been convicted of the offence, except that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they may appeal from the determination of guilt as
    if it were a conviction in respect of the offence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister may appeal from the decision not to
    convict the offender of the offence as if that decision were a
    finding of not guilty in respect of the offence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the offender may plead [*autrefois
    convict*]{.otherLang lang="fr"} in respect of any subsequent charge
    relating to the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to section 730 of [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A reference in any Act of Parliament to a discharge
  under section 730 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) is deemed to
  include an absolute discharge under subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355738){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355738.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.8-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.8"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Restitution]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court martial to consider restitution
order

- **[[203.81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A court martial that imposes a
  sentence on an offender or directs that an offender be discharged
  absolutely shall consider making a restitution order under section
  203.9.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry by court martial

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after a finding of guilt and in
  any event before imposing the sentence or directing that the offender
  be discharged absolutely, the court martial shall inquire of the
  prosecutor whether reasonable steps have been taken to provide the
  victims with an opportunity to indicate whether they are seeking
  restitution for their losses and damages, the amount of which must be
  readily ascertainable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjournment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On application of the prosecutor or on its own
  motion, the court martial may adjourn the proceedings to permit the
  victims to indicate whether they are seeking restitution or to
  establish their losses and damages, if the court martial is satisfied
  that the adjournment would not interfere with the proper
  administration of military justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Victims and other persons may indicate whether they
  are seeking restitution by completing a form prescribed in regulations
  made by the Governor in Council and by filing it in accordance with
  the procedures provided for in regulations made by the Governor in
  Council, and, if they are seeking restitution, shall establish, in the
  same manner, their losses and damages, the amount of which must be
  readily ascertainable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a victim seeks restitution and the court martial
  does not make a restitution order, it shall include in the court
  record a statement of its reasons for not doing so.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355753){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355753.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restitution order

**[[203.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.9 .sectionLabel}** A court martial
that imposes a sentence on an offender or directs that an offender be
discharged absolutely may, on application of the Director of Military
Prosecutions or on its own motion, in addition to any other measure
imposed on the offender, order that the offender make restitution to
another person as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of damage to, or the loss or destruction
  of, the property of any person as a result of the commission of the
  offence or the arrest or attempted arrest of the offender, by paying
  to the person an amount that is not more than the replacement value of
  the property as of the date the order is imposed, less the value of
  any part of the property that is returned to that person as of the
  date it is returned, if the amount is readily ascertainable;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of bodily or psychological harm to any
  person as a result of the commission of the offence or the arrest or
  attempted arrest of the offender, by paying to the person an amount
  that is not more than all pecuniary damages incurred as a result of
  the harm, including loss of income or support, if the amount is
  readily ascertainable; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of bodily harm or threat of bodily harm
  to a person --- who at the relevant time was the offender's spouse,
  common-law partner or child or any other member of the offender's
  household --- as a result of the commission of the offence or the
  arrest or attempted arrest of the offender, by paying to the person,
  independently of any amount ordered to be paid under paragraphs (a)
  and (b), an amount that is not more than the actual and reasonable
  expenses incurred by that person, as a result of moving out of the
  offender's household, for temporary housing, food, child care and
  transportation, if the amount is readily ascertainable.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ability to pay

**[[203.901]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.901 .sectionLabel}** The offender's
financial means or ability to pay does not prevent the court martial
from making an order under section 203.9.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355757){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355757.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment under order

**[[203.902]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.902 .sectionLabel}** In making an
order under section 203.9, the court martial shall require the offender
to pay the full amount specified in the order by the day specified in
the order, unless the court martial is of the opinion that the amount
should be paid in instalments, in which case the court martial shall set
out a periodic payment scheme in the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355761){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355761.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one person

**[[203.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.91 .sectionLabel}** An order under
section 203.9 may be made in respect of more than one person, in which
case the order must specify the amount that is payable to each person.
The order may also specify the order of priority in which those persons
are to be paid.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355764){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355764.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.91-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.91"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcing restitution order

- **[[203.92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.92
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An offender who fails to pay an
  amount that is ordered to be paid in a restitution order by the day
  specified in the order or who fails to make a periodic payment
  required under the order is in default of the order, and the person
  who was to be paid the amount or to whom the periodic payment was to
  be made, as the case may be, may, by filing the order, enter as a
  judgment any amount ordered to be paid that remains unpaid under the
  order in any civil court that has jurisdiction to enter a judgment for
  that amount, and that judgment is enforceable against the offender in
  the same manner as if it were a judgment rendered against the offender
  in that court in civil proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moneys found on offender

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} All or any part of an amount that is ordered to be
  paid in a restitution order may be taken out of moneys found in the
  possession of the offender at the time of their arrest if the court
  martial making the order, on being satisfied that ownership of or
  right to possession of those moneys is not disputed by claimants other
  than the offender, so directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150009-1355770){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355770.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.92-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.92"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of order

**[[203.93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.93 .sectionLabel}** A court martial
that makes a restitution order shall cause notice of the content of the
order, or a copy of the order, to be given to the person to whom the
restitution is ordered to be paid.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63(E)](#1150009-1355773){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150009-1355773.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-203.93-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 203.93"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil remedy not affected

**[[203.94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.94 .sectionLabel}** A civil remedy
for an act or omission is not affected by reason only that an order of
restitution has been made in respect of that act or omission.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
:::

#### [Passing of Sentence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377786 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Only one sentence to be passed

**[[203.95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203.95 .sectionLabel}** Only one sentence
shall be passed on an offender at a trial under the Code of Service
Discipline and, if the offender is convicted of more than one offence,
the sentence is good if any one of the offences would have justified it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 62
:::

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Provisions Applicable to Imprisonment and Detention]{.HTitleText2} {#h-377791 .Subheading}

#### [Computation of Term]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377792 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencement of term

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and
  148(1) and sections 215 to 217, the term of a punishment of
  imprisonment or detention shall commence on the day on which the court
  martial pronounces sentence on the offender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time counted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The only time that shall be reckoned toward the
  completion of a term of a punishment of imprisonment or detention
  shall be the time that the offender spends in civil custody or service
  custody while under the sentence in which that punishment is included.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special case

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a punishment referred to in subsection (2)
  cannot lawfully be carried out by reason of a vessel being at sea or
  in a port at which there is no suitable place of incarceration, the
  offender shall as soon as practicable, having regard to the exigencies
  of the service, be sent to a place where the punishment can lawfully
  be carried out, and the period of time prior to the date of arrival of
  the offender at that place shall not be reckoned toward the completion
  of the term of the punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 204
- 1998, c. 35, s. 57
- 2013, c. 24, s. 63
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355628){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355628.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150065-1355837){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150065-1355837.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Service Prisons and Detention Barracks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377802 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service prisons and detention barracks

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Such
  places as are designated by the Minister for the purpose shall be
  service prisons and detention barracks and any hospital or other place
  for the reception of sick persons to which a person who is a service
  convict, service prisoner or service detainee has been admitted shall,
  in so far as relates to that person, be deemed to be part of the place
  to which that person has been committed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective disciplinary measures for
  service prisons and detention barracks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The nature of and the manner of imposing corrective
  measures for breach of the regulations, orders and rules applicable in
  respect of service prisons and detention barracks by a person
  committed thereto as the result of a sentence passed on that person,
  and the terms and conditions of remission for good conduct of any part
  of a punishment involving incarceration, shall be as prescribed in
  regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Corrective measures referred to in subsection (2)
  shall not include whipping, paddling or any of the punishments
  referred to in paragraphs 139(1)(a) to (l) and shall not be so imposed
  as to increase the duration of any punishment involving a term of
  incarceration.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 177
:::

**[[206 to 214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206_to_214
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 59\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Suspension of Imprisonment or Detention]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377813 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of execution of punishment

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an offender is sentenced to imprisonment or detention, the execution
  of the punishment may be suspended by the court martial that imposes
  the punishment or, if the offender's sentence is affirmed, is
  substituted or is imposed on appeal, by the Court Martial Appeal
  Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of victim's safety and
  security

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial or the Court Martial Appeal
  Court, as the case may be, makes a decision that the execution of the
  punishment be suspended, it shall include in the decision a statement
  that it has considered the safety and security of every victim of the
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to victim

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The court martial or the Court Martial Appeal
  Court, as the case may be, shall, on request by a victim of the
  offence, cause a copy of the decision to be given to the victim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In suspending the execution of a punishment, the
  court martial or the Court Martial Appeal Court, as the case may be,
  shall impose the following conditions on the offender:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to keep the peace and be of good behaviour;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to attend any hearing under section 215.2 when
    ordered to do so by the appropriate person referred to in paragraph
    215.2(1)(a) or (b); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an offender who is not an officer or
    a non-commissioned member, to notify the Provost Marshal in advance
    of any change of name or address, and to promptly notify the Provost
    Marshal of any change of employment or occupation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial or the Court Martial Appeal Court
  may, in addition to the conditions described in subsection (2), impose
  any reasonable conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 215
- 1998, c. 35, s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 64
- [2019, c. 15, s. 34](#1149831-1355516){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149831-1355516.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150044-1355811){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150044-1355811.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 215"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Varying conditions

**[[215.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215.1 .sectionLabel}** On application by
an offender, a condition imposed under subsection 215(3) or varied,
added or substituted under this section or section 215.2 may be varied,
or another condition may be substituted for that condition, by
