- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a military judge, in the case of a condition imposed,
  varied, added or substituted by a court martial; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court, in the
  case of a condition imposed, varied, added or substituted by that
  Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 64
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150044-1355825){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150044-1355825.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215.1-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 215.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing into breach of conditions

- **[[215.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On application by a representative
  of the Canadian Forces who is a member of a class designated for that
  purpose by regulations made by the Governor in Council, a
  determination of whether an offender has breached a condition imposed
  under section 215 or varied, added or substituted under section 215.1
  or this section may be made by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a military judge, in the case of a condition
    imposed, varied, added or substituted by a court martial; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court, in the
    case of a condition imposed, varied, added or substituted by that
    Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of suspension or changes to
  conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b)
  determines, after giving the offender and the applicant an opportunity
  to make representations, that the offender has breached a condition,
  the person may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} revoke the suspension of a punishment and commit
    the offender or, if the person is not empowered to commit the
    offender, direct an authority so empowered to do so; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vary any conditions imposed under subsection 215(3)
    or varied, added or substituted under section 215.1 or this section,
    or add or substitute other conditions, as the person sees fit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 64
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150044-1355835){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150044-1355835.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-215.2-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 215.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-appearance of accused person

**[[215.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215.3 .sectionLabel}** A person who orders
an offender to attend for a hearing under section 215.2 may, if the
offender fails to attend as ordered, issue a warrant for the offender's
arrest in the form prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in
Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 64
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[suspending
authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section and section 217, [[suspending
  authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any authority prescribed to be a
  suspending authority in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of imprisonment or detention

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A suspending authority may suspend a punishment of
  imprisonment or detention, whether or not the offender has already
  been committed to undergo that punishment, if there are imperative
  reasons relating to military operations or the offender's welfare.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A suspending authority that suspends a punishment
  shall provide written reasons for the suspension to any person
  prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committal after suspension

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A suspending authority may --- if the reasons
  described in subsection (2) no longer apply or if the offender's
  conduct is inconsistent with the reasons for which the punishment was
  suspended --- revoke the suspension of a punishment and commit the
  offender or, if the person is not empowered to commit the offender,
  direct an authority so empowered to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committing authority may postpone
  committal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an offender has been sentenced to imprisonment
  or detention and suspension of the punishment has been recommended,
  the authority empowered to commit the offender to a penitentiary,
  civil prison, service prison or detention barrack, as the case may be,
  may postpone committal until directions of a suspending authority have
  been obtained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory suspension of detention

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A suspending authority shall suspend a punishment of
  detention in the circumstances prescribed by the Governor in Council
  in regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 216
- 1998, c. 35, s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 65
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149993-1355660){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149993-1355660.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of suspension before committal

- **[[216.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a punishment is suspended
  before committal to undergo the punishment, the offender shall, if in
  custody, be discharged from custody and the term of the punishment
  shall not commence until the offender has been ordered to be committed
  to undergo that punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of suspension after committal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a punishment is suspended after committal to
  undergo the punishment, the offender shall be discharged from the
  place in which the offender is incarcerated and the currency of the
  punishment shall be arrested after the day of that discharge until the
  offender is again ordered to be committed to undergo that punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review and remission

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  punishment has been suspended, it may at any time, and shall at
  intervals of not more than three months, be reviewed by a suspending
  authority. The suspending authority may, at the time of the review and
  in accordance with regulations made by the Governor in Council, remit
  the punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic remission of punishments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A punishment, except a punishment referred to in
  subsection (3), that has been suspended shall be deemed to be wholly
  remitted on the expiration of a period, commencing on the day the
  suspension was ordered, equal to the term of the punishment less any
  time during which the offender has been incarcerated following
  pronouncement of the sentence, unless the punishment has been put into
  execution prior to the expiration of that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic remission of detention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A punishment of detention that has been suspended is
  deemed to be wholly remitted on the expiration of one year commencing
  on the day the suspension was ordered, unless the punishment has been
  put into execution prior to the expiration of that period.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 217
- 1998, c. 35, s. 61
- 2013, c. 24, s. 66
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2013, c.
24, s. 67\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-218-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 218"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Committal to Imprisonment or Detention]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377883 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committing authority

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may prescribe or appoint authorities for the purposes of this
  section and section 220 and, in this section and section 220, an
  authority prescribed or appointed under this subsection is referred to
  as a [[committing authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrants for committal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A committal order, in such form as is prescribed in
  regulations, made by a committing authority is a sufficient warrant
  for the committal of a service convict, service prisoner or service
  detainee to any lawful place of confinement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority for transfer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A committing authority may, by warrant, order that a
  service convict, service prisoner or service detainee be transferred,
  from the place to which that convict, prisoner or detainee has been
  committed to undergo punishment, to any other place in which that
  punishment may lawfully be put into execution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Custody pending delivery on committal
  and during transfer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A service convict, service prisoner or service
  detainee, until delivered to the place where that convict, prisoner or
  detainee is to undergo punishment or while being transferred from one
  such place to another such place, may be held in any place, either in
  service custody or in civil custody, or at one time in service custody
  and at another time in civil custody, as occasion may require, and may
  be transferred from place to place by any mode of conveyance, under
  such restraint as is necessary for the safe conduct of that convict,
  prisoner or detainee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 187
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committal of service convicts

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  service convict whose punishment of imprisonment for life or for two
  years or more is to be put into execution shall as soon as practicable
  be committed to a penitentiary to undergo punishment according to law,
  except that a committing authority may, in accordance with regulations
  made by the Governor in Council, order that a service convict be
  committed to a service prison to undergo the punishment or any part of
  the punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committal when unexpired term less than
  two years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a committing authority orders the committal to
  a penitentiary of a service convict, part of whose punishment has been
  undergone in a service prison, the service convict may be so committed
  notwithstanding that the unexpired portion of the term of that
  punishment is less than two years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committal of service prisoners

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A service prisoner whose punishment of imprisonment
  for less than two years is to be put into execution shall as soon as
  practicable be committed to a civil prison to undergo punishment
  according to law, except that a committing authority may, in
  accordance with regulations made by the Governor in Council, order
  that a service prisoner be committed to a service prison or detention
  barrack to undergo the punishment or part thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Committal of service detainees

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A service detainee whose punishment of detention is
  to be put into execution shall as soon as practicable be committed to
  a detention barrack to undergo the punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 220
- 1998, c. 35, s. 62
:::

#### [Temporary Removal from Incarceration]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377906 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority for temporary removal

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** Where the exigencies of
the service so require, a service convict, service prisoner or service
detainee may, by an order made by a committing authority referred to in
section 219 or 220, be removed temporarily from the place to which he
has been committed for such period as may be specified in that order
but, until returned to that place, any person removed pursuant to this
section shall be retained in service custody or civil custody, as
occasion may require, and no further committal order is necessary on the
return of the person to that place.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 188
:::

#### [Rules Applicable to Service Convicts and Service Prisoners]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377911 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of penitentiaries and civil prisons
to apply

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  service convict, while undergoing punishment in a penitentiary, or a
  service prisoner, while undergoing punishment in a civil prison, shall
  be dealt with in the same manner as other prisoners in the place where
  that convict or prisoner is undergoing punishment, and all rules
  applicable in respect of a person sentenced by a civil court to
  imprisonment in a penitentiary or civil prison, as the case may be, in
  so far as circumstances permit, apply accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction and discretion of Parole
  Board of Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the punishment of a service convict undergoing
  punishment in a penitentiary or of a service prisoner undergoing
  punishment in a civil prison is not suspended under this Act within
  six months after the date of the committal of that convict or prisoner
  to that penitentiary or civil prison, the Parole Board of Canada has,
  subject to Part II of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), exclusive jurisdiction and absolute discretion
  to grant, refuse to grant, or revoke the parole of that convict or
  prisoner.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 222
- 1992, c. 20, s. 215
- 1998, c. 35, s. 63
- 2012, c. 1, s. 160
- [2019, c. 15, s. 35](#1149835-1355519){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149835-1355519.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20130228.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Validity of Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377919 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Legalization and rectification

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** The custody of a
service convict, service prisoner or service detainee is not illegal by
reason only of informality or error in or in respect of a document
containing a warrant, order or direction issued in pursuance of this
Act, or by reason only that the document deviates from the prescribed
form, and any such document may be amended appropriately at any time by
the authority that issued it in the first instance or by any other
authority empowered to issue documents of the same nature.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 190
:::

#### [Mental Disorder during Imprisonment or Detention]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377924 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons in penitentiaries or civil
prisons

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** A service convict or
service prisoner who, having been released from the Canadian Forces, is
suffering from a mental disorder while undergoing punishment in a
penitentiary or civil prison shall be treated in the same manner as if
the convict or prisoner were a person undergoing a term of imprisonment
in the penitentiary or civil prison by virtue of the sentence of a civil
court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 224
- 1991, c. 43, s. 20
:::

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1991, c.
43, s. 20\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Transfer of Offenders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377930 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of offenders

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has been found guilty of an offence by a civil court in
  Canada or by a civil or military tribunal of any country other than
  Canada and sentenced to a term of incarceration may, with the approval
  of the Chief of the Defence Staff or an officer designated by the
  Chief of the Defence Staff, be transferred to the custody of the
  appropriate civil or military authorities of Canada for incarceration
  under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imprisonment or detention of offenders
  transferred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person transferred under subsection (1) may, in
  lieu of the incarceration to which that person was sentenced, be
  imprisoned or detained for the term or the remainder of the term of
  incarceration to which he was sentenced as though that person had been
  sentenced to that term by a court martial, and the provisions of this
  Division are applicable in respect of every person so transferred as
  though the person had been so sentenced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been found guilty of an offence by a
  civil court in Canada shall not,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if sentenced by the civil court to a term of less
    than two years, be transferred under subsection (1) without the
    consent of the attorney general of the province in which that person
    is incarcerated; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if sentenced by the civil court to imprisonment for
    life or a term of two years or more, be transferred under
    subsection (1) without the consent of the Attorney General of
    Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 226
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 64, 92
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355631){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355631.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-226-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 226"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Parole Eligibility]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377942 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence of imprisonment for life

- **[[226.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A court martial that imposes a
  punishment of imprisonment for life shall pronounce the following
  sentence:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has been convicted of
    having committed traitorously an offence of misconduct in the
    presence of an enemy (section 73 or 74), an offence related to
    security (section 75) or an offence in relation to prisoners of war
    (section 76), imprisonment for life without eligibility for parole
    until the person has served 25 years of the sentence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has been convicted of
    an offence of high treason or an offence of first degree murder,
    imprisonment for life without eligibility for parole until the
    person has served 25 years of the sentence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has been convicted of
    an offence of second degree murder and has previously been convicted
    of culpable homicide that is murder, imprisonment for life without
    eligibility for parole until the person has served 25 years of the
    sentence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has been convicted of
    an offence of second degree murder, imprisonment for life without
    eligibility for parole until the person has served at least 10 years
    of the sentence or any greater number of years, not being more than
    25, that has been substituted under subsection (2); or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person who has been convicted of
    any other offence, imprisonment for life with normal eligibility for
    parole.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions of [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 745.1 to 746.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require, to a sentence of imprisonment for life that is
  imposed under this Act, and for that purpose

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in sections 745.2 to 745.3 of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) to a jury shall be read as a
    reference to the panel of a General Court Martial; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a conviction that took place outside
    Canada, a reference in section 745.6 of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) to the province in which a conviction took
    place shall be read as a reference to the province in which the
    offender is incarcerated when they make an application under that
    section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 68, 132
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court martial to delay parole

- **[[226.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 120 of the
  [Corrections and Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), if a
  person receives a sentence of imprisonment for life that is imposed
  otherwise than as a minimum punishment or a sentence of imprisonment
  for two years or more on conviction for an offence set out in Schedule
  I or II to that Act that is punishable under section 130 of this Act,
  a court martial may order that the portion of the sentence that must
  be served before the person may be released on full parole is one half
  of the sentence or 10 years, whichever is less.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court martial may only make the order if it is
  satisfied, having regard to the circumstances of the commission of the
  offence and the person's character and circumstances, that the
  expression of society's denunciation of the offence or the objective
  of specific or general deterrence requires that the order be made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criminal organization offences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 120 of the [Corrections and
  Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), if a person receives a
  sentence of imprisonment for life that is imposed otherwise than as a
  minimum punishment or a sentence of imprisonment for two years or more
  on conviction under this Act for a criminal organization offence, the
  court martial may order that the portion of the sentence that must be
  served before the person may be released on full parole is one half of
  the sentence or 10 years, whichever is less.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court martial to delay parole

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 120 of the [Corrections and
  Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), if a person receives a
  sentence of imprisonment of two years or more, including a sentence of
  imprisonment for life, on conviction under this Act for a terrorism
  offence, the court martial shall order that the portion of the
  sentence that must be served before the person may be released on full
  parole is one half of the sentence or 10 years, whichever is less,
  unless the court martial is satisfied, having regard to the
  circumstances of the commission of the offence and the person's
  character and circumstances, that the expression of society's
  denunciation of the offence and the objectives of specific or general
  deterrence would be adequately served by a period of parole
  ineligibility determined in accordance with the [Corrections and
  Conditional Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The paramount objectives that are to guide the court
  martial under this section are denunciation and specific or general
  deterrence, with the rehabilitation of the person, in all cases, being
  subordinate to those paramount objectives.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 68
:::

### [DIVISION 8.1]{.HLabel2}[Sex Offender Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-377970 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377971 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[crime of a sexual nature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[crime of a sexual nature]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a crime
    referred to in subsection 3(2) of the [Sex Offender Information
    Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7). ([crime de nature
    sexuelle]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[database]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[database]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 3(1) of the [Sex Offender Information Registration
    Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7). ([banque de données]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence within the meaning of paragraph (a),
      (c), (c.1), (d) or (d.1) of the definition [[designated
      offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 490.011(1) of the
      [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is punishable under section
      130 of this Act;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence within the meaning of paragraph (b) of
      the definition [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
      subsection 490.011(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that
      is punishable under section 130 of this Act;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an attempt or conspiracy to commit an offence
      referred to in paragraph (a); or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an attempt or conspiracy to commit an offence
      referred to in paragraph (b). ([infraction
      désignée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[finding of not responsible on account of mental disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[finding of not responsible on account of mental
    disorder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a verdict of not criminally
    responsible on account of mental disorder within the meaning of
    subsection 672.1(1) of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([verdict de non-responsabilité pour cause de
    troubles mentaux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary reserve]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary
    reserve]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an officer, or non-commissioned
    member, of the reserve force

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} who is required, whether on active service or
      not, to perform military or any other form of duty or training;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whose primary duty is not the supervision,
      administration and training of cadet organizations referred to in
      section 46; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} who is required to undergo annual
      training. ([officier ou militaire du rang de la première
      réserve]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pardon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pardon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a conditional pardon granted
    under Her Majesty's royal prerogative of mercy or under section 748
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that has not been
    revoked. ([pardon]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[prescribed form]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[prescribed form]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a form prescribed in
    the regulations made by the Governor in Council. ([formulaire
    réglementaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[record suspension]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[record suspension]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a record suspension,
    as defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Criminal Records
    Act](/eng/acts/C-47), that has not been revoked or ceased to have
    effect. ([suspension du casier]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[registration centre]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registration centre]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as
    in subsection 3(1) of the [Sex Offender Information Registration
    Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7). ([bureau d'inscription]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Review Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Review Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Review Board
    established or designated for a province under subsection 672.38(1)
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([commission
    d'examen]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 227
- 1998, c. 35, s. 65
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2012, c. 1, s. 153
- 2014, c. 25, s. 37
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227-20120313.html "Link to previous version of section 227"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Order to Comply with the [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7)]{.HTitleText3} {#h-377993 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[227.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial imposes a
  sentence on a person for an offence referred to in paragraph (a)
  or (c) of the definition [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  in section 227 or finds the person not responsible on account of
  mental disorder for such an offence, it shall make an order in the
  prescribed form requiring the person to comply with the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) for the applicable
  period specified in section 227.02.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- if intent established

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial imposes a sentence on a person
  for an offence referred to in paragraph (b) or (d) of the definition
  [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227, it shall,
  on application of the prosecutor, make an order in the prescribed form
  requiring the person to comply with the [Sex Offender Information
  Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) for the applicable period specified
  in section 227.02 if the prosecutor establishes beyond a reasonable
  doubt that the person committed the offence with the intent to commit
  an offence referred to in paragraph (a) or (c) of that definition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order --- if previous offence
  established

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial imposes a sentence on a person
  for a designated offence in connection with which an order may be made
  under subsection (1) or (2) or finds the person not responsible on
  account of mental disorder for such an offence, it shall, on
  application of the prosecutor, make an order in the prescribed form
  requiring the person to comply with the [Sex Offender Information
  Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) for the applicable period specified
  in section 227.02 if the prosecutor establishes that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person was, before or after the coming into
    force of this paragraph, previously convicted of, or found not
    responsible on account of mental disorder for, an offence referred
    to in paragraph (a) or (c) of the definition [[designated
    offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227 of this Act or in
    paragraph (a), (c), (c.1), (d), (d.1) or (e) of the definition
    [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection
    490.011(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person was not served with a notice under
    section 227.08 of this Act or section 490.021 or 490.02903 of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in connection with that offence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} no order was made under subsection (1) or under
    subsection 490.012(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in
    connection with that offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to make order

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial does not consider the matter
  under subsection (1) or (3) at that time,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Military Judge shall cause the Court
    Martial Administrator to convene a Standing Court Martial to do so;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Court Martial Administrator shall, within 90
    days after the day on which the sentence was imposed or the person
    was found not responsible on account of mental disorder, convene the
    court martial; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for greater certainty, the person continues to be
    liable to be dealt with under the Code of Service Discipline for
    that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (3)(a), a previous
  conviction includes a conviction for an offence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for which a person is given an adult sentence
    within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the [Youth Criminal Justice
    Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is made in ordinary court within the meaning
    of subsection 2(1) of the [Young Offenders Act](/eng/acts/Y-1),
    chapter Y-1 of the Revised Statutes of Canada, 1985.

- [(5) and (6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2010, c. 17, s. 47\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 47
- 2014, c. 25, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.01-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date order begins

- **[[227.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An order made under section 227.01
  begins on the day on which it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection 227.01(1) or (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ends 10 years after it was made if the maximum term
    of imprisonment for the offence in connection with which it was made
    is five years or less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ends 20 years after it was made if the maximum term
    of imprisonment for the offence is 10 or 14 years; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} applies for life if the maximum term of
    imprisonment for the offence is life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection 227.01(1) applies
  for life if the person is convicted of, or found not responsible on
  account of mental disorder for, more than one offence referred to in
  paragraph (a) or (c) of the definition [[designated
  offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection 227.01(1) or (2)
  applies for life if the person is, or was at any time, subject to an
  obligation under section 227.06 of this Act, section 490.019 or
  490.02901 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 36.1 of
  the [International Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection 227.01(1) or (2)
  applies for life if the person is, or was at any time, subject to an
  order made previously under section 227.01 of this Act or section
  490.012 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection 227.01(3) applies for
  life.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 48
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.02-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.02"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for termination order

- **[[227.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is subject to an order
  may apply for a termination order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if five years have elapsed since the order was
    made, in the case of an order referred to in paragraph 227.02(2)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if 10 years have elapsed since the order was made,
    in the case of an order referred to in paragraph 227.02(2)(b); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if 20 years have elapsed since the order was made,
    in the case of an order referred to in paragraph 227.02(2)(c) or
    subsection 227.02(2.1), (3) or (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple orders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is subject to more than one order made
  under section 227.01 may apply for a termination order if 20 years
  have elapsed since the most recent order was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pardon or record suspension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2), a person may apply
  for a termination order once they receive a pardon or once a record
  suspension is ordered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be in relation to every order
  that is in effect. If a person is subject to an obligation under
  section 227.06 of this Act, section 490.019 or 490.02901 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 36.1 of the [International
  Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6), the application shall
  also be in relation to that obligation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person whose application is refused may re-apply if
  five years have elapsed since they made the previous application. They
  may also re-apply once they receive a pardon or once a record
  suspension is ordered. However, they may not re-apply under this
  subsection if an order is made with respect to them under section
  227.01 of this Act or section 490.012 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) after the previous application was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be made to the Chief Military
  Judge if the applicant is subject to the Code of Service Discipline or
  is an officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary reserve at
  the time. In any other case, the application shall be made to a court
  under section 490.015 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court martial

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the application, the Chief Military
  Judge shall cause the Court Martial Administrator to convene a
  Standing Court Martial to try the issue.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2008, c. 29, s. 20
- 2010, c. 17, s. 49
- 2012, c. 1, s. 154
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.03-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.03"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination order

- **[[227.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall make a
  termination order if it is satisfied that the person has established
  that the impact on them of continuing an order or an obligation,
  including on their privacy or liberty, would be grossly
  disproportionate to the public interest in protecting society through
  the effective prevention or investigation of crimes of a sexual
  nature, to be achieved by the registration of information relating to
  sex offenders under the [Sex Offender Information Registration
  Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall give reasons for the
  decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Provost Marshal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial makes a termination order, it
  shall cause the Provost Marshal to be notified of the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 50
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.04-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.04"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

- **[[227.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When a court martial makes an order
  under section 227.01, it shall cause

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order to be read by or to the person who is
    subject to it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the order to be given to that person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that person to be informed of sections 4 to 7.1 of
    the [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7),
    section 119.1 of this Act and sections 490.031 and 490.0311 of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the order to be sent to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the Review Board that is responsible for making a
      disposition with respect to that person, if applicable,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person in charge of the place in which the
      person who is subject to the order is to serve the custodial
      portion of a sentence or is to be detained in custody as part of a
      disposition under Division 7 of this Part, if applicable, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the Provost Marshal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice on disposition by Review Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Review Board shall cause a copy of the order to be
  given to the person who is subject to the order when it directs,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the exercise of the powers and performance of
    the duties assigned to it under paragraph 672.54(a) of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), that the person be discharged absolutely; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the exercise of the powers and performance of
    the duties assigned to it under paragraph 672.54(b) of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46), that the person be discharged subject to
    conditions, unless the conditions restrict the person's liberty in a
    manner and to an extent that prevent them from complying with
    sections 4, 4.1, 4.3 and 6 of the [Sex Offender Information
    Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice before release

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person in charge of the place in which the person
  who is subject to the order is serving the custodial portion of a
  sentence, or is detained in custody, before their release or discharge
  shall give them a copy of the order not earlier than 10 days before
  their release or discharge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.05-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.05"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Notice and Obligation to Comply with the [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) --- Convictions Before September 12, 2008]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378081 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to comply

**[[227.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.06 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
served with a notice in the prescribed form shall comply with the [Sex
Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) for the
applicable period specified in section 227.09 of this Act unless an
exemption order is made under subsection 227.1(4) of this Act or
subsection 490.023(2) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons who may be served

- **[[227.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal may serve a
  person with a notice if, on the day on which this section comes into
  force, they are subject to a sentence for an offence referred to in
  paragraph (a) or (c) of the definition [[designated
  offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227 or have not been
  discharged absolutely or unconditionally released from custody under
  Division 7 of this Part in connection with such an offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice shall not be served on a person if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they may be served with a notice under section
    490.021 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have been finally acquitted of, or have
    received a free pardon granted under Her Majesty's royal prerogative
    of mercy or under section 748 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
    for, every offence in connection with which the notice may be served
    on them; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an application has been made for an order under
    subsection 227.01(3) of this Act or subsection 490.012(3) of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in relation to any offence in
    connection with which the notice may be served on them.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.07-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.07"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for and method of service

- **[[227.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.08
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The notice shall be personally
  served within one year after the day on which section 227.07 comes
  into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person is unlawfully at large or is in breach of
  any terms of their sentence or their discharge or release under
  Division 7 of this Part, or of any conditions set under this Part,
  that relate to residence, the notice may be served by registered mail
  at their last known address.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An affidavit of the person who served the notice,
  sworn before a commissioner or other person authorized to take
  affidavits, is evidence of the service and the notice if it sets out
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who served the notice has charge of the
    appropriate records and has knowledge of the facts in the particular
    case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the notice was personally served on, or mailed to,
    the person to whom it was directed on a named day; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person who served the notice identifies a true
    copy of the notice as an exhibit attached to the affidavit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person who served the notice shall send a copy of
  the affidavit and the notice to the Provost Marshal without delay.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.08-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.08"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date obligation begins

- **[[227.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.09
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The obligation under section 227.06
  begins

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} either one year after the day on which the person
    is served with the notice, or when an exemption order is refused
    under subsection 227.1(4) of this Act or subsection 490.023(2) of
    the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), whichever is later; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when an exemption order is quashed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date obligation ends

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The obligation ends when an exemption order is made
  on an appeal from a decision made under subsection 227.1(4) of this
  Act or subsection 490.023(2) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of obligation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (2) does not apply earlier, the
  obligation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ends 10 years after the person was sentenced, or
    found not responsible on account of mental disorder, for the offence
    listed in the notice if the maximum term of imprisonment for the
    offence is five years or less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ends 20 years after the person was sentenced, or
    found not responsible on account of mental disorder, for the offence
    listed in the notice if the maximum term of imprisonment for the
    offence is 10 or 14 years;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} applies for life if the maximum term of
    imprisonment for the offence listed in the notice is life; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} applies for life if, at any time, the person was
    convicted of, or found not responsible on account of mental disorder
    for, more than one offence that is referred to in paragraph (a)
    or (c) of the definition [[designated
    offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227 of this Act or in
    paragraph (a), (c), (c.1), (d), (d.1) or (e) of the definition
    [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection
    490.011(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) and if more than
    one of those offences is listed in the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2014, c. 25, s. 39
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.09-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.09"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for exemption order

- **[[227.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is not subject to an
  order under section 227.01 of this Act or section 490.012 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) may apply for an order exempting them
  from the obligation within one year after they are served with a
  notice under section 227.08.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be made to the Chief Military
  Judge if the applicant is subject to the Code of Service Discipline or
  is an officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary reserve at
  the time. In any other case, the application shall be made to a court
  under section 490.023 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court martial

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the application, the Chief Military
  Judge shall cause the Court Martial Administrator to convene a
  Standing Court Martial to try the issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall make an exemption order if it
  is satisfied that the person has established that the impact of the
  obligation on them, including on their privacy or liberty, would be
  grossly disproportionate to the public interest in protecting society
  through the effective prevention or investigation of crimes of a
  sexual nature, to be achieved by the registration of information
  relating to sex offenders under the [Sex Offender Information
  Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall give reasons for the
  decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of information from database

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial makes an exemption order, it
  shall also make an order requiring the Royal Canadian Mounted Police
  to permanently remove from the database all information that relates
  to the person that was registered in the database on receipt of the
  notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2008, c. 29, s. 21
- 2010, c. 17, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.1-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

**[[227.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.11 .sectionLabel}** If the court
martial refuses to make an exemption order or if the Court Martial
Appeal Court dismisses an appeal from such a decision or quashes an
exemption order, it shall cause the Provost Marshal to be notified of
the decision and shall cause the person who applied for the order to be
informed of sections 4 to 7.1 of the [Sex Offender Information
Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7), section 119.1 of this Act and
sections 490.031 and 490.0311 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.11-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for termination order

- **[[227.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is subject to an
  obligation under section 227.06 may apply for a termination order
  unless they are also subject to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an obligation under section 490.019 or 490.02901 of
    the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 36.1 of the
    [International Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6) that
    began later; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order under section 227.01 of this Act or
    section 490.012 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that began
    later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person may apply for a termination order if the
  following period has elapsed since they were sentenced, or found not
  responsible on account of mental disorder, for an offence referred to
  in paragraph (a) or (c) of the definition [[designated
  offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} five years if the maximum term of imprisonment for
    the offence is five years or less;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 10 years if the maximum term of imprisonment for
    the offence is 10 or 14 years; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 20 years if the maximum term of imprisonment for
    the offence is life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If more than one offence is listed in the notice
  served under section 227.08, the person may apply for a termination
  order if 20 years have elapsed since they were sentenced, or found not
  responsible on account of mental disorder, for the most recent offence
  referred to in paragraph (a) or (c) of the definition [[designated
  offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227 of this Act or in
  paragraph (a), (c), (c.1), (d), (d.1) or (e) of the definition
  [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 490.011(1)
  of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pardon or record suspension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (2) and (3), a person may apply
  for a termination order once they receive a pardon or once a record
  suspension is ordered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person whose application is refused may apply again
  if five years have elapsed since they made the previous application.
  They may also apply again once they receive a pardon or once a record
  suspension is ordered. However, they may not apply again if, after the
  previous application was made, they become subject to an obligation
  under section 490.019 or 490.02901 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or under section 36.1 of the [International
  Transfer of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6) or to an order under
  section 227.01 of this Act or section 490.012 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The application shall be made to the Chief Military
  Judge if the applicant is subject to the Code of Service Discipline or
  is an officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary reserve at
  the time. In any other case, the application shall be made to a court
  under section 490.026 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court martial

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the application, the Chief Military
  Judge shall cause the Court Martial Administrator to convene a
  Standing Court Martial to try the issue.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2008, c. 29, s. 22
- 2010, c. 17, s. 53
- 2012, c. 1, s. 155
- 2014, c. 25, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.12-20120313.html "Link to previous version of section 227.12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination order

- **[[227.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall make an
  order terminating the obligation if it is satisfied that the person
  has established that the impact on them of continuing the obligation,
  including on their privacy or liberty, would be grossly
  disproportionate to the public interest in protecting society through
  the effective prevention or investigation of crimes of a sexual
  nature, to be achieved by the registration of information relating to
  sex offenders under the [Sex Offender Information Registration
  Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court martial shall give reasons for the
  decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial makes a termination order, it
  shall cause the Provost Marshal to be notified of the decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 54
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.13-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed application

**[[227.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.14 .sectionLabel}** If a person is
eligible to apply for both an exemption order under section 227.1 and a
termination order under section 227.12 within one year after they are
served with a notice under section 227.08, an application within that
period for one order is deemed to be an application for both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
:::

#### [Suspension of Time Limits, Proceedings and Obligations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378179 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination --- inability to act for
operational reasons

- **[[227.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may
  determine that a person who is subject to the Code of Service
  Discipline, or who is an officer, or non-commissioned member, of the
  primary reserve, is, for operational reasons, unable to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} apply for an exemption order under section 227.1 of
    this Act or section 490.023 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
    within the required period;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} appeal the legality of a decision made under
    subsection 227.01(2), 227.04(1), 227.1(4) or 227.13(1) of this Act
    --- or appeal a decision made under subsection 490.012(2),
    490.016(1), 490.023(2), 490.027(1), 490.02905(2), 490.02909(1) or
    490.02913(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) --- within the
    required period;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} participate in a proceeding relating to an
    exemption order referred to in paragraph (a) or in an appeal
    proceeding referred to in paragraph (b); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} comply with section 4, 4.1, 4.3 or 6 of the [Sex
    Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) within the
    required period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of determination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Chief of the Defence Staff makes a
  determination, the following rules apply:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination under paragraph
    (1)(a), the running of the period during which the person may apply
    for an exemption order is suspended from the day on which the
    operational reasons first apply until 45 days after the day on which
    they cease to apply;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination under paragraph
    (1)(b), the running of the period during which the person may appeal
    the legality of a decision, or a decision, is suspended from the day
    on which the operational reasons first apply until 45 days after the
    day on which they cease to apply;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination under paragraph
    (1)(c),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any proceeding relating to an exemption order is
      suspended from the day on which the operational reasons first
      apply until 45 days after the day on which they cease to apply, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an application may be made to appeal the
      legality of a decision, or a decision, after the day on which the
      operational reasons first apply, but any appeal proceeding is
      suspended from the day on which the operational reasons first
      apply until 45 days after the day on which they cease to apply;
      and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a determination under paragraph
    (1)(d), the person's obligation to comply with the relevant section
    of the [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7)
    is suspended from the day on which the operational reasons first
    apply until 15 days after the day on which they cease to apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors for consideration

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may make a
  determination only if he or she is of the opinion that the operational
  reasons clearly outweigh in importance the public interest in applying
  the provisions of the Act that, but for the determination, would apply
  in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Minister

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall notify the
  Minister before making a determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of operational reasons

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} Every 15 days after a determination is made, the
  Chief of the Defence Staff shall consider whether the operational
  reasons cease to apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall notify the
  Minister without delay that a determination has been made under this
  section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall notify the
  Provost Marshal without delay of the determination, the date on which
  the operational reasons first apply and the date on which they cease
  to apply, and the Provost Marshal shall notify the person without
  delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal shall notify the following
  persons without delay of a determination made under paragraph (1)(b)
  or (c), the effect of the determination, the date on which the
  suspension of the time limit or proceeding first applies and the date
  on which it ceases to apply:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister or counsel instructed by the Minister
    if the decision in relation to which an appeal may be brought was
    made under this Act, or the Minister or counsel instructed by the
    Minister and the Court Martial Administrator if the proceeding was
    commenced under this Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the attorney general of a province or the minister
    of justice of a territory if the decision in relation to which an
    appeal may be brought was made, or the proceeding was commenced, in
    that jurisdiction under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 55
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.15-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination --- information relating to
an operation

- **[[227.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff may
  determine that the communication, under section 6 of the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7), of information that
  relates to an operation could jeopardize national security,
  international relations or the security of an operation that is within
  a class of operations designated by a regulation made under paragraph
  227.2(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall notify the
  Minister without delay that a determination has been made under this
  section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff shall notify the
  Provost Marshal without delay of the determination, and the Provost
  Marshal shall notify without delay any participant in the operation
  who is required to comply with section 6 of the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of determination

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A participant in the operation is exempted from the
  requirement under section 6 of the [Sex Offender Information
  Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) to provide the information relating
  to the operation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.16-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[227.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.17 .sectionLabel}** The [Statutory
Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does not apply to a determination made
by the Chief of the Defence Staff under subsection 227.15(1) or
227.16(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual Report

- **[[227.171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.171
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief of the Defence Staff
  shall, within 30 days after the end of each year, submit a report to
  the Minister on the operation of sections 227.15 and 227.16 for that
  year that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of determinations made under each of
    paragraphs 227.15(a) to (d) and the duration of the suspension
    resulting from each determination; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of determinations made under subsection
    227.16(1) and the number of persons exempted under subsection
    227.16(4) as a result of each determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall cause a copy of the report to be
  laid before each House of Parliament on any of the first 15 days on
  which that House is sitting after the Minister receives the report.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
:::

#### [Disclosure of Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378235 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure

- **[[227.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At the request of the Provost
  Marshal, the Commissioner of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police or a
  person authorized by the Commissioner shall disclose information that
  is registered in the database, or the fact that information is
  registered in the database, to the Provost Marshal if the disclosure
  is necessary to enable the Provost Marshal to determine

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether a person may be served with a notice under
    section 227.08;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of a proceeding under section
    227.01 or subsection 227.04(1), 227.1(4) or 227.13(1) or for the
    purpose of an appeal respecting the legality of a decision made
    under any of those provisions, whether a person who is subject to
    the Code of Service Discipline, or who is an officer, or
    non-commissioned member, of the primary reserve, is, or was at any
    time, required to comply with an order made under section 227.01 of
    this Act or section 490.012 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
    or with an obligation under section 227.06 of this Act, section
    490.019 or 490.02901 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or
    section 36.1 of the [International Transfer of Offenders
    Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of enabling compliance with the
    [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7),
    whether a person who is subject to the Code of Service Discipline,
    or who is an officer, or non-commissioned member, of the primary
    reserve, is required to comply with an order made under section
    227.01 of this Act or section 490.012 of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or with an obligation under section 227.06 of
    this Act, section 490.019 or 490.02901 of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or section 36.1 of the [International Transfer
    of Offenders Act](/eng/acts/I-20.6); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether a person who is subject to the Code of
    Service Discipline, or who is an officer, or non-commissioned
    member, of the primary reserve, may be the subject of a
    determination under subsection 227.15(1) or 227.16(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure by Provost Marshal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal shall disclose the information

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to a prosecutor if the disclosure is necessary for
    the purpose of a proceeding for an order under section 227.01, a
    termination order under subsection 227.04(1) or 227.13(1) or an
    exemption order under subsection 227.1(4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the Minister or counsel instructed by the
    Minister if the disclosure is necessary for the purpose of an appeal
    respecting the legality of a decision made in a proceeding referred
    to in paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to a person's commanding officer if the disclosure
    is necessary to enable the person to comply with the [Sex Offender
    Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to the Chief of the Defence Staff if the disclosure
    is necessary for the purpose of a determination under subsection
    227.15(1) or 227.16(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure in proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The prosecutor or the Minister or counsel instructed
  by the Minister may, if the information is relevant to the proceeding
  or appeal referred to in paragraph (2)(a) or (b), disclose it to the
  presiding judge, court or court martial.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 56
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.18-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 227.18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure

- **[[227.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person, in connection with a
  proceeding or an appeal other than one referred to in paragraph
  227.18(2)(a) or (b), discloses the fact that information relating to
  them is registered in the database, the Provost Marshal shall request
  that the Commissioner of the Royal Canadian Mounted Police disclose
  all of the information relating to the person that is registered in
  the database. The Commissioner or a person authorized by the
  Commissioner shall disclose the information to the Provost Marshal
  without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure by Provost Marshal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal shall disclose the information

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the officer conducting the summary hearing and
    to a person who provides legal advice to the officer with respect to
    the hearing, in the case of a summary hearing; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the prosecutor or to the Minister or counsel
    instructed by the Minister in any other case.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 15, s. 36\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure in proceedings

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The officer who conducted the summary hearing may
  disclose the information to a review authority, and to a person who
  provides legal advice to the review authority, with respect to a
  review of a finding that a person has committed a service infraction
  or of any sanction imposed by that officer, if the information is
  relevant to the review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure in proceedings

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The prosecutor or the Minister or counsel instructed
  by the Minister may disclose the information to the presiding judge,
  court or court martial in the proceeding or appeal or in any
  subsequent appeal, or to a review authority and to a person who
  provides legal advice to the review authority with respect to a review
  of a finding of guilty made or a punishment imposed in the proceeding
  or appeal, if the information is relevant to the proceeding, appeal or
  review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure in proceedings

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A review authority may disclose the information to
  another review authority and to a person who provides legal advice to
  the other review authority with respect to a review by that review
  authority of a finding of guilty made or punishment imposed in a
  proceeding or appeal referred to in subsection (1) or in any
  subsequent appeal, if the information is relevant to the review.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2010, c. 17, s. 57
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
- [2019, c. 15, s. 36](#1149839-1355524){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149839-1355524.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-227.19-20140601.html "Link to previous version of section 227.19"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Authorizations, Designations and Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378270 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations by Governor in Council

**[[227.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.2 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the means by which designated classes of
  persons who are subject to the Code of Service Discipline and
  officers, or non-commissioned members, of the primary reserve are
  required to report under section 4, 4.1 or 4.3, or to provide
  notification under section 6, of the [Sex Offender Information
  Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) to registration centres designated
  under paragraph (e);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} designating classes of operations in respect of which
  a determination may be made under subsection 227.16(1);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorizing persons or classes of persons in or
  outside Canada to collect information under the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) that relates to persons
  who are subject to the Code of Service Discipline and to officers, or
  non-commissioned members, of the primary reserve;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} authorizing persons or classes of persons in or
  outside Canada to register information under the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) that relates to persons
  who are subject to the Code of Service Discipline and to officers, or
  non-commissioned members, of the primary reserve; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} designating places or classes of places in or outside
  Canada as registration centres for the purposes of the [Sex Offender
  Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7) and the area, or
  classes of persons who are subject to the Code of Service Discipline
  and officers, or non-commissioned members, of the primary reserve,
  served by each registration centre.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

**[[227.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227.21 .sectionLabel}** The Chief of the
Defence Staff, the Provost Marshal, the Chief Military Judge or a
commanding officer may authorize a person to communicate or disclose
information, or give notice, under this Division on their behalf.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 4
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

[Previous
Version](section-227.21-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 227.21"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 9]{.HLabel2}[Appeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378284 .Subheading}

#### [General Provisions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378285 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[legality]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[illegal]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, the expressions [[legality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and
[[illegal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} shall be deemed to relate either to
questions of law alone or to questions of mixed law and fact.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 228
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
:::

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998, c.
35, s. 67\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Right to Appeal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378291 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by person tried

**[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** Every person subject to
the Code of Service Discipline has, subject to subsection 232(3), the
right to appeal to the Court Martial Appeal Court from a court martial
in respect of any of the following matters:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with leave of the Court or a judge thereof, the
  severity of the sentence, unless the sentence is one fixed by law;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the decision to make an order under subsection
  745.51(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the legality of any finding of guilty;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the legality of the whole or any part of the
  sentence;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a finding of unfit to stand trial or
  not responsible on account of mental disorder;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a disposition made under section 201,
  202 or 202.16;

- [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a finding made under subsection
  202.161(4) or a decision made under subsection 202.162(3) about
  revoking such a finding;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision made under any of
  subsections 196.14(1) to (3);

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision made under subsection
  227.01(2);

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the legality of an order made under section 147.1 or
  226.2 and, with leave of the Court or a judge of the Court, the
  reasonableness of any period imposed under section 147.2;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the legality of an order made under section 148 and
  the legality or, with leave of the Court or a judge of the Court, the
  severity of any condition imposed under that section;

- [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision not to make an order
  under subsection 180.05(1) or of a decision to make or not to make an
  order under subsection 180.07(1);

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the legality or, with leave of the Court or a judge
  of the Court, the severity of a restitution order made under section
  203.9 or the legality of an order made under section 249.25; or

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a suspension of a sentence of
  imprisonment or detention and the legality or, with leave of the Court
  or a judge of the Court, the severity of any condition imposed under
  subsection 215(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 230
- 1991, c. 43, s. 21
- 2000, c. 10, s. 2
- 2007, c. 5, s. 5, c. 22, s. 45
- 2010, c. 17, s. 58
- 2011, c. 5, s. 8
- 2013, c. 24, s. 69
- [2014, c. 6, s. 29](#1034516-1353214){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034516-1353214.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 37](#1149848-1355526){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149848-1355526.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149981-1355527){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149981-1355527.html"
  role="button"}
:::
