[Previous
Version](section-230-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 230"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by Minister

**[[230.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister, or
counsel instructed by the Minister for that purpose, has, subject to
subsection 232(3), the right to appeal to the Court Martial Appeal Court
from a court martial in respect of any of the following matters:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} with leave of the Court or a judge thereof, the
  severity of the sentence, unless the sentence is one fixed by law;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the decision not to make an order under subsection
  745.51(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the legality of any finding of not guilty;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the legality of the whole or any part of the
  sentence;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision of a court martial that
  terminates proceedings on a charge or that in any manner refuses or
  fails to exercise jurisdiction in respect of a charge;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a finding of unfit to stand trial or
  not responsible on account of mental disorder;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a disposition made under section 201,
  202 or 202.16;

- [(f.01)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision not to make a finding
  under subsection 202.161(4) or a decision made under subsection
  202.162(3) about revoking such a finding;

- [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} the legality of an order for a stay of proceedings
  made under subsection 202.121(7);

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision made under any of
  subsections 196.14(1) to (3);

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision made under subsection
  227.01(2);

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the legality of an order made under section 147.1 or
  226.2 and, with leave of the Court or a judge of the Court, the
  reasonableness of any period imposed under section 147.2;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} the legality of an order made under section 148 and
  the legality or, with leave of the Court or a judge of the Court, the
  severity of any condition imposed under that section;

- [(j.1)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a decision to make an order under
  subsection 180.05(1) or 180.07(1);

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the legality or, with leave of the Court or a judge
  of the Court, the severity of a restitution order made under section
  203.9 or the legality of an order made under section 249.25; or

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the legality of a suspension of a sentence of
  imprisonment or detention and the legality or, with leave of the Court
  or a judge of the Court, the severity of any condition imposed under
  subsection 215(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 21
- 2000, c. 10, s. 3
- 2005, c. 22, s. 59
- 2007, c. 5, s. 6, c. 22, s. 46
- 2010, c. 17, s. 59
- 2011, c. 5, s. 9
- 2013, c. 24, s. 70
- [2014, c. 6, s. 30](#1034518-1353216){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149981-1355530){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149981-1355530.html"
  role="button"}

[Previous
Version](section-230.1-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 230.1"){.PITLink}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal from order

**[[230.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230.2 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
subsection 232(3), a person who applied for an exemption order under
section 227.1 or a termination order under section 227.03 or 227.12 and
the Minister or counsel instructed by the Minister have the right to
appeal to the Court Martial Appeal Court in respect of the legality of
the decision made by the court martial.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other rights preserved

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** The right of any person
to appeal from the finding or sentence of a court martial shall be
deemed to be in addition to and not in derogation of any rights that the
person has under the law of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 198
:::

#### [Entry of Appeals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378334 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appeal or application for leave to appeal under this Division shall be
  stated on a form to be known as a Notice of Appeal, which shall
  contain particulars of the grounds on which the appeal is founded and
  shall be signed by the appellant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Notice of Appeal is not invalid by reason only of
  informality or the fact that it deviates from the prescribed form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No appeal or application for leave to appeal under
  this Division shall be entertained unless the Notice of Appeal is
  delivered within thirty days after the date on which the court martial
  terminated its proceedings to the Registry of the Court Martial Appeal
  Court or, in such circumstances as may be prescribed by the Governor
  in Council in regulations, to a person prescribed in those
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Appeal Court or a judge thereof may
  at any time extend the time within which a Notice of Appeal must be
  delivered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forwarding statement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a Notice of Appeal is delivered pursuant to
  subsection (3) to a person prescribed by the Governor in Council in
  regulations, the person shall forward the Notice of Appeal to the
  Registry of the Court Martial Appeal Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 232
- 1991, c. 43, s. 22
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
- 2007, c. 5, s. 8(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-232-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 232"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Appeals from Dispositions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378348 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Automatic suspension of certain
dispositions

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if the
  disposition appealed from is a disposition made under section 202, the
  filing of a Notice of Appeal in accordance with section 232 suspends
  the application of the disposition pending the determination of the
  appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discretionary powers respecting
  suspension of dispositions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court may, on
  application of any party who gives notice to each of the other parties
  within the time and in the manner prescribed under subsection 244(1),
  where the judge is satisfied that the mental condition of the accused
  justifies the taking of such action,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the application of a
    disposition made under section 202 not be suspended pending the
    determination of the appeal;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the application of a
    disposition made under paragraph 202.16(1)(a) be suspended pending
    the determination of the appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by order, direct that the application of a
    disposition appealed from that was made under section 201 or
    paragraph 202.16(1)(b) or (c) be suspended pending determination of
    the appeal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the application of a disposition is suspended
    pursuant to subsection (1) or by virtue of an order made under
    paragraph (b), make such other disposition, other than a disposition
    under section 202 or paragraph 202.16(1)(a), in respect of the
    accused as is applicable and appropriate in the circumstances
    pending the determination of the appeal; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} give such directions as the judge thinks necessary
    for expediting the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of suspension of disposition

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the application of a disposition appealed from
  is suspended pursuant to subsection (1) or by virtue of an order made
  under paragraph (2)(b),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case where no disposition was in force in
    respect of the accused immediately before the coming into force of
    the disposition appealed from, any order for the interim release or
    detention of the accused that is in force immediately prior to the
    coming into force of the disposition appealed from continues in
    force, subject to the making of a disposition under paragraph
    (2)(c), pending the determination of the appeal; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the disposition in force
    immediately before the coming into force of the disposition appealed
    from continues in force, subject to the making of a disposition
    under paragraph (2)(c).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 233
- 1991, c. 43, s. 22
- [2014, c. 6, s. 31](#1034529-1353220){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034529-1353220.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-233-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 233"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Court Martial Appeal Court of Canada]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378364 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court established

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is hereby established a Court
  Martial Appeal Court of Canada, which shall hear and determine all
  appeals referred to it under this Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judges

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The judges of the Court Martial Appeal Court are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not fewer than four judges of the Federal Court of
    Appeal or the Federal Court to be designated by the Governor in
    Council; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any additional judges of a superior court of
    criminal jurisdiction who are appointed by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deputy judges of the Court

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2.2), any former judge of
  the Court Martial Appeal Court may, at the request of the Chief
  Justice of that Court made with the approval of the Governor in
  Council, act as a judge of the Court and while so acting has all the
  powers of a judge of the Court and shall be referred to as a deputy
  judge of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Governor in Council

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may approve the making of
  requests under subsection (2.1) in general terms or for particular
  periods or purposes, and may limit the number of persons who may act
  under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Salary

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} A person who acts as a judge under subsection (2.1)
  shall be paid a salary for the period he or she acts at the rate fixed
  by the [Judges Act](/eng/acts/J-1) for a judge of the Federal Court of
  Appeal or the Federal Court, other than a Chief Justice, less any
  amount otherwise payable to him or her under that Act in respect of
  that period, and shall also be paid the travel allowances that a judge
  is entitled to be paid under the [Judges Act](/eng/acts/J-1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Giving of judgment after judge ceases
  to hold office

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} If a judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court
  resigns his or her office or is appointed to another court or
  otherwise ceases to hold office, he or she may, at the request of the
  Chief Justice of the Court Martial Appeal Court, at any time within
  eight weeks after that event give judgment in any cause, action or
  matter previously tried by or heard before him or her as if he or she
  had continued in office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Justice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall designate one of the
  judges of the Court Martial Appeal Court to be the Chief Justice
  thereof, who shall preside, when present, at any sittings of the Court
  and shall, subject to subsection (4), appoint another judge to preside
  at any sittings of the Court at which the Chief Justice is not
  present.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity of Chief Justice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the office of Chief Justice is vacant, or the
  Chief Justice is absent from Canada or is unable or unwilling to act,
  his powers shall be exercised and his duties performed by the senior
  judge who is in Canada and is able and willing to Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 234
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
- 2002, c. 8, s. 153
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-234-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 234"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sittings and hearings

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court Martial Appeal Court may sit and hear appeals at any place or
  places, and the Chief Justice of the Court shall arrange for sittings
  and hearings as may be required.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing of appeals and other matters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every appeal shall be heard by three judges of the
  Court Martial Appeal Court sitting together, the decision of the
  majority of whom shall be the decision of the Court, and any other
  matter before the Court shall be disposed of by the Chief Justice or
  by such other judge or judges of the Court as the Chief Justice may
  designate for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of dissent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an appeal has been wholly or partially
  dismissed by the Court Martial Appeal Court and there has been dissent
  in the Court, the appellant shall forthwith be informed of that
  dissent.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 235
- R.S., 1985, c. 41 (1st Supp.), s. 13
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Superior court of record

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Court Martial Appeal Court is a superior court of record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Appeal Court may hear evidence
  including new evidence, as it may deem expedient and the Court may sit
  in camera or in public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The officers, clerks and employees appointed to the
  Courts Administration Service shall perform the duties of their
  respective offices in relation to the Court Martial Appeal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization to execute Chief
  Justice's functions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice of the Court Martial Appeal Court
  may authorize any other judges of the Court to exercise or perform any
  of the powers or functions of the Chief Justice under this section and
  sections 234 and 235.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 236
- 2002, c. 8, s. 154
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-236-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 236"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** A judge of the Court
Martial Appeal Court is entitled to be paid travel allowances under the
[Judges Act](/eng/acts/J-1) as for attendances as judge of the Federal
Court or the superior court to which the judge so entitled belongs.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 201
- R.S., c. 10(2nd Supp.), s. 64
- 1984, c. 40, s. 47(F)
:::

#### [Disposition of Appeals by Court Martial Appeal Court of Canada]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378410 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on appeal against finding of
guilty

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the hearing of an appeal respecting the legality of a finding of
  guilty on any charge, the Court Martial Appeal Court, if it allows the
  appeal, may set aside the finding and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter a finding of not guilty in respect of the
    charge; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct a new trial by court martial on the charge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of setting aside finding of
  guilty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court Martial Appeal Court has set aside a
  finding of guilty and no other finding of guilty remains, the whole of
  the sentence ceases to have force and effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence where findings partly set
  aside

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court Martial Appeal Court has set aside a
  finding of guilty but another finding of guilty remains, the Court
  may, except where it allows an appeal under section 240.1,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affirm the sentence imposed by the court martial if
    the court martial could legally have imposed that sentence on the
    finding of guilty that remains; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} substitute for the sentence imposed by the court
    martial a sentence that is warranted in law.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 238
- 1991, c. 43, s. 23
- 1998, c. 35, s. 68
- 2008, c. 29, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-238-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 238"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution of finding

- **[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an appellant has been found
  guilty of an offence and the court martial could, on the charge, have
  found the appellant guilty under section 133, 134 or 136 of some other
  offence or could have found the appellant guilty of some other offence
  on any alternative charge that was laid and, on the actual finding, it
  appears to the Court Martial Appeal Court that the facts proved the
  appellant guilty of that other offence, the Court may, instead of
  allowing or dismissing the appeal, substitute for the finding of
  guilty made by the court martial a finding of guilty of that other
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentence on substituted finding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the substitution of a finding of guilty under
  subsection (1), the Court Martial Appeal Court may, except where it
  allows an appeal under section 240.1,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affirm the sentence imposed by the court martial if
    the court martial could legally have imposed that sentence on the
    substituted finding of guilty; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} substitute for the sentence imposed by the court
    martial a sentence that is warranted in law.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1991, c. 43, s. 24\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 239
- 1991, c. 43, s. 24
- 1998, c. 35, s. 69
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against not guilty finding

- **[[239.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the hearing of an appeal
  respecting the legality of a finding of not guilty on any charge, the
  Court Martial Appeal Court may, where it allows the appeal, set aside
  the finding and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct a new trial by court martial on that charge;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} except if the finding is that of a General Court
    Martial, enter a finding of guilty with respect to the offence for
    which, in its opinion, the accused person should have been found
    guilty but for the illegality and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} impose the sentence in accordance with
      subsections (2) and (3), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} remit the matter to the court martial and direct
      it to impose a sentence in accordance with subsections (2) and
      (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no other finding of guilty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court Martial Appeal Court has entered a
  finding of guilty and there is no other finding of guilty, the Court
  or the court martial shall impose a sentence that is warranted in law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where another finding of guilty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Court Martial Appeal Court has entered a
  finding of guilty and there is another finding of guilty, the Court or
  the court martial may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affirm the sentence imposed by the court martial,
    if the court martial could legally have imposed the sentence on all
    of the findings; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} substitute for the sentence imposed by the court
    martial a sentence that is warranted in law.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 25
- 2008, c. 29, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-239.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 239.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against decision

**[[239.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239.2 .sectionLabel}** On the hearing of
an appeal respecting the legality of a decision referred to in paragraph
230.1(d), the Court Martial Appeal Court may, if it allows the appeal,
set aside the decision and direct a new trial by court martial on the
charge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 25
- 2008, c. 29, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-239.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 239.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution of new sentence where
illegal sentence set aside

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** On the hearing of an
appeal respecting the legality of a sentence imposed by a court martial,
the Court Martial Appeal Court, if it allows the appeal, may substitute
for the sentence imposed by the court martial a sentence that is
warranted in law.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 240
- 1991, c. 43, s. 26
- 1998, c. 35, s. 70
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against severity of sentence

**[[240.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240.1 .sectionLabel}** On the hearing of
an appeal respecting the severity of a sentence, the Court Martial
Appeal Court shall consider the fitness of the sentence and, if it
allows the appeal, may, on such evidence as it thinks fit to require or
receive, substitute for the sentence imposed by the court martial a
sentence that is warranted in law.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 26
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against finding of unfit or not
responsible

- **[[240.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the hearing of an appeal against
  a finding of unfit to stand trial or not responsible on account of
  mental disorder, the Court Martial Appeal Court shall, subject to
  subsection (2), direct a new trial by court martial if it allows the
  appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Finding after close of case for
  prosecution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the finding of unfit to stand trial was made
  after the close of the case for the prosecution, the Court may,
  notwithstanding that the finding is proper, allow the appeal, set
  aside the finding and enter a finding of not guilty on any charge if
  it is of the opinion that the accused should have been acquitted on
  the charge at the close of the case for the prosecution.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 26
- 2008, c. 29, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 240.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against disposition

**[[240.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240.3 .sectionLabel}** On the hearing of
an appeal respecting the legality of a disposition made under section
201, 202 or 202.16, the Court Martial Appeal Court may, where it allows
the appeal, set aside the disposition and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make any disposition under section 201 or 202.16 that
  the court martial could have made;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a disposition made by a General
  Court Martial, remit the matter to the court martial for a rehearing,
  in whole or in part, in accordance with any directions that the Court
  considers appropriate; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} make any other order that justice requires.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 26
- 2008, c. 29, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240.3-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 240.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

- **[[240.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Court Martial Appeal Court may
  allow an appeal against an order made under subsection 202.121(7) for
  a stay of proceedings, if the Court Martial Appeal Court is of the
  opinion that the order is unreasonable or cannot be supported by the
  evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Court Martial Appeal Court allows the appeal,
  it may set aside the order for a stay of proceedings and restore the
  finding that the accused person is unfit to stand trial and the
  disposition made in respect of the accused person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 22, s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal against order or decision

- **[[240.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On the hearing of an appeal
  respecting the legality of a decision made under subsection 227.01(2),
  227.04(1), 227.1(4) or 227.13(1), the Court Martial Appeal Court, or
  another court hearing the appeal, may dismiss the appeal, allow it and
  order a new hearing, quash the order or make an order that may be made
  under that provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Court Martial Appeal Court or other court
  makes an order that may be made under subsection 227.01(2), it shall
  cause the requirements set out in section 227.05 to be fulfilled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements relating to notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Court Martial Appeal Court or other court
  makes an order that may be made under subsection 227.04(1) or
  227.13(1), it shall cause the Provost Marshal to be notified of the
  decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of information from database

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Court Martial Appeal Court or other court
  makes an exemption order that may be made under subsection 227.1(4),
  it shall also make the order referred to in subsection 227.1(6).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2007, c. 5, s. 9
- 2010, c. 17, s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240.5-20110415.html "Link to previous version of section 240.5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special power to disallow appeal

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding
anything in this Division, the Court Martial Appeal Court may disallow
an appeal if, in the opinion of the Court, to be expressed in writing,
there has been no substantial miscarriage of justice.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 241
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New sentence

**[[241.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241.1 .sectionLabel}** Where a new
sentence is substituted under subsection 238(3), 239(2) or 239.1(3) or
section 240 or 240.1, the sentence imposed by the court martial ceases
to have effect.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 27
:::

**[[241.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241.2 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998,
c. 35, s. 71\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New trial

**[[241.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241.3 .sectionLabel}** Where the Court
Martial Appeal Court directs a new trial on a charge under section 238,
239.1, 239.2 or 240.2, the accused person shall be tried again as if no
trial on that charge had been held.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1991, c. 43, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers to suspend new punishment

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** If a punishment
included in a sentence has been dealt with under subsection 238(3),
239(2) or 239.1(3) or section 240 or 240.1, the new punishment is
subject to suspension in the same manner and to the same extent as if it
had been imposed by the court martial that tried the appellant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 242
- 1991, c. 43, s. 28
- [2019, c. 15, s. 39](#1149852-1355533){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149852-1355533.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-242-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 242"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal deemed abandoned

**[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** Where a review of a
disposition in respect of which an appeal is taken under paragraph
230(e) by any person is commenced under the review provisions of the
[Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) by that person, the appeal shall be
deemed to have been abandoned.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 243
- 1991, c. 43, s. 29
:::

#### [Rules of Appeal Procedure]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378514 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Justice may make rules

- **[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Justice of the Court Martial Appeal Court may, with the approval
  of the Governor in Council, make rules respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the seniority of members of the Court for the
    purpose of presiding at appeals;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the practice and procedure to be observed at
    hearings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of appeals;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of reviews of directions made under
    Division 3;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the production of the minutes of the proceedings of
    any court martial in respect of which an appeal is taken;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the production of all other documents and records
    relating to an appeal;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which new evidence may be introduced;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which, on the hearing of an
    appeal, the appellant may attend or appear before the Court;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the provision for and the payment of fees of
    counsel for an appellant or a respondent, other than the Minister;

  - [(h.1)]{.lawlabel} the awarding and regulating of costs in the Court
    in favour of or against appellants and respondents; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which an appeal may be
    considered to be abandoned for want of prosecution, and the summary
    disposition by the Court of such appeals and of appeals showing no
    substantial grounds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No rule made under this section has effect until it
  has been published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 244
- 1998, c. 35, s. 72
:::

#### [Appeal to Supreme Court of Canada]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378533 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by person tried

- **[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person subject to the Code of Service Discipline may appeal to the
  Supreme Court of Canada against a decision of the Court Martial Appeal
  Court

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on any question of law on which a judge of the
    Court Martial Appeal Court dissents; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on any question of law, if leave to appeal is
    granted by the Supreme Court of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister, or counsel instructed by the Minister
  for that purpose, may appeal to the Supreme Court of Canada against a
  decision of the Court Martial Appeal Court

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on any question of law on which a judge of the
    Court Martial Appeal Court dissents; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on any question of law, if leave to appeal is
    granted by the Supreme Court of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing and determination by Supreme
  Court of Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Supreme Court of Canada, in respect of the
  hearing and determination of an appeal under this section, has the
  same powers, duties and functions as the Court Martial Appeal Court
  has under this Act, and sections 238 to 242 apply with such
  adaptations and modifications as the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When appeal deemed abandoned

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An appeal to the Supreme Court of Canada that is not
  brought on for hearing by the appellant at the session of the Supreme
  Court of Canada during which the judgment appealed from was pronounced
  by the Court Martial Appeal Court, or at the next session of the
  Supreme Court of Canada, shall be deemed to be abandoned, unless
  otherwise ordered by the Supreme Court of Canada or a judge thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 245
- R.S., 1985, c. 34 (3rd Supp.), s. 14
- 1997, c. 18, s. 134
:::

**[[246 to 248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246_to_248
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 73\]]{.Repealed}

### [DIVISION 10]{.HLabel2}[Release Pending Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378550 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release by court martial

**[[248.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.1 .sectionLabel}** Every person
sentenced to a period of detention or imprisonment by a court martial
has, within twenty-four hours after being so sentenced, the right to
apply to that court martial or, in any circumstances that may be
provided for by regulations made by the Governor in Council, to a
military judge, for a direction that the person be released from
detention or imprisonment until the expiration of the time to appeal
referred to in subsection 232(3) and, if there is an appeal, until the
determination of the appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 74
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release by judge of the CMAC

**[[248.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.2 .sectionLabel}** Every person
sentenced to a period of detention or imprisonment by a court martial
who appeals under Division 9 has the right, if the person has not
applied under section 248.1, to apply to a judge of the Court Martial
Appeal Court or, in any circumstances that may be provided for by
regulations made by the Governor in Council, to a military judge, for a
direction that the person be released from detention or imprisonment
until the determination of the appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 74
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court may direct release

- **[[248.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On hearing an application to be
  released from detention or imprisonment, the court martial, the
  military judge or the judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court, as the
  case may be, may direct that the person making the application be
  released as provided for in sections 248.1 and 248.2 if the person
  establishes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application under section 248.1,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that the person intends to appeal,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is against sentence only, that it
      would cause unnecessary hardship if the person were placed or
      retained in detention or imprisonment,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} that the person will surrender himself into
      custody when directed to do so, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} that the person's detention or imprisonment is
      not necessary in the interest of the public or the Canadian
      Forces; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an application under section 248.2,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that the appeal is not frivolous,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the appeal is against sentence only, that it
      would cause unnecessary hardship if the person were placed or
      retained in detention or imprisonment,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} that the person will surrender himself into
      custody when directed to do so, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} that the person's detention or imprisonment is
      not necessary in the interest of the public or the Canadian
      Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of victim's safety and
  security

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the court martial, the military judge or the judge
  of the Court Martial Appeal Court, as the case may be, directs that
  the person be released, the court martial, military judge or judge
  shall include in the direction a statement that the safety and
  security of every victim of the alleged offence has been considered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to victim

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court martial, military judge or judge, as the
  case may be, shall, on request by a victim of the alleged offence,
  cause a copy of the direction to be given to the victim.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 75
- [2019, c. 15, s. 40](#1149856-1355554){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149856-1355554.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-248.3-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 248.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of representative of Canadian
Forces

**[[248.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.4 .sectionLabel}** On the hearing of
an application to be released, counsel acting on behalf of the Canadian
Forces shall be permitted to make representations if counsel so wishes
after representations by or on behalf of the person making the
application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 76(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undertaking if application granted

**[[248.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.5 .sectionLabel}** If an application
for release is granted, the court martial, the military judge or the
judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court, as the case may be, may direct
that the person making the application be released on giving an
undertaking to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} remain under military authority;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} surrender himself or herself into custody when
  directed to do so; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} comply with any other reasonable conditions that are
  stipulated.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 77
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release from detention or imprisonment

**[[248.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.6 .sectionLabel}** Where a person is
directed to be released from detention or imprisonment pursuant to this
Division, the person in whose custody that person is shall forthwith
release that person on his giving the undertaking referred to in section
248.5.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to duty

**[[248.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.7 .sectionLabel}** An officer or
non-commissioned member who is released from detention or imprisonment
pursuant to this Division shall be returned to duty unless the Chief of
the Defence Staff, or an officer designated by the Chief of the Defence
Staff, otherwise directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 92
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of conditions

- **[[248.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The conditions of an undertaking
  referred to in section 248.5 may, on application by the person who
  gave the undertaking or by counsel for the Canadian Forces, be
  reviewed by the Court Martial Appeal Court and that Court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirm the conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vary the conditions; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} substitute such other conditions as it sees fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New undertaking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the conditions of an undertaking referred to in
  section 248.5 have been varied or substituted pursuant to subsection
  (1), the person who gave the undertaking shall forthwith be placed in
  custody unless the person gives an undertaking to comply with such
  varied or substituted conditions.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, s. 78(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Breach of undertaking

- **[[248.81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, on application by counsel for
  the Canadian Forces, an authority referred to in subsection (2) is
  satisfied, on cause being shown, that an undertaking given by a person
  under section 248.5 has been breached or is likely to be breached,
  that authority may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} cancel the direction that authorized the person to
    be released and direct that the person be detained in custody; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct that the person may remain at liberty on his
    giving a new undertaking in accordance with section 248.5.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of authority

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authority to whom an application under
  subsection (1) may be made is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the undertaking was given in respect of a
    direction made pursuant to an application under section 248.1, a
    military judge; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (3), where the undertaking
    was given in respect of a direction made pursuant to an application
    under section 248.2, a judge of the Court Martial Appeal Court.

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 35, s. 79\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the circumstances provided for in regulations made
  by the Governor in Council, the authority to whom an application under
  subsection (1) may be made in respect of a direction made pursuant to
  an application under section 248.2 is a military judge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to make representations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person referred to in subsection (1) has the
  right to be present at the hearing of the application referred to in
  that subsection and the right to make representations at that hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1993, c. 34, s. 95(F)
- 1998, c. 35, s. 79
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[248.82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.82 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Justice
of the Court Martial Appeal Court, with the approval of the Governor in
Council, may make rules respecting applications under sections 248.2,
248.8 and 248.81.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to CMAC

- **[[248.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.9
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following persons, namely,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person whose application to be released from
    detention or imprisonment pursuant to this Division is refused, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who is the subject of an order under
    section 248.81

  may appeal that decision or order to the Court Martial Appeal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Canadian Forces may appeal any direction under
  this Division that a person be released from detention or imprisonment
  or any order under section 248.81.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds may be considered

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When hearing an appeal under this section, the Court
  Martial Appeal Court may, in all cases where an appeal has been filed,
  take into consideration the grounds of appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of provisions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of this Division apply, with such
  modifications as the circumstances require, to any appeal under this
  section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 57
- 1998, c. 35, ss. 80, 92
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender into custody

**[[248.91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248.91 .sectionLabel}** A person released
pending appeal under this Division may surrender himself or herself into
custody at any time to serve a sentence of detention or imprisonment
imposed on the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 81
:::

### [DIVISION 11]{.HLabel2}[Petition for New Trial]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378640 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to petition on new evidence

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who has been tried and
  found guilty by a court martial has a right, on grounds of new
  evidence discovered subsequent to the trial, to petition the Minister
  for a new trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to CMAC for determination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may refer a petition to the Court
  Martial Appeal Court for a hearing and determination by that Court as
  if it were an appeal by the petitioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to CMAC for opinion

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may refer a petition or any question
  relating to a petition to the Court Martial Appeal Court for its
  opinion, and that Court shall furnish its opinion accordingly.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}New trial

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that a petition
  should be granted, the Minister may order a new trial and the
  petitioner may be tried again as if no trial had been held.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 249
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- [2019, c. 15, s. 41](#1149858-1355566){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149858-1355566.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 249"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royal prerogative

**[[249.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.1 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this
Division in any manner limits or affects Her Majesty's royal prerogative
of mercy.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- [2019, c. 15, s. 41](#1149858-1355571){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149858-1355571.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 249.1"){.PITLink}
:::

2019, c. 15, s. 41\]]{.Repealed}

:::

2019, c. 15, s. 41\]]{.Repealed}

### [DIVISION 12]{.HLabel2}[Miscellaneous Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378708 .Subheading}

#### [Right to be Represented]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378709 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be represented

**[[249.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.17 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
liable to be charged, dealt with and tried under the Code of Service
Discipline has the right to be represented in the circumstances and in
the manner prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Defence Counsel Services]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378714 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[249.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may appoint an officer
  who is a barrister or advocate with at least ten years standing at the
  bar of a province to be the Director of Defence Counsel Services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel Services holds office
  during good behaviour for a term of not more than four years. The
  Minister may remove the Director of Defence Counsel Services from
  office for cause on the recommendation of an inquiry committee
  established under regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of inquiry committee

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An inquiry committee has the same powers, rights
  and privileges --- other than the power to punish for contempt --- as
  are vested in a superior court of criminal jurisdiction with respect
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the attendance, swearing and examination of
    witnesses;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the production and inspection of documents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the enforcement of its orders; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} all other matters necessary or proper for the due
    exercise of its jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel Services is eligible
  to be re-appointed on the expiration of a first or subsequent term of
  office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 71
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.18-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 249.18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties and functions

**[[249.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.19 .sectionLabel}** The Director of
Defence Counsel Services provides, and supervises and directs the
provision of, legal services prescribed in regulations made by the
Governor in Council to persons who are liable to be charged, dealt with
and tried under the Code of Service Discipline.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relationship to Judge Advocate General

- **[[249.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel
  Services acts under the general supervision of the Judge Advocate
  General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General instructions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Judge Advocate General may issue general
  instructions or guidelines in writing in respect of defence counsel
  services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions must be public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel Services shall ensure
  that the general instructions and guidelines are available to the
  public.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Barristers and advocates to assist

- **[[249.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel
  Services may be assisted by persons who are barristers or advocates
  with standing at the bar of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counsel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel Services may engage
  on a temporary basis the services of counsel to assist the Director of
  Defence Counsel Services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Director of Defence Counsel Services may, subject
  to any applicable Treasury Board directives, establish the terms and
  conditions of engagement and fix the remuneration and expenses of
  counsel engaged under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 72(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 249.21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Witnesses at Courts Martial and before Commissioners]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378752 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summonses to witnesses

- **[[249.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person required to give
  evidence before a court martial may be summoned by a military judge,
  the Court Martial Administrator or the court martial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summonses to witnesses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person required to give evidence before a
  commissioner taking evidence under this Act may be summoned by a
  military judge, the Court Martial Administrator or the commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of documents

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person summoned under this section may be required
  to bring and produce at the court martial or before the commissioner
  taking evidence under this Act any documents in the possession or
  under the control of the person that relate to the matters in issue.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Warrant for Arrest on Non-Appearance of Accused]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-appearance of accused

**[[249.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.23 .sectionLabel}** Where an accused
person has been duly summoned or ordered to appear before a court
martial, the court martial may issue a warrant in the form prescribed in
regulations made by the Governor in Council for the arrest of the
accused person if the accused person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fails to appear as summoned or ordered; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} having appeared before the court martial, fails to
  attend before the court martial as required.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Effect of New Punishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378769 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Force and effect

**[[249.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.24 .sectionLabel}** If a new
punishment is substituted for a punishment imposed by a court martial,
the new punishment has force and effect as if it had been imposed by the
court martial in the first instance and the provisions of the Code of
Service Discipline apply accordingly. However, if the new punishment
involves incarceration, the term of the new punishment is to be reckoned
from the date of substitution.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- [2019, c. 15, s. 42](#1149862-1355586){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149862-1355586.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.24-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 249.24"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Restitution of Property]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378774 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restitution of property

- **[[249.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A court martial that finds a person
  guilty of an offence shall order that any property obtained by the
  commission of the offence be restored to the person apparently
  entitled to it if, at the time of the trial, the property is before
  the court martial or has been detained so that it can be immediately
  restored under the order to the person so entitled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restitution where no conviction, but
  offence committed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an accused person is tried for an offence but
  is not convicted and it appears to the court martial that an offence
  has been committed, the court martial may order that any property
  obtained by the commission of the offence shall be restored to the
  person apparently entitled to it if, at the time of the trial, the
  property is before the court martial or has been detained so that it
  can be immediately restored under the order to the person so entitled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order shall not be made in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} property to which an innocent purchaser for value
    has acquired lawful title;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a valuable security that has been paid or
    discharged in good faith by a person who was liable to pay or
    discharge it; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a negotiable instrument that has, in good faith,
    been taken or received by transfer or delivery for valuable
    consideration by a person who had no notice and no reasonable cause
    to suspect that an offence had been committed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution of order for restitution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order made under this section shall be executed by
  the persons by whom the process of the court martial is ordinarily
  executed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 74
- [2019, c. 15, s. 46](#1149876-1355637){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149876-1355637.html"
  role="button"}
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1149995-1355663){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149995-1355663.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.25-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 249.25"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Reference to Ranks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378789 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to ranks

**[[249.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.26 .sectionLabel}** Every reference
in this Part to the rank of an officer or non-commissioned member
includes a person who holds any equivalent relative rank, whether that
person is attached, seconded or on loan to the Canadian Forces.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Criminal Record]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378794 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convictions for certain offences

- **[[249.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is convicted of any of
  the following offences, or who has been convicted of any of them
  before the coming into force of this section, has not been convicted
  of a criminal offence:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence described in section 85, 86, 87, 89, 90,
    91, 95, 96, 97, 99, 101, 101.1, 102, 103, 108, 109, 112, 116, 117,
    118, 118.1, 120, 121, 122, 123, 126 or 129 for which the offender is
    sentenced to one or more of the following punishments:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a severe reprimand,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a reprimand,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a fine not exceeding basic pay for one month,
      or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a minor punishment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an offence under section 130 that constitutes a
    contravention within the meaning of the [Contraventions
    Act](/eng/acts/C-38.7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Criminal Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An offence referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b)
  does not constitute an offence for the purposes of the [Criminal
  Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 75
- [2019, c. 15, s. 63](#1150069-1175614){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1150069-1175614.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-249.27-20180901.html "Link to previous version of section 249.27"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Complaints About or by Military Police]{.HTitleText1} {#h-378808 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378809 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Chairperson]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chairperson of the
    Complaints Commission. ([président]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Complaints Commission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Complaints Commission]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Military
    Police Complaints Commission established by subsection
    250.1(1). ([Commission]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[conduct complaint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[conduct complaint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a complaint about
    the conduct of a member of the military police made under subsection
    250.18(1). ([plainte pour inconduite]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[interference complaint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[interference complaint]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a complaint
    about interference with a military police investigation made under
    subsection 250.19(1). ([plainte pour ingérence]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[military police]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[military police]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2013, c. 24, s.
    76\]]{.Repealed}

[[Provost Marshal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Provost Marshal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2007, c. 5, s.
    10\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 250
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2007, c. 5, s. 10
- 2013, c. 24, s. 76
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250-20080912.html "Link to previous version of section 250"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Military Police Complaints Commission]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378820 .Subheading}

#### [Establishment and Organization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378821 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commission established

- **[[250.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is established a commission,
  called the Military Police Complaints Commission, consisting of a
  Chairperson and not more than four other members to be appointed by
  the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full- or part-time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each member holds office as a full-time or a
  part-time member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure of office and removal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Each member holds office during good behaviour for a
  term not exceeding five years but may be removed by the Governor in
  Council for cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A member is eligible to be re-appointed on the
  expiration of a first or subsequent term of office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of full-time members

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Full-time members shall devote the whole of their
  time to the performance of their duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest --- part-time
  members

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Part-time members may not accept or hold any office
  or employment during their term of office that is inconsistent with
  their duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An officer, a non-commissioned member or an employee
  of the Department is not eligible to be a member of the Complaints
  Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The members are entitled to be paid for their
  services the remuneration and allowances fixed by the Governor in
  Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel and living expenses

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The members are entitled to be paid reasonable travel
  and living expenses incurred by them in the course of their duties
  while absent from their ordinary place of work, if full-time members,
  or their ordinary place of residence, if part-time members, subject to
  any applicable Treasury Board directives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of members

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The members are deemed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to be employed in the public service for the
    purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to be employees for the purposes of the [Government
    Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to be employed in the federal public administration
    for the purposes of any regulations made pursuant to section 9 of
    the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath of office

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Every member shall, before commencing the duties of
  office, take the following oath of office:

  I, ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}, do solemnly swear
  (or affirm) that I will faithfully and honestly fulfil my duties as a
  member of the Military Police Complaints Commission in conformity with
  the requirements of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), and of
  all rules and instructions under that Act applicable to the Military
  Police Complaints Commission, and that I will not disclose or make
  known to any person not legally entitled to it any knowledge or
  information obtained by me by reason of my office. (*And in the case
  of an oath*: So help me God.)

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2003, c. 22, ss. 224(E), 225(E)
- 2010, c. 12, s. 1755
- 2013, c. 24, s. 77(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.1-20120316.html "Link to previous version of section 250.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Chairperson]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378851 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief executive officer

- **[[250.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson is the chief
  executive officer of the Complaints Commission and has supervision
  over and direction of its work and staff.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of the absence or incapacity of the
  Chairperson or if the office of Chairperson is vacant, the Minister
  may authorize any member of the Complaints Commission to exercise the
  powers and perform the duties and functions of the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may delegate to a member of the
  Complaints Commission any of the Chairperson's powers, duties or
  functions under this Act, except the power to delegate under this
  subsection and the duty to submit an annual report under subsection
  250.17(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Head Office]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378861 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head Office

**[[250.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.12 .sectionLabel}** The head office
of the Complaints Commission shall be at the place in Canada designated
by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Staff]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378866 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

- **[[250.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employees that are necessary for
  the proper conduct of the work of the Complaints Commission shall be
  appointed in accordance with the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Experts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Complaints Commission may, with the approval of
  the Treasury Board, engage on a temporary basis the services of
  counsel and other persons having technical or specialized knowledge to
  assist the Complaints Commission in its work, establish the terms and
  conditions of their engagement and fix and pay their remuneration and
  expenses.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Duty to Act Expeditiously]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378874 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to act expeditiously

**[[250.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.14 .sectionLabel}** The Complaints
Commission shall deal with all matters before it as informally and
expeditiously as the circumstances and the considerations of fairness
permit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Rules]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378879 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[250.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.15 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson
may make rules respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner of dealing with matters and business
  before the Complaints Commission, including the conduct of
  investigations and hearings by the Complaints Commission;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the apportionment of the work of the Complaints
  Commission among its members and the assignment of members to review
  complaints; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the performance of the duties and functions of the
  Complaints Commission.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Immunity]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378887 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of members

**[[250.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.16 .sectionLabel}** No criminal or
civil proceedings lie against any member of the Complaints Commission,
or against any person acting on behalf of the Complaints Commission, for
anything done, reported or said in good faith in the exercise or
purported exercise of a power or in the performance or purported
performance of a duty or function of the Complaints Commission.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

#### [Annual Report]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378892 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual Report

- **[[250.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.17
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall, within three
  months after the end of each year, submit to the Minister a report of
  the Complaints Commission's activities during that year and its
  recommendations, if any.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall have a copy of the report laid
  before each House of Parliament on any of the first fifteen days on
  which that House is sitting after the Minister receives it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Complaints]{.HTitleText2} {#h-378900 .Subheading}

#### [SUBDIVISION 1]{.HLabel3}[Right to Complain]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378901 .Subheading}

##### [Conduct Complaints]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378902 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints about military police

- **[[250.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any person, including any officer or
  non-commissioned member, may make a complaint under this Division
  about the conduct of a member of the military police in the
  performance of any of the policing duties or functions that are
  prescribed for the purposes of this section in regulations made by the
  Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complainant need not be affected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A conduct complaint may be made whether or not the
  complainant is affected by the subject-matter of the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No penalty for complaint

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person may not be penalized for exercising the
  right to make a conduct complaint so long as the complaint is made in
  good faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 78
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.18-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.18"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Interference Complaints]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378912 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaints by military police

- **[[250.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.19
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any member of the military police
  who conducts or supervises a military police investigation, or who has
  done so, and who believes on reasonable grounds that any officer or
  non-commissioned member or any senior official of the Department has
  improperly interfered with the investigation may make a complaint
  about that person under this Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Improper interference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, improper
  interference with an investigation includes intimidation and abuse of
  authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No penalty for complaint

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person may not be penalized for exercising the
  right to make an interference complaint so long as the complaint is
  made in good faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 79
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.19-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.19"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Time Limit]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378922 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

**[[250.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.2 .sectionLabel}** No complaint may be
made more than one year after the event giving rise to the complaint
unless the Chairperson, at the request of the complainant, decides that
it is reasonable in the circumstances to extend the time.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

##### [To Whom Complaint May be Made]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378927 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}To whom complaint may be made

- **[[250.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A conduct complaint or an
  interference complaint may be made, either orally or in writing, to
  the Chairperson, the Judge Advocate General or the Provost Marshal. A
  conduct complaint may also be made to any member of the military
  police.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acknowledgement and notice of complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person who receives a complaint shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the complaint is not in writing, put it in
    writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that an acknowledgement of its receipt is
    sent as soon as practicable to the complainant; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that notice of the complaint is sent as soon
    as practicable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a conduct complaint, to the
      Chairperson and the Provost Marshal,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an interference complaint
      concerning an officer or a non-commissioned member, to the
      Chairperson, the Chief of the Defence Staff, the Judge Advocate
      General and the Provost Marshal, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an interference complaint
      concerning a senior official of the Department, to the
      Chairperson, the Deputy Minister, the Judge Advocate General and
      the Provost Marshal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 80(F), 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to subject of conduct complaint

**[[250.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.22 .sectionLabel}** As soon as
practicable after receiving or being notified of a conduct complaint,
the Provost Marshal shall send a written notice of the substance of the
complaint to the person whose conduct is the subject of the complaint
unless, in the Provost Marshal's opinion, to do so might adversely
affect or hinder any investigation under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 81(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.22-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to subject of interference
complaint

**[[250.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.23 .sectionLabel}** As soon as
practicable after receiving or being notified of an interference
complaint, the Chairperson shall send a written notice of the substance
of the complaint to the person who is the subject of the complaint
unless, in the Chairperson's opinion, to do so might adversely affect or
hinder any investigation under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.23-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.23"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Withdrawal of Complaint]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378949 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal

- **[[250.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.24
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A complainant may withdraw a
  complaint by sending a written notice to the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of withdrawal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall send a notice in writing of the
  withdrawal to the Provost Marshal and the person who was the subject
  of the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 82(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.24-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.24"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Record of Complaints]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378957 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of complaints

**[[250.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.25 .sectionLabel}** The Provost
Marshal shall establish and maintain a record of all complaints received
under this Division and, on request, make available any information
contained in that record to the Complaints Commission.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.25-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.25"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Subdivision 2]{.HTitleText3} {#h-378962 .Subheading}

##### [Disposal of Conduct Complaints]{.HTitleText4} {#h-378963 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provost Marshal responsible

- **[[250.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal is responsible
  for dealing with conduct complaints.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint about Provost Marshal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a conduct complaint is about the conduct of the
  Provost Marshal, the Chief of the Defence Staff is responsible for
  dealing with the complaint and has all the powers and duties of the
  Provost Marshal under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.26-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline for resolving or disposing of
complaint

**[[250.261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.261 .sectionLabel}** The Provost
Marshal shall resolve or dispose of a conduct complaint --- other than a
complaint that results in an investigation of an alleged criminal or
service offence --- within one year after receiving or being notified of
it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 83
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Informal resolution

- **[[250.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On receiving or being notified of a
  conduct complaint, the Provost Marshal shall consider whether it can
  be disposed of informally and, with the consent of the complainant and
  the person who is the subject of the complaint, the Provost Marshal
  may attempt to resolve it informally.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the complaint is of
  a type prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statements not admissible

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No answer given or statement made by the complainant
  or the person who is the subject of the complaint in the course of
  attempting to resolve a complaint informally may be used in any
  disciplinary, criminal, civil or administrative proceedings, other
  than a hearing or proceeding in respect of an allegation that, with
  intent to mislead, the complainant or the person who is the subject of
  the complaint gave an answer or made a statement knowing it to be
  false.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to refuse or end informal
  resolution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal may direct that no attempt at
  informal resolution be started or that an attempt be ended if, in the
  opinion of the Provost Marshal,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is frivolous, vexatious or made in
    bad faith; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is one that could more appropriately
    be dealt with according to a procedure provided under another Part
    of this Act or under any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a direction is made under subsection (4), the
  Provost Marshal shall send to the complainant and the person who is
  the subject of the complaint a notice in writing setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the direction and the reasons why it was made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the right of the complainant to refer the complaint
    to the Complaints Commission for review if the complainant is not
    satisfied with the direction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of informal resolution

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a conduct complaint is resolved informally,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the details of its resolution must be set out in
    writing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the complainant and the person who is the subject
    of the complaint must give their written agreement to the resolution
    of the complaint; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Provost Marshal must notify the Chairperson of
    the resolution of the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 84(F), 107(F), 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.27-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to investigate

- **[[250.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any attempts at informal
  resolution, the Provost Marshal shall investigate a conduct complaint
  as soon as practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to refuse or end investigation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Provost Marshal may direct that no investigation
  of a conduct complaint be started or that an investigation be ended
  if, in the opinion of the Provost Marshal,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is frivolous, vexatious or made in
    bad faith;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is one that could more appropriately
    be dealt with according to a procedure provided under another Part
    of this Act or under any other Act of Parliament; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} having regard to all the circumstances,
    investigation or further investigation is not necessary or
    reasonably practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a direction is made under subsection (2), the
  Provost Marshal shall send to the complainant and, if the person who
  is the subject of the complaint was notified of the complaint under
  section 250.22, to that person, a notice in writing setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the direction and the reasons why it was made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the right of the complainant to refer the complaint
    to the Complaints Commission for review if the complainant is not
    satisfied with the direction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 85(F), 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
