Version](section-250.28-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report on investigation

**[[250.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.29 .sectionLabel}** On the completion
of an investigation into a conduct complaint, the Provost Marshal shall
send to the complainant, the person who is the subject of the complaint
and the Chairperson a report setting out

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a summary of the complaint;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the findings of the investigation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a summary of any action that has been or will be
  taken with respect to disposition of the complaint; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the right of the complainant to refer the complaint
  to the Complaints Commission for review if the complainant is not
  satisfied with the disposition of the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 86(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.29-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status reports

- **[[250.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within sixty days after receiving or
  being notified of a conduct complaint, the Provost Marshal shall, if
  the complaint has not been resolved or disposed of before that time,
  and then each thirty days afterwards until the complaint is dealt
  with, send to the following persons a report on the status of the
  complaint:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complainant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who is the subject of the complaint; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Six-month report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a conduct complaint has not been resolved or
  disposed of within six months, the Provost Marshal shall in each
  report sent after that period explain why not.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No report shall be sent to the person who is the
  subject of a conduct complaint if, in the opinion of the Provost
  Marshal, sending the report might adversely affect or hinder any
  investigation under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 87(F), 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.3-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.3"){.PITLink}
:::

##### [Review by Complaints Commission]{.HTitleText4} {#h-379031 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to Complaints Commission

- **[[250.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A complainant who is dissatisfied
  with a direction under subsection 250.27(4) or 250.28(2) in respect of
  a conduct complaint or the disposition of a conduct complaint as set
  out in a report under section 250.29 may refer the complaint in
  writing to the Complaints Commission for review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be provided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a complainant refers a complaint to the Complaints
  Commission under subsection (1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Chairperson shall send to the Provost Marshal a
    copy of the complaint; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Provost Marshal shall provide the Chairperson
    with a copy of the notice sent under subsection 250.27(5) or
    250.28(3), or of the report sent under section 250.29, in respect of
    the complaint and all information and materials relevant to the
    complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.31-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review by Chairperson

- **[[250.32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.32
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson shall review the
  complaint to which a request for review relates as soon as practicable
  after receiving the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson may investigate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In conducting a review of a complaint, the
  Chairperson may investigate any matter relating to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At the completion of the review, the Chairperson
  shall send a report to the Minister, the Chief of the Defence Staff
  and the Provost Marshal setting out the Chairperson's findings and
  recommendations with respect to the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.32-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status reports

- **[[250.33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.33
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within sixty days after a complaint
  is referred to the Commission for a review, the Chairperson shall, if
  the review has not been completed, and then each thirty days
  afterwards until it is completed, send a report on the status of the
  complaint to the complainant and the person who is the subject of the
  complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Six-month report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the review has not been completed within six
  months, the Chairperson shall in each report sent after that period
  explain why not.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No report shall be sent to the person who is the
  subject of a conduct complaint if, in the Chairperson's opinion,
  sending the report might adversely affect or hinder any investigation
  under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.33-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.33"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [SUBDIVISION 3]{.HLabel3}[Disposal of Interference Complaints]{.HTitleText3} {#h-379059 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibility

- **[[250.34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.34
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson is responsible for
  dealing with interference complaints.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation may be by Provost Marshal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson considers it appropriate to do so,
  the Chairperson may ask the Provost Marshal to investigate an
  interference complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for refusal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Provost Marshal does not consent to
  investigate, the Provost Marshal shall notify the Chairperson in
  writing of the reason why the consent was not given.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.34-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to investigate

- **[[250.35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson or the Provost
  Marshal, as the case may be, shall investigate an interference
  complaint as soon as practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to refuse or end investigation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may direct that no investigation of
  an interference complaint be started or that an investigation be ended
  if, in the Chairperson's opinion,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is frivolous, vexatious or made in
    bad faith;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is one that could more appropriately
    be dealt with according to a procedure provided under another Part
    of this Act or under any other Act of Parliament; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} having regard to all the circumstances,
    investigation or further investigation is not necessary or
    reasonably practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson makes a direction, the Chairperson
  shall send to the complainant, the person who is the subject of the
  complaint, the Chief of the Defence Staff or the Deputy Minister, as
  the case may be, the Judge Advocate General and the Provost Marshal a
  notice in writing setting out the direction and the reasons why it was
  made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 88(F), 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.35-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.35"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report on investigation

**[[250.36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.36 .sectionLabel}** On the completion
of an investigation into an interference complaint, the Chairperson
shall prepare and send a report setting out a summary of the complaint
and the Chairperson's findings and recommendations to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Chief of the Defence Staff, in the case of a
  complaint against an officer or a non-commissioned member;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Deputy Minister, in the case of a complaint
  against a senior official of the Department;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Judge Advocate General; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the Provost Marshal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 89(F), 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.36-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status reports

- **[[250.37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.37
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within sixty days after being
  notified of an interference complaint, the Chairperson shall, if the
  complaint has not been resolved, disposed of or otherwise dealt with
  before that time, and then each thirty days afterwards until the
  complaint is dealt with, send a report on the status of the complaint
  to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complainant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who is the subject of the complaint;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Judge Advocate General; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Provost Marshal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Six-month report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a complaint has not been dealt with within six
  months, the Chairperson shall in each report sent after that period
  explain why not.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No report shall be sent to the person who is the
  subject of a complaint if, in the Chairperson's opinion, sending the
  report might adversely affect or hinder any investigation under this
  Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 107(F), 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.37-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.37"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Investigations and Hearings by Complaints Commission]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379103 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public interest

- **[[250.38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.38
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If at any time the Chairperson
  considers it advisable in the public interest, the Chairperson may
  cause the Complaints Commission to conduct an investigation and, if
  warranted, to hold a hearing into a conduct complaint or an
  interference complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawn complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may cause an investigation to be held
  in respect of a complaint even if it has been withdrawn.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson decides to cause an investigation
  to be held, the Chairperson shall send a notice in writing of the
  decision and the reasons for the decision to the complainant, the
  person who is the subject of the complaint, the Minister, the Chief of
  the Defence Staff or the Deputy Minister, as the case may be, the
  Judge Advocate General and the Provost Marshal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No notice shall be sent to the person who is the
  subject of the complaint if, in the Chairperson's opinion, sending the
  notice might adversely affect or hinder any investigation under this
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties suspended

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson acts in respect of a conduct
  complaint under subsection (1), the Provost Marshal is not required to
  investigate, report on or otherwise deal with the complaint until the
  Provost Marshal receives a report under section 250.53 with respect to
  the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 90(F), 107(F), 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.38-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.38"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report on investigation

**[[250.39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.39 .sectionLabel}** On completion of
an investigation under subsection 250.38(1), the Chairperson shall
prepare and send to the Minister, the Chief of the Defence Staff or the
Deputy Minister, as the case may be, the Judge Advocate General and the
Provost Marshal a report in writing setting out the Chairperson's
findings and recommendations with respect to the complaint, unless the
Chairperson has caused, or intends to cause, a hearing to be held to
inquire into the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.39-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.39"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assignment of members to conduct hearing

- **[[250.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson decides to cause
  a hearing to be held, the Chairperson shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assign one or more members of the Complaints
    Commission to conduct the hearing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send a notice in writing of the decision and the
    reasons for the decision to the complainant, the person who is the
    subject of the complaint, the Minister, the Chief of the Defence
    Staff or the Deputy Minister, as the case may be, the Judge Advocate
    General and the Provost Marshal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, the member or members
  of the Complaints Commission who conduct a hearing are deemed to be
  the Complaints Commission.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 91(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.4-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

- **[[250.41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When conducting a hearing, the
  Complaints Commission has, in relation to the complaint before it,
  power

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to summon and enforce the attendance of witnesses
    and compel them to give oral or written evidence on oath and to
    produce any documents and things under their control that it
    considers necessary to the full investigation and consideration of
    matters before it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to receive and accept any evidence and information
    that it sees fit, whether admissible in a court of law or not.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (1), the Complaints
  Commission may not receive or accept

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any evidence or other information that would be
    inadmissible in a court of law by reason of any privilege under the
    law of evidence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any answer given or statement made before a board
    of inquiry or summary investigation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any answer or statement that tends to criminate the
    witness or subject the witness to any proceeding or penalty and that
    was in response to a question at a hearing under this Division into
    another complaint;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any answer given or statement made before a court
    of law or tribunal; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any answer given or statement made while attempting
    to resolve a conduct complaint informally under subsection
    250.27(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing in public

**[[250.42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.42 .sectionLabel}** A hearing is to
be held in public, except that the Complaints Commission may order the
hearing or any part of the hearing to be held in private if it is of the
opinion that during the course of the hearing any of the following
information will likely be disclosed:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} information that, if disclosed, could reasonably be
  expected to be injurious to the defence of Canada or any state allied
  or associated with Canada or the detection, prevention or suppression
  of subversive or hostile activities;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} information that, if disclosed, could reasonably be
  expected to be injurious to the administration of justice, including
  law enforcement; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} information affecting a person's privacy or security
  interest, if that interest outweighs the public's interest in the
  information.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2015, c. 3, s. 134(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.42-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.42"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of hearing

- **[[250.43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} As soon as practicable before the
  commencement of a hearing, the Complaints Commission shall serve a
  notice in writing of the time and place appointed for the hearing on
  the complainant and the person who is the subject of the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convenience to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person on whom a notice is served wishes to
  appear before the Complaints Commission, the Complaints Commission
  must consider the convenience of that person in fixing the time and
  the place for the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay of hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the complaint relates to conduct that is also the
  subject of disciplinary or criminal proceedings before a court or
  tribunal of first instance, the hearing may not take place until the
  disciplinary or criminal proceedings are completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 92(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.43-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.43"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of persons interested

**[[250.44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.44 .sectionLabel}** The Complaints
Commission shall afford a full and ample opportunity, in person or by
counsel, to present evidence, to cross-examine witnesses and to make
representations at the hearing to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complainant and the person who is the subject of
  the complaint, if they wish to appear; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other person who satisfies the Complaints
  Commission that the person has a substantial and direct interest in
  the hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 93(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.44-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.44"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness not excused from testifying

- **[[250.45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.45
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In a hearing, no witness shall be
  excused from answering any question relating to the complaint before
  the Complaints Commission when required to do so by the Complaints
  Commission on the ground that the answer to the question may tend to
  criminate the witness or subject the witness to any proceeding or
  penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Answer not receivable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No answer given or statement made by a witness in
  response to a question described in subsection (1) may be used or
  receivable against the witness in any disciplinary, criminal,
  administrative or civil proceeding, other than a hearing or proceeding
  in respect of an allegation that the witness gave the answer or made
  the statement knowing it to be false.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

**[[250.46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.46 .sectionLabel}** Travel and living
expenses incurred in appearing before the Complaints Commission shall,
in the discretion of the Complaints Commission, be paid in accordance
with applicable Treasury Board directives, to the complainant and to the
person who is the subject of the complaint, and to their counsel, if the
Complaints Commission holds a hearing at a place in Canada that is not
their ordinary place of residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 108(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.46-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.46"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of documents, etc.

**[[250.47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.47 .sectionLabel}** Documents and
things presented to the Complaints Commission at a hearing shall, on
request, be returned to the person who presented them within a
reasonable time after completion of the Complaints Commission's report
on the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

**[[250.48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.48 .sectionLabel}** On completion of
a hearing, the Complaints Commission shall prepare and send to the
Minister, the Chief of the Defence Staff or the Deputy Minister, as the
case may be, the Judge Advocate General and the Provost Marshal a report
in writing setting out its findings and recommendations with respect to
the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.48-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.48"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Review and Final Report]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379186 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review --- conduct complaint

- **[[250.49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.49
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a report under
  subsection 250.32(3) or section 250.39 or 250.48 in respect of a
  conduct complaint, the Provost Marshal shall review the complaint in
  light of the findings and recommendations set out in the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Provost Marshal is the subject of the
  complaint, the review shall be conducted by the Chief of the Defence
  Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 94(F), 107(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.49-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.49"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review --- interference complaint

- **[[250.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a report under section
  250.36, 250.39 or 250.48 in respect of an interference complaint, the
  complaint shall be reviewed in light of the findings and
  recommendations set out in the report by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Chief of the Defence Staff, if the person who
    is the subject of the complaint is an officer or a non-commissioned
    member; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Deputy Minister, if the person who is the
    subject of the complaint is a senior official of the Department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Chief of the Defence Staff or the Deputy
  Minister is the subject of the complaint, the review shall be
  conducted by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 95(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.5-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of action

- **[[250.51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.51
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The person who reviews a report
  under section 250.49 or 250.5 shall notify in writing the Minister and
  the Chairperson of any action that has been or will be taken with
  respect to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person decides not to act on any findings or
  recommendations set out in the report, the reasons for not so acting
  must be included in the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of action

- **[[250.52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.52
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister reviews a report by
  reason of subsection 250.5(2), the Minister shall notify the
  Chairperson in writing of any action that has been or will be taken
  with respect to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister decides not to act on any findings or
  recommendations set out in the report, the reasons for not so acting
  must be included in the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final report by Chairperson

- **[[250.53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250.53
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After receiving and considering a
  notice sent under section 250.51 or 250.52, the Chairperson shall
  prepare a final report in writing setting out the Chairperson's
  findings and recommendations with respect to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recipients of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the final report shall be sent to the
  Minister, the Deputy Minister, the Chief of the Defence Staff, the
  Judge Advocate General, the Provost Marshal, the complainant, the
  person who is the subject of the complaint and all persons who have
  satisfied the Complaints Commission that they have a substantial and
  direct interest in the complaint.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, s. 96(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-250.53-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 250.53"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART V]{.HLabel1}[Miscellaneous Provisions Having General Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-379224 .Part}

### [Oaths]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379225 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oaths

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** At summary hearings and
courts martial, and at proceedings before a military judge, board of
inquiry or commissioner taking evidence under this Act, an oath must be
taken by or administered to the following persons in the manner and in
the forms prescribed in regulations made by the Governor in Council:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the officer conducting the summary hearing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the judge presiding at the court martial;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} each member of the panel of the court martial;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} each member of the board of inquiry;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the commissioner;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} court reporters;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} interpreters; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 16 of the [Canada Evidence
  Act](/eng/acts/C-5), witnesses.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 251
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- [2019, c. 15, s. 43](#1149866-1355589){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149866-1355589.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-251-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 251"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Solemn affirmation instead of oath

- **[[251.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is required to take an
  oath under this Act may, instead of taking an oath, make a solemn
  affirmation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A solemn affirmation has the same force and effect as
  an oath.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecutions under the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An oath or a solemn affirmation under this Act has,
  in respect of any prosecution under the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the same force and effect as an oath taken
  before a civil court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
:::

### [Witness Fees and Allowances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379247 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness fees and allowances

**[[251.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251.2 .sectionLabel}** A person, other
than an officer or non-commissioned member or an officer or employee of
the Department, summoned or attending to give evidence before a court
martial, the Grievances Committee, the Military Judges Inquiry
Committee, the Military Police Complaints Commission, a board of
inquiry, a commissioner taking evidence under this Act or any inquiry
committee established under the regulations is entitled in the
discretion of that body to receive the like fees and allowances for so
doing as if summoned to attend before the Federal Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 82
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 97, 106(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-251.2-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 251.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Disposal by Civil Authorities of Deserters and Absentees without Leave]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379252 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section and sections 253 and 254,
  [[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a justice as defined in the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of arrest on reasonable grounds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any peace officer who on reasonable grounds believes
  or, if no peace officer is immediately available, any officer or
  non-commissioned member who believes on reasonable grounds that a
  person is a deserter or an absentee without leave may apprehend that
  person and forthwith bring the person before a justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issue of warrant

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A justice, if satisfied by evidence on oath that a
  deserter or an absentee without leave is, or is believed on reasonable
  grounds to be, within the jurisdiction of that justice, may issue a
  warrant authorizing the deserter or absentee without leave to be
  apprehended and brought forthwith before that or any other justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justice's power to examine into case

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is brought before a justice charged
  with being a deserter or absentee without leave under this Act, the
  justice may examine into the case in like manner as if that person
  were brought before the justice accused of an indictable offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 252
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 58
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of person brought before justice

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  justice, if satisfied either by evidence on oath or by the admission
  of a person brought before the justice under section 252 that the
  person is a deserter or absentee without leave, shall cause him to be
  delivered into service custody in such manner as the justice may deem
  most expedient and, until the person can be so delivered, the justice
  may cause the person to be held in civil custody for such time as
  appears to the justice reasonably necessary for the purpose of
  delivering the person into service custody.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Verification of admission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person has admitted to being a deserter or
  absentee without leave and evidence of the truth or falsehood of the
  admission is not then forthcoming, the justice before whom the person
  is brought shall remand him for the purpose of obtaining information
  respecting the truth or falsehood of the admission and, for that
  purpose, the justice shall transmit a report, which shall contain the
  particulars and be in the form prescribed by the Minister, to such
  authorities of the Canadian Forces as the Minister may prescribe.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remands

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A justice before whom a person is brought under
  section 252 may from time to time remand him for a period not
  exceeding eight days on each appearance before the justice, but the
  whole period during which a person is so remanded shall not be longer
  than appears to the justice reasonably necessary for the purpose of
  obtaining the information referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report following disposal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a justice before whom a person is brought under
  section 252 causes him to be delivered into service custody or to be
  held in civil custody, the justice shall transmit a report, which
  shall contain the particulars and be in the form prescribed by the
  Minister, to such authorities of the Canadian Forces as the Minister
  may prescribe.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 214
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivery by constable into service
custody

- **[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a person surrenders himself to
  a constable and admits desertion or absence without leave, the
  constable in charge of the police station to which the person is
  brought shall forthwith inquire into the case and, if from the
  admission it appears to the constable on inquiring into the case that
  the person is a deserter or absentee without leave, the constable may
  cause the person to be delivered into service custody, without
  bringing the person before a justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report where person delivered into
  service custody

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a constable causes a person to be delivered
  into service custody pursuant to subsection (1), the constable shall
  transmit a report, which shall contain the particulars and be in the
  form prescribed by the Minister, to such authorities of the Canadian
  Forces as the Minister may prescribe.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 214
:::

### [Certificate of Civil Courts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379282 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmission of certificate where person
tried civilly

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** Where any person
subject to the Code of Service Discipline has at any time been tried by
a civil court, the clerk of that court or other authority having custody
of the records of the court shall, if required by any officer of the
Canadian Forces, transmit to that officer a certificate setting out the
offence for which that person was tried, together with the judgment or
order of the court thereon, and shall be allowed for that certificate
the fee authorized by law.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 215
:::

### [Duties respecting Incarceration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379287 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution of warrants

- **[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every warden, governor, jailer,
  commanding officer, commandant or other keeper of a penitentiary,
  civil prison, service prison or detention barrack shall take
  cognizance of any warrant of committal purporting to be signed by a
  committing authority referred to in section 219 or 220, shall receive
  and detain, according to the exigency of that warrant, the person
  referred to therein and delivered into the custody of that warden,
  governor, jailer, commanding officer, commandant or other keeper, as
  the case may be, and shall confine that person until discharged or
  delivered over in due course of law.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1991, c. 43, s. 30\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 256
- 1991, c. 43, s. 30
:::

### [Manoeuvres]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379294 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization by Minister

- **[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of training the Canadian Forces, the Minister may
  authorize the execution of military exercises or movements, referred
  to in this section as "manoeuvres", over and on such parts of Canada
  and during such periods as are specified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notice of manoeuvres shall, by appropriate
  publication, be given to the inhabitants of any area concerned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Units and other elements of the Canadian Forces may
  execute manoeuvres on and pass over such areas as are specified under
  subsection (1), stop or control all traffic thereover whether by
  water, land or air, draw water from such sources as are available, and
  do all things reasonably necessary for the execution of the
  manoeuvres.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interference

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any person who wilfully obstructs or interferes with
  manoeuvres authorized under this section and any animal, vehicle,
  vessel or aircraft under the person's control may be forcibly removed
  by any constable or by any officer, or by any non-commissioned member
  on the order of any officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bar of action

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No action lies by reason only of the execution of
  manoeuvres authorized under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 257
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

**[[258 and 259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258_and_259
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s.
74\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

**[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** Any person who suffers
loss, damage or injury by reason of the exercise of any of the powers
conferred by section 257 shall be compensated from the Consolidated
Revenue Fund.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 260
- R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 74
:::

### [Exemption from Tolls]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379313 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties or tolls on roads, bridges, etc.

- **[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  duties or tolls, otherwise payable by law in respect of the use of any
  pier, wharf, quay, landing-place, highway, road, right-of-way, bridge
  or canal, shall be paid by or demanded from any unit or other element
  of the Canadian Forces or any officer or non-commissioned member when
  on duty or any person under escort or in respect of the movement of
  any materiel, except that the Minister may authorize payment of duties
  and tolls in respect of that use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section affects the liability for
  payment of duties or tolls lawfully demandable in respect of any
  vehicles or vessels other than those belonging to or in the service of
  Her Majesty.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 261
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Ships in Convoy]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379321 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Master of merchant ship to obey convoying
officer

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** Every master or other
person in command of a merchant or other vessel under the convoy of any
of Her Majesty's Canadian ships shall obey the directions of the
commanding officer of the convoy or the directions of the commanding
officer of any of Her Majesty's Canadian ships in all matters relating
to the navigation or security of the convoy and shall take such
precautions for avoiding the enemy as may be directed by any such
commanding officer and, if the master or other person fails to obey the
directions, that commanding officer may compel obedience by force of
arms, without being liable for any loss of life or property that may
result from the use of that force.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 222
:::

### [Salvage]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379326 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crown may claim for salvage services

**[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** Where salvage services
are rendered by or with the aid of a vessel or aircraft belonging to or
in the service of Her Majesty and used in the Canadian Forces, Her
Majesty may claim salvage for those services and has the same rights and
remedies in respect of those services as any other salvor would have had
if the vessel or aircraft had belonged to that other salvor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 223
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent of Minister to salvage claim

- **[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  claim for salvage services by the commander or crew or part of the
  crew of a vessel or aircraft belonging to or in the service of Her
  Majesty and used in the Canadian Forces shall be finally adjudicated
  on unless the consent of the Minister to the prosecution of the claim
  is proved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for giving consent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, the consent of the
  Minister may be given at any time before final adjudication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any document purporting to give the consent of the
  Minister for the purpose of this section is evidence of that consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claim dismissed if no consent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a claim for salvage services is prosecuted and
  the consent of the Minister is not proved, the claim shall be
  dismissed with costs.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 223
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may accept offers of settlement

- **[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on the recommendation of the Attorney General of Canada,
  accept, on behalf of Her Majesty and the commander and crew or part of
  the crew, offers of settlement made with respect to claims for salvage
  services rendered by vessels or aircraft belonging to or in the
  service of Her Majesty and used in the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distribution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The proceeds of any settlement made under
  subsection (1) shall be distributed in such manner as the Governor in
  Council may prescribe.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 223
:::

**[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c.
26, s. 311\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-266-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 266"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Limitation or Prescription Periods, Liability and Exemptions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379350 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction of execution against officers
and non-commissioned members

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** No judgment or order
given or made against an officer or non-commissioned member by any court
in Canada shall be enforced by the levying of execution on any arms,
ammunition, equipment, instruments or clothing used by that officer or
non-commissioned member for military purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 267
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from jury service

**[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** Every officer and
non-commissioned member of the reserve force on active service and every
officer and non-commissioned member of the regular force and special
force is exempt from serving on a jury.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 268
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation or prescription period

- **[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless an action or other proceeding
  is commenced within two years after the day on which the act, neglect
  or default complained of occurred, no action or other proceeding lies
  against Her Majesty or any person for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an act done in pursuance or execution or intended
    execution of this Act or any regulations or military or departmental
    duty or authority;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any neglect or default in the execution of this Act
    or any regulations or military or departmental duty or authority; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an act or any neglect or default that is incidental
    to an act, neglect or default described in paragraph (a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecutions

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A prosecution in respect of an offence --- other
  than an offence under this Act, the [Geneva Conventions
  Act](/eng/acts/G-3) or the [Crimes Against Humanity and War Crimes
  Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9) --- relating to an act, neglect or default
  described in subsection (1) may not be commenced after six months from
  the day on which the act, neglect or default occurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving provision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) is in bar of proceedings
  against any person under the Code of Service Discipline.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 269
- 2013, c. 24, s. 99
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-269-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 269"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Actions barred

**[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** No action or other
proceeding lies against any officer or non-commissioned member in
respect of anything done or omitted by the officer or non-commissioned
member in the execution of his duty under the Code of Service
Discipline, unless the officer or non-commissioned member acted, or
omitted to act, maliciously and without reasonable and probable cause.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 270
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

### [Compensation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379375 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation to certain public service
employees

- **[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Compensation may, to such extent, in
  such manner and to such persons as the Governor in Council may by
  regulation prescribe, be paid in respect of disability or death
  resulting from injury or disease or aggravation thereof incurred by
  any person while

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} employed in the federal public administration,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} employed under the direction of any part of the
    federal public administration, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} engaged, with or without remuneration, in an
    advisory, supervisory or consultative capacity in or on behalf of
    the federal public administration,

  and performing any function in relation to the Canadian Forces or any
  forces cooperating with the Canadian Forces, if the injury or disease
  or aggravation thereof arose out of or was directly connected with the
  performance of that function.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No compensation shall be paid under subsection (1) in
  respect of any disability or death for which a pension is paid or
  payable by virtue of any of the provisions of the [Pension
  Act](/eng/acts/P-6).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 271
- 1998, c. 35, s. 83
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-271-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 271"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Dependants]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379387 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest of dependants

**[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** The dependants, as
defined by regulation, of officers and non-commissioned members on
service or active service in any place out of Canada who are alleged to
have committed an offence under the laws applicable in that place may be
arrested by a member of the military police and may be handed over to
the appropriate authorities of that place.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 272
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 100
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-272-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 272"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Jurisdiction of Civil Courts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379392 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences committed outside Canada

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** Where a person subject
to the Code of Service Discipline does any act or omits to do anything
while outside Canada which, if done or omitted in Canada by that person,
would be an offence punishable by a civil court, that offence is within
the competence of, and may be tried and punished by, a civil court
having jurisdiction in respect of such an offence in the place in Canada
where that person is found in the same manner as if the offence had been
committed in that place, or by any other court to which jurisdiction has
been lawfully transferred.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 231
:::

### [Inspections]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379397 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[273.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the inspection, in accordance with the
  custom or practice of the service, of any person or thing in, on or
  about

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any defence establishment, work for defence or
    materiel, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any quarters under the control of the Canadian
    Forces or the Department; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the access to, exclusion from and safety
  and conduct of persons in, on or about any defence establishment, work
  for defence or materiel, including, without restricting the generality
  of the foregoing, regulations

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} respecting the inspection of persons and property
    entering, exiting or on any such place or materiel, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} requiring any person, as a condition of being
    given access to that place or materiel, to submit, on demand, to a
    search of the person and the person's personal or movable property
    while entering or exiting that place or materiel or any restricted
    area within that place or materiel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 59
- 1998, c. 35, s. 84
:::

### [Searches]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379408 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Searches

**[[273.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.2 .sectionLabel}** Except as provided
for by regulations made pursuant to section 273.1, the following,
namely,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} quarters under the control of the Canadian Forces or
  the Department and occupied for residential purposes by any person
  subject to the Code of Service Discipline either alone or with that
  person's dependants, as well as any locker or storage space located in
  those quarters and exclusively used by that person or those dependants
  for personal purposes, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the personal or movable property of any person
  subject to the Code of Service Discipline located in, on or about any
  defence establishment, work for defence or materiel,

may be searched only if a warrant for that purpose has been issued or
the search is otherwise authorized by law.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 59
- 1998, c. 35, s. 85
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant by commanding officer

**[[273.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.3 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections
273.4 and 273.5, a commanding officer who is satisfied by information on
oath that there is in any quarters, locker, storage space or personal or
movable property referred to in section 273.2

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} anything on or in respect of which any offence
  against this Act has been or is believed on reasonable grounds to have
  been committed,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} anything that there are reasonable grounds to believe
  will afford evidence with respect to the commission of an offence
  against this Act, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} anything that there are reasonable grounds to believe
  is intended to be used for the purpose of committing any offence
  against the person for which a person may be arrested without warrant,

may issue a warrant authorizing any officer or non-commissioned member
named in the warrant, assisted by such other officers and
non-commissioned members as are necessary, or a peace officer, to search
the quarters, locker, storage space or personal or movable property for
any such thing, and to seize and carry it before that commanding
officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 59
- 1998, c. 35, s. 86
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigating commanding officer

**[[273.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.4 .sectionLabel}** The commanding
officer who carries out or directly supervises the investigation of any
matter may issue a warrant pursuant to section 273.3 in relation to that
investigation only if that commanding officer believes on reasonable
grounds that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions for the issuance of the warrant exist;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no other commanding officer is readily available to
  determine whether the warrant should be issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 59
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Military police

**[[273.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.5 .sectionLabel}** Section 273.3 does
not apply to a commanding officer of a military police unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 59
:::

### [Public Service]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379434 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public service

- **[[273.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  Governor in Council or the Minister may authorize the Canadian Forces
  to perform any duty involving public service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Law enforcement assistance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council, or the Minister on the
  request of the Minister of Public Safety and Emergency Preparedness or
  any other Minister, may issue directions authorizing the Canadian
  Forces to provide assistance in respect of any law enforcement matter
  if the Governor in Council or the Minister, as the case may be,
  considers that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assistance is in the national interest; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the matter cannot be effectively dealt with except
    with the assistance of the Canadian Forces.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of
  assistance that is of a minor nature and limited to logistical,
  technical or administrative support.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authority of the Minister under this section is
  subject to any directions issued by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 35, s. 87
- 2005, c. 10, s. 34
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-273.6-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 273.6"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Independent Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379448 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[273.601]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273.601
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall cause an
  independent review of the following provisions, and their operation,
  to be undertaken:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sections 18.3 to 18.6;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 29 to 29.28;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Parts III and IV; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sections 251, 251.2, 256, 270, 272, 273 to 273.5
    and 302.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall cause a report of a review to be
  laid before each House of Parliament within seven years after the day
  on which this section comes into force, and within every seven-year
  period after the tabling of a report under this subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amending legislation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} However, if an Act of Parliament amends this Act
  based on an independent review, the next report shall be tabled within
  seven years after the day on which the amending Act is assented to.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 101
:::
