## [PART VI]{.HLabel1}[Aid of the Civil Power]{.HTitleText1} {#h-379573 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[attorney
general]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, [[attorney general]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the attorney
general of any province, the acting attorney general of a province or
any minister of a government of a province who performs for the time
being the duties of a provincial attorney general.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 232
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Riot or disturbance

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** The Canadian Forces,
any unit or other element thereof and any officer or non-commissioned
member, with materiel, are liable to be called out for service in aid of
the civil power in any case in which a riot or disturbance of the peace,
beyond the powers of the civil authorities to suppress, prevent or deal
with and requiring that service, occurs or is, in the opinion of an
attorney general, considered as likely to occur.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 275
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception in case of certain reserves

**[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this Part
shall be deemed to impose liability to serve in aid of the civil power,
without his consent, on an officer or non-commissioned member of the
reserve force who is, by virtue of the terms of his enrolment, liable to
perform duty on active service only.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 276
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attorney general of province may
requisition aid

**[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** Where a riot or
disturbance occurs or is considered as likely to occur, the attorney
general of the province in which the place where the riot or disturbance
occurs or is considered as likely to occur is situated, on the
initiative of the attorney general or on the receipt of notification
from a judge of a superior, county or district court having jurisdiction
in the place that the services of the Canadian Forces are required in
aid of the civil power, may, by requisition in writing addressed to the
Chief of the Defence Staff, require the Canadian Forces, or such part
thereof as the Chief of the Defence Staff or such officer as the Chief
of the Defence Staff may designate considers necessary, to be called out
on service in aid of the civil power.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 235
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Call out of Canadian Forces

**[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** On receiving a
requisition in writing made by an attorney general under section 277,
the Chief of the Defence Staff, or such officer as the Chief of the
Defence Staff may designate, shall, subject to such directions as the
Minister considers appropriate in the circumstances and in consultation
with that attorney general and the attorney general of any other
province that may be affected, call out such part of the Canadian Forces
as the Chief of the Defence Staff or that officer considers necessary
for the purpose of suppressing or preventing any actual riot or
disturbance or any riot or disturbance that is considered as likely to
occur.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 278
- 2004, c. 15, s. 79
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-278-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 278"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of requisition

**[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** A requisition of an
attorney general under this Part may be in the following form, or to the
like effect, and the form may, subject to section 280, be varied to suit
the facts of the case:

Province of

To Wit

Whereas information has been received by me from responsible persons (or
a notification has been received by me from a judge of a (superior)
(county) (district) court having jurisdiction in ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}) that a riot or disturbance of
the peace beyond the powers of the civil authorities to suppress (or to
prevent or to deal with) and requiring the aid of the Canadian Forces to
that end has occurred and is in progress (or is considered as likely to
occur) at ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot};

And whereas it has been made to appear to my satisfaction that the
Canadian Forces are required in aid of the civil power;

Now therefore I, ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}, the
Attorney General of ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot},
under and by virtue of the powers conferred by the [National Defence
Act](/eng/acts/N-5), do hereby require you to call out the Canadian
Forces or such part thereof as you consider necessary for the purpose of
suppressing (or preventing or dealing with) the riot or disturbance.

Dated at ![blank line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}, this ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot} day of ![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}, 19![blank
line](/img/ii_spacer.gif){.Leader-dot}.

[Attorney General]{.FormBlank}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 279
- R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}What requisition must state

- **[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In a
  requisition made under this Part, it shall be stated that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} information has been received by the attorney
    general from responsible persons, or a notification has been
    received by the attorney general from a judge, that a riot or
    disturbance beyond the powers of the civil authorities to suppress
    or to prevent or to deal with, as the case may be, has occurred or
    is considered as likely to occur and that the Canadian Forces are
    required in aid of the civil power; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it has been made to appear to the satisfaction of
    the attorney general that the Canadian Forces are so required.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s.
  76\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Province bound by statements,
  undertakings and promises in requisition

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every statement of fact contained in a requisition
  made under this Part is conclusive and binding on the province on
  behalf of which the requisition is made, and every undertaking or
  promise in the requisition is binding on the province and not open to
  question or dispute by reason of alleged incompetence or lack of
  authority on the part of the attorney general or for any other reason.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement not open to dispute

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A statement of fact contained in a requisition made
  under this Part is not open to dispute by the Chief of the Defence
  Staff.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 280
- R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 76
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry and report by attorney general

**[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** Where a requisition is
made under this Part, the attorney general of the province concerned
shall, within seven days after the making of the requisition, cause an
inquiry to be made into the circumstances that occasioned the calling
out of the Canadian Forces or any part thereof, and the attorney general
shall send a report on the circumstances to such member of the Queen's
Privy Council for Canada as is designated by the Governor in Council for
the purpose of this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 281
- 1995, c. 11, s. 44
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When officers and non-commissioned
members have powers of constables

**[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** Officers and
non-commissioned members when called out for service in aid of the civil
power shall, without further authority or appointment and without taking
oath of office, be held to have, in addition to their powers and duties
as officers and non-commissioned members, all of the powers and duties
of constables, so long as they remain so called out, but they shall act
only as a military body and are individually liable to obey the orders
of their superior officers.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 282
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration, increase and diminution of aid
of civil power

**[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** The Canadian Forces or
any part thereof called out in aid of the civil power shall remain on
duty, in such strength as the Chief of the Defence Staff or such officer
as the Chief of the Defence Staff may designate deems necessary or
orders, until notification that the Canadian Forces are no longer
required in aid of the civil power is received from the attorney general
of the province concerned and, from time to time as in the opinion of
the Chief of the Defence Staff the exigencies of the situation require,
the Chief of the Defence Staff may increase or diminish the number of
officers and non-commissioned members called out.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 283
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

**[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S.,
1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 77\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advances

**[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** The moneys required to
meet the expenses and costs occasioned by the calling out of the
Canadian Forces as provided for in this Part and for the services
rendered by them shall be paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund by
the authority of the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 285
- R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 77
:::

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Offences Triable by Civil Courts]{.HTitleText1} {#h-379635 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379636 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability to civil trial

- **[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  person, including an officer or non-commissioned member, is liable to
  be tried in a civil court in respect of any offence prescribed in this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special provision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No charge against an officer or non-commissioned
  member in respect of any offence prescribed in this Part shall, if the
  complainant is any other officer or non-commissioned member, be tried
  by a civil court unless the consent thereto in writing of the
  commanding officer of the accused officer or non-commissioned member
  has first been obtained.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 286
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287 .sectionLabel}** No prosecution in a
civil court shall be commenced against a person in respect of an offence
prescribed in this Part, other than any of the offences referred to in
section 298, except within six months after the date of commission of
the offence charged.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 244
:::

### [Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-379648 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Breach of regulations respecting defence
establishments, works and materiel

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes regulations respecting the access to, exclusion from, and
safety and conduct of any persons in, on or about any defence
establishment, work for defence or materiel is guilty of an offence and
liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one thousand
dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding twelve months or to
both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 245
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False answer on enrolment

**[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
appears before another person for the purpose of being enrolled and
knowingly makes a false answer to any question relating to the enrolment
put by or by direction of that other person to the person appearing for
that purpose is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction,
to a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars or to imprisonment for a
term not exceeding three months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 246
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False medical certificate

**[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290 .sectionLabel}** Every medical
practitioner who signs a false medical certificate or other document in
respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the examination of a person for the purpose of
  enrolment,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the service or release of an officer or
  non-commissioned member, or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disability or alleged disability of a person,
  purported to have arisen or to have been contracted during, in the
  course of, or as a result of the service of that person as an officer
  or non-commissioned member,

is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not
exceeding one thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
exceeding twelve months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 290
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unlawful usage in advertising, trade or
service

- **[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who uses

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the words "Canadian Forces" or "Canadian Armed
    Forces" or the name of any component, unit or other element thereof
    or any abbreviation thereof or any words or letters likely to be
    mistaken therefor,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any picture or other representation of a member of
    the Canadian Forces, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any uniform, mark, badge or insignia in use in the
    Canadian Forces,

  in any advertising or in any trade or service, having been requested
  in writing by the Minister to cease that usage, is guilty of an
  offence punishable on summary conviction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's consent required for
  prosecution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings in respect of an offence under this
  section shall be instituted without the consent of the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 248
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personation

**[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
falsely personates any other person in respect of any duty, act or thing
required to be performed or done under this Act by that other person is
guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not
exceeding one thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
exceeding twelve months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 249
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False representation of desertion

**[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
falsely represents himself to any military or civil authority to be a
deserter from Her Majesty's Forces is guilty of an offence and liable,
on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars or to
imprisonment for a term not exceeding three months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 250
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to attend parade

- **[[294]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every officer or non-commissioned
  member of the reserve force who without lawful excuse neglects or
  refuses to attend any parade or training at the place and hour
  appointed therefor is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction for each offence, if an officer, to a fine not exceeding
  fifty dollars and, if a non-commissioned member, to a fine not
  exceeding twenty-five dollars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Each absence an offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Absence from any parade or training referred to in
  subsection (1) is, in respect of each day on which the absence occurs,
  a separate offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 294
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Neglecting personal equipment

**[[295]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-295 .sectionLabel}** Every officer or
non-commissioned member of the reserve force who fails to keep in proper
order any personal equipment or who appears on parade or on any other
occasion with the personal equipment of that officer or non-commissioned
member out of proper order, unserviceable or deficient in any respect is
guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not
exceeding forty dollars for each offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 295
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interruption or hindering of training or
march

**[[296]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-296 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
without reasonable excuse interrupts or hinders the Canadian Forces
while training or while on the march is guilty of an offence and liable,
on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars and
may be taken into custody and detained by any person by the order of an
officer until the training or march is over for the day.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 253
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hampering manoeuvres

**[[297]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-297 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
without reasonable excuse obstructs or interferes with manoeuvres
authorized under section 257 is guilty of an offence and liable, on
summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 254
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unlawful disposal, removal or possession
of property

- **[[298]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-298
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} unlawfully disposes of or removes any property,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} when lawfully required, refuses to deliver up any
    property that is in the possession of that person, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} without lawful cause, the proof of which lies on
    that person, has possession of any property,

  is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine
  not exceeding one hundred dollars for each offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[property]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any public property under the
  control of the Minister, non-public property and property of any of
  Her Majesty's Forces or of any forces cooperating therewith.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 255
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accessories to desertion and absence
without leave

- **[[299]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-299
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} procures, persuades, aids, assists or counsels an
    officer or non-commissioned member to desert or absent himself
    without leave, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in an emergency, aids, assists, harbours or
    conceals an officer or non-commissioned member who is a deserter or
    an absentee without leave and who does not satisfy the court that he
    did not know that the officer or non-commissioned member was a
    deserter or an absentee without leave,

  is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine
  not exceeding one thousand dollars and not less than one hundred
  dollars or to imprisonment for any term not exceeding twelve months or
  to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of Judge Advocate General

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate that appears to have been signed by the
  Judge Advocate General, or by any person whom the Judge Advocate
  General may appoint for that purpose, attesting that an officer or
  non-commissioned member was convicted or discharged absolutely under
  this Act of desertion or absence without leave or that the officer or
  non-commissioned member was or has been continuously absent without
  leave for six months or more, and setting out the date of commencement
  and, if applicable, the duration of the desertion, absence without
  leave or continuous absence without leave, is for the purposes of
  proceedings under this section evidence of the facts attested to in
  that certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 299
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
- 2013, c. 24, s. 103
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-299-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 299"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aid to intending deserters or absentees

**[[300]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-300 .sectionLabel}** Every person who,
knowing that an officer or non-commissioned member is about to desert or
absent himself without leave, aids or assists the officer or
non-commissioned member in attempting to desert or absent himself
without leave is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction,
to a fine not exceeding one thousand dollars or to imprisonment for any
term not exceeding twelve months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 300
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Miscellaneous offences

**[[301]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-301 .sectionLabel}** Every person who

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} wilfully obstructs, impedes or otherwise interferes
  with any other person in the execution of any duty that under this Act
  or regulations, the other person is required to perform,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} counsels any other person not to perform any duty
  that, under this Act or regulations, the other person is required to
  perform,

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} does an act to the detriment of any other person in
  consequence of the other person having performed a duty that, under
  this Act or regulations, the other person is required to perform,

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} interferes with or impedes, directly or indirectly,
  the recruiting of the Canadian Forces,

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} wilfully produces any disease or infirmity in, maims
  or injures himself or any other person with a view to enabling himself
  or the other person to avoid service in the Canadian Forces,

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} with intent to enable any other person to render
  himself, or to induce the belief that the other person is, permanently
  or temporarily unfit for service in the Canadian Forces, supplies to
  or for the other person any drug or preparation calculated or likely
  to render the other person, or lead to the belief that the other
  person is, permanently or temporarily unfit for that service, or

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} gives or receives, or is in any way concerned in the
  giving or receiving, of any valuable consideration in respect of
  enrolment, release or promotion in the Canadian Forces,

is guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not
exceeding one thousand dollars or to imprisonment for any term not
exceeding twelve months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 258
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences of contempt

**[[302]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-302 .sectionLabel}** Every person is guilty
of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine of not more
than five hundred dollars or to imprisonment for a term of not more than
six months or to both, where the person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on being duly summoned as a witness under Part II,
  III or IV makes default in attending;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} being in attendance as a witness in any proceeding
  under Part II, III or IV,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} refuses to take an oath or make a solemn
    affirmation legally required of that person,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} refuses to produce any document or thing under
    that person's control and required to be produced by that person, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} refuses to answer any question that requires an
    answer;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} at any proceeding under Part II, III or IV, uses
  insulting or threatening language or causes any interference or
  disturbance;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} prints observations or uses words likely to bring a
  proceeding under Part II, III or IV into disrepute or likely to
  influence improperly a board of inquiry, the Grievances Committee, the
  Military Judges Inquiry Committee, a court martial, a military judge,
  an officer conducting a summary hearing, a commissioner taking
  evidence under this Act, the Military Police Complaints Commission, an
  inquiry committee established under the regulations or a witness at a
  proceeding under Part II, III or IV; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} displays contempt, in any other manner whatever, at
  any proceeding under Part II, III or IV.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 302
- 1998, c. 35, s. 90
- 2013, c. 24, ss. 104, 106(E)
- [2019, c. 15, s. 44](#1149870-1355591){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149870-1355591.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-302-20131018.html "Link to previous version of section 302"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication prohibited

- **[[303]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall publish in any document, or broadcast or transmit in any
  way, any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the contents of an application made under section
    180.03;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any evidence taken, information given or
    submissions made at a hearing under subsection 180.04(1) or
    180.06(2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the determination of a military judge in respect of
    the making of an order under subsection 180.05(1) or 180.07(1) and
    the reasons provided under section 180.08, unless the military
    judge, after taking into account the interests of military justice
    and the right to privacy of the person to whom the record relates,
    orders that the determination and the reasons may be published,
    broadcast or transmitted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) is guilty
  of an offence punishable on summary conviction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[record]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[record]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has
  the same meaning as in section 180.01.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 303
- R.S., 1985, c. 22 (4th Supp.), s. 78
- [2019, c. 15, s. 45](#1149874-1355908){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149874-1355908.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-303-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 303"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply --- orders under
sections 183.5 and 183.6

- **[[303.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who fails to comply
  with an order made under section 183.5 or 183.6 is guilty of an
  offence punishable on summary conviction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, an order referred to in
  subsection (1) applies to prohibit, in relation to proceedings taken
  against any person who fails to comply with the order, the publication
  in any document or the broadcasting or transmission in any way of
  information that could identify a victim, witness or military justice
  system participant whose identity is protected by the order.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 15, s. 45](#1149874-1355611){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1149874-1355611.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Breach of regulations respecting
quartering, billeting and encamping

**[[304]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-304 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes regulations respecting the quartering, billeting and
encamping of a unit or other element of the Canadian Forces or of an
officer or non-commissioned member is guilty of an offence and liable,
on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one hundred dollars.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. N-5, s. 304
- R.S., 1985, c. 31 (1st Supp.), s. 60
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Improper exaction of tolls

**[[305]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-305 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
receives or demands a duty or toll in contravention of section 261 is
guilty of an offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not
exceeding one hundred dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
exceeding three months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 262
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with convoy orders

**[[306]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-306 .sectionLabel}** Every person who fails
to comply with directions given under section 262 is guilty of an
offence and liable, on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding two
thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding twelve
months or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. N-4, s. 263
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applications for employment

**[[307]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-307 .sectionLabel}** Every person who uses
or authorizes the use of an application form, for or relating to any of
the following matters, that contains a question that by its terms
requires the applicant to disclose a conviction for an offence referred
to in paragraph 249.27(1)(a) or (b) is guilty of an offence and liable
on summary conviction to a fine of not more than \$500 or to
imprisonment for a term of not more than six months, or to both:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} employment in any department set out in Schedule I to
  the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} employment by any Crown corporation, as defined in
  subsection 83(1) of the [Financial Administration
  Act](/eng/acts/F-11);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} enrolment in the Canadian Forces; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} employment in or in connection with the operation of
  any work, undertaking or business that is within the legislative
  authority of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 24, s. 105
:::

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2008, c. 29, s. 28

  - ###### Review {#review .MarginalNote}

    - [28]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within two years after the day on
      which this Act receives royal assent, a comprehensive review of
      the provisions and operation of this Act shall be undertaken by
      the committee of either the Senate or the House of Commons or of
      both Houses of Parliament that is designated or established by the
      Senate or the House of Commons or by both Houses of Parliament, as
      the case may be, for that purpose.

    - ###### Report {#report .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within one year after the review is undertaken,
      or within any longer period that the Senate or the House of
      Commons or both Houses of Parliament may authorize, the committee
      shall submit a report on the review to Parliament, including a
      statement of any changes that the committee recommends.

- --- 2008, c. 29, s. 29

  - ###### Reference to General Court Martial {#reference-to-general-court-martial .MarginalNote}

    [29]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs 239.1(1)(b) and
    240.3(b) of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), any reference
    to a General Court Martial is also a reference to a Disciplinary
    Court Martial.

- --- 2012, c. 1, par. 163(b)

  - ###### Pending applications --- references in other legislation {#pending-applications-references-in-other-legislation .MarginalNote}

    [163]{.lawlabel} A reference to an application for a record
    suspension in the following provisions, as enacted by this Part, is
    deemed also to be a reference to an application for a pardon that is
    not finally disposed of on the day on which this section comes into
    force:

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 202.14(2)(h) of the [National Defence
      Act](/eng/acts/N-5); and

- --- 2012, c. 1, par. 165(e)

  - ###### Pardons in effect --- references in other legislation {#pardons-in-effect-references-in-other-legislation .MarginalNote}

    [165]{.lawlabel} A reference to a record suspension in the following
    provisions, as enacted by this Part, is deemed also to be a
    reference to a pardon that is granted or issued under the [Criminal
    Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47):

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the definition [[record
      suspension]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 227 of the [National
      Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5); and

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 109

  - ###### Military judges continuing in office {#military-judges-continuing-in-office .MarginalNote}

    [109]{.lawlabel} A person who, immediately before the coming into
    force of this section, held office as a military judge shall
    continue in office as if the person had been appointed under
    subsection 165.21(1) of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5),
    as enacted by section 41.

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 110

  - ###### Members of Inquiry Committee continuing in office {#members-of-inquiry-committee-continuing-in-office .MarginalNote}

    [110]{.lawlabel} A person who, immediately before the coming into
    force of this section, held office as a member of an Inquiry
    Committee established under subsection 165.21(2) of the [National
    Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as it read before the coming into force
    of section 41, shall continue in office as if the person had been
    appointed under subsection 165.31(1) of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted by section 45.

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 111

  - ###### Members of Compensation Committee continuing in office {#members-of-compensation-committee-continuing-in-office .MarginalNote}

    [111]{.lawlabel} A person who, immediately before the coming into
    force of this section, held office as a member of a Compensation
    Committee established under subsection 165.22(2) of the [National
    Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as it read before the coming into force
    of section 41, shall continue in office as if the person had been
    appointed under subsection 165.33(1) of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted by section 45.

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 112

  - ###### Inquiry by Inquiry Committee {#inquiry-by-inquiry-committee .MarginalNote}

    [112]{.lawlabel} An inquiry under subsection 165.21(2) of the
    [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as it read before the coming
    into force of section 41, that, immediately before the coming into
    force of this section, had not been completed shall be continued as
    an inquiry under sections 165.31 and 165.32 of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted by section 45.

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 113

  - ###### Review by Compensation Committee {#review-by-compensation-committee .MarginalNote}

    [113]{.lawlabel} A review under subsection 165.22(2) of the
    [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as it read before the coming
    into force of section 41, that, immediately before the coming into
    force of this section, had not been completed shall be continued as
    an inquiry under sections 165.33 to 165.37 of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted by section 45.

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 114

  - ###### Limitation or prescription period {#limitation-or-prescription-period .MarginalNote}

    [114]{.lawlabel} The limitation or prescription period set out in
    subsection 269(1) of the [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as
    enacted by section 99, applies only in respect of an act, neglect or
    default that occurs after the coming into force of section 99.

- --- 2014, c. 6, s. 31.1

  - ###### Review {#review-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [31.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within five years after
      sections 21 to 31 come into force, a comprehensive review of the
      operation of sections 197 to 233 of the [National Defence
      Act](/eng/acts/N-5) is to be undertaken by a committee of the
      Senate, of the House of Commons or of both Houses of Parliament
      that may be designated or established by the Senate, the House of
      Commons or both Houses of Parliament, as the case may be, for that
      purpose.

    - ###### Report {#report-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within a year, or such further time as authorized
      by the Senate, the House of Commons or both Houses of Parliament,
      as the case may be, after the review is undertaken, the Committee
      referred to in subsection (1) must submit a report on that review
      to the Senate, the House of Commons or both Houses of Parliament,
      as the case may be, including a statement of any changes
      recommended by the Committee.

- --- 2014, c. 25, s. 45.1

  - ###### Review {#review-2 .MarginalNote}

    - [45.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within five years after this
      section comes into force, a comprehensive review of the provisions
      and operation of this Act shall be undertaken by such committee of
      the House of Commons as may be designated or established by the
      House for that purpose.

    - ###### Report {#report-2 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1)
      shall, within a year after a review is undertaken pursuant to that
      subsection or within such further time as the House may authorize,
      submit a report on the review to the Speaker of the House,
      including a statement of any changes the committee recommends.

- --- 2019, c. 15, ss. 63(1), (35)

  - ###### [2013, c. 24]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-24 .MarginalNote}

    - [63]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[other
      Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Strengthening Military Justice
      in the Defence of Canada Act.

    - [(35)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (34) applies, the portion of
      paragraph 249.27(1)(a) of the [National Defence
      Act](/eng/acts/N-5) before subparagraph (i), as enacted by that
      subsection (34), is deemed to have been enacted on the day on
      which section 75 of the other Act came into force.

- --- 2019, c. 15, s. 66

  - ###### Proceedings {#proceedings .MarginalNote}

    [66]{.lawlabel} The [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as it
    read immediately before the coming into force of section 25 of this
    Act, applies

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of proceedings against a person who is
      alleged to have committed a service offence that were commenced by
      the laying of a charge before the coming into force of that
      section 25; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of all matters related to those
      proceedings.

- --- 2019, c. 15, s. 67

  - ###### Section 203.1 of [National Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5) {#section-203.1-of-national-defence-act .MarginalNote}

    [67]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 203.1(2)(c) and (i) of the [National
    Defence Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted, respectively, by paragraphs
    63(21)(d) and (e), apply only in respect of sentences imposed in
    respect of conduct engaged in on or after the day on which those
    paragraphs 63(21)(d) and (e) produce their effects.

## AMENDMENTS NOT IN FORCE {#amendments-not-in-force .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 12

  - ###### [R.S., c. 31(1st Supp.), s. 60 (Sch. I, s. 13)]{.HistoricalNote} {#r.s.-c.-311st-supp.-s.-60-sch.-i-s.-13 .MarginalNote}

    [12]{.lawlabel} Subsection 30(4) of the Act is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Reinstatement {#reinstatement .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to regulations made by the Governor in
      Council, the Chief of the Defence Staff may cancel the release or
      transfer of an officer or non-commissioned member if the officer
      or non-commissioned member consents and the Chief of the Defence
      Staff is satisfied that the release or transfer was improper.

    - ###### Deeming provision {#deeming-provision .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} An officer or non-commissioned member whose
      release or transfer is cancelled is, except as provided in
      regulations made by the Governor in Council, deemed for the
      purpose of this Act or any other Act not to have been released or
      transferred.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 13

  - ###### [1998, c. 35, s. 10]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-35-s.-10 .MarginalNote}

    [13]{.lawlabel} Subsection 35(1) of the Act is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Rates and conditions of pay {#rates-and-conditions-of-pay .MarginalNote}

      - [35]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The rates and conditions of
        issue of pay of officers and non-commissioned members, other
        than those mentioned in paragraph 12(3)(a), shall be established
        by the Treasury Board.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2013, c. 24, s. 46

  - [46]{.lawlabel} The Act is amended by adding the following after
    section 165.37:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Costs payable {#costs-payable .MarginalNote}

      [165.38]{.lawlabel} If the military judges are represented at an
      inquiry of the Military Judges Compensation Committee, the costs
      of representation shall be paid in the amount and manner, and
      according to the terms and conditions, prescribed by regulations
      made by the Governor in Council.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2015, c. 23, s. 32

  - ###### 2010, c. 17, s. 45(2) {#c.-17-s.-452 .MarginalNote}

    [32]{.lawlabel} Subsection 119.1(3) of the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5) is replaced by the following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Proof of certain facts by certificate {#proof-of-certain-facts-by-certificate .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings under subsection (1), a
      certificate of a person referred to in paragraph 16(2)(b.1) of the
      [Sex Offender Information Registration Act](/eng/acts/S-8.7)
      stating that the person named in the certificate failed to report
      under section 4, 4.1, 4.2 or 4.3 --- or provide information under
      section 5 or notify a person under subsection 6(1) or (1.01) ---
      of that Act is evidence of the statements contained in it without
      proof of the signature or official character of the person
      appearing to have signed it.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2024, c. 23, par. 12(d)

  - ###### Replacement of "child pornography" {#replacement-of-child-pornography .MarginalNote}

    [12]{.lawlabel} Every reference to "child pornography" is replaced
    by a reference to "child sexual abuse and exploitation material" in
    the following provisions:

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subsection 183.5(5) of the [National Defence
      Act](/eng/acts/N-5), as enacted by section 28 of An Act to amend
      the National Defence Act and to make related and consequential
      amendments to other Acts, chapter 15 of the Statutes of Canada,
      2019.
